# More adjectives: E–F

> Adjectives E–F beyond the grammar groups — the everyday adjectives that a triage of WordNet’s adjective lemmas showed a learner needs.

[← The grammar of adjectives](../README.md) &middot; [All groups](README.md)

Most English adjectives behave: they go before a noun or after *be*, and they compare with *-er* or *more*. These are those — but each still says how it compares, where it can stand, and what it takes. WordNet lists about twenty thousand adjectives; most of the rest are technical, archaic or British-only, and a triage pass left them out on purpose.

---
### eagle-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːɡəlˈaɪd/ &middot; *ee-guhl-YD*
**Русский:** зоркий, внимательный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eagle-eyed*, *most eagle-eyed*
**Form:** compound — noun + *eyed*

Very good at seeing or noticing small details.

1. An *eagle-eyed* reader spotted the typo on page one.
2. The goalie was *eagle-eyed* all game.
3. The *most eagle-eyed* fans noticed the hidden clue in the trailer.

---

### earnest

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɜːrnɪst/ &middot; *UR-nist*
**Русский:** серьёзный, искренний
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more earnest*, *most earnest*
**Contrast:** *in earnest* is a phrase: *The work began in earnest* (seriously, fully); *Are you in earnest?* (Do you really mean it?).

Very serious and sincere, sometimes too serious.

1. He's an *earnest* young man who wants to help.
2. They had a long, *earnest* conversation about the future.
3. Her second speech was *more earnest* and less funny.

---

### earthen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɜːrθən/ &middot; *UR-thuhn*
**Русский:** земляной; глиняный
**Type:** material
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -en from *earth*

Made of earth or baked clay.

1. The old cabin had an *earthen* floor.
2. Engineers inspected the *earthen* dam after the flood.
3. Water was kept cool in large *earthen* jars.

---

### earthly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɜːrθli/ &middot; *URTH-lee*
**Русский:** земной; (после no/any) какой-либо
**Type:** classifying; also emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ly from *earth*
**Opposite:** *heavenly*

Earthly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to life in this world, not to heaven or the spirit.** (земной, мирской)

1. He gave away all his *earthly* possessions.
2. Their *earthly* life was hard, but their faith was strong.

**2. After *no*, *any* or *what*: at all, possible.** (какой бы то ни было, никакой)

3. There's no *earthly* reason to wake up at five.
4. What *earthly* use is a phone without a battery?

---

### earthy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɜːrθi/ &middot; *UR-thee*
**Русский:** землистый; простой, грубоватый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *earthier*, *earthiest*
**Form:** -y from *earth*

Earthy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Tasting, smelling or looking like soil.** (землистый, с запахом земли)

1. Mushrooms have an *earthy* flavor.
2. The room was decorated in warm, *earthy* colors.
3. This coffee is *earthier* than the Colombian one.

**2. Direct and natural, and not shy about the body or sex.** (простой, приземлённый, грубоватый)

4. She has an *earthy* sense of humor.
5. His grandmother was a warm, *earthy* woman who laughed a lot.

---

### east

**Pronunciation:** /iːst/ &middot; *EEST*
**Русский:** восточный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *west*
**Contrast:** In *drive east* it is an adverb, and in *the East* a noun. *east* is mostly for fixed names and parts: *the East Coast*, *the east side*; *eastern* is the general word: *eastern Europe*, *eastern states*.

East has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In, on or facing the east.** (восточный)

1. They live on the *east* side of town.
2. Take the *east* exit from the parking garage.
3. We moved to the *East* Coast last year.

**2. Of wind: blowing from the east.** (восточный (о ветре))

4. A cold *east* wind blew all day.
5. The *east* winds brought rain from the ocean.

---

### East African

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːst ˈæfrɪkən/ &middot; *eest AF-ri-kuhn*
**Русский:** восточноафриканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *east* + *African*

Of or from eastern Africa, such as Kenya, Tanzania, Ethiopia or Uganda.

1. *East African* runners dominate long-distance races.
2. The restaurant serves *East African* food.
3. Swahili is spoken in many *East African* countries.

---

### East German

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːst ˈdʒɜːrmən/ &middot; *eest JUR-muhn*
**Русский:** восточногерманский, гэдээровский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *east* + *German*
**Contrast:** Historical: the German Democratic Republic existed from 1949 to 1990. For the region today, say *eastern German* or *in eastern Germany*.

Of or from the former German Democratic Republic.

1. His parents escaped from *East German* territory in 1961.
2. *East German* athletes won many Olympic medals.
3. The car she drove was *East German*.

---

### eastbound

**Pronunciation:** /ˈiːstbaʊnd/ &middot; *EEST-bownd*
**Русский:** идущий на восток
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *east* + *-bound*
**Opposite:** *westbound*

Traveling or leading toward the east.

1. There's a crash in the *eastbound* lanes of I-90.
2. The *eastbound* train leaves at 7:15.
3. Is this platform *eastbound* or westbound?

---

### easterly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈiːstərli/ &middot; *EES-tur-lee*
**Русский:** восточный (о направлении, ветре)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an easterly wind*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *most easterly* means 'farthest east'
**Form:** -ly from *east*
**Opposite:** *westerly*
**Contrast:** An *easterly* wind blows *from* the east, but an *easterly* direction is *toward* the east.

Easterly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Toward or in the east.** (восточный, в восточном направлении)

1. The ship sailed in an *easterly* direction.
2. Maine is the *most easterly* state in the country.

**2. Of wind: blowing from the east.** (восточный (о ветре))

3. Strong *easterly* winds kept the boats in the harbor.
4. The forecast calls for light *easterly* breezes.

---

### Eastern Orthodox

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːstərn ˈɔːrθədɑːks/ &middot; *EES-turn OR-thuh-dahks*
**Русский:** православный
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *Eastern* + *Orthodox*
**Contrast:** In American English the Russian Church is usually called *Russian Orthodox*; *Eastern Orthodox* names the whole family of Orthodox churches. *orthodox* with a small *o* means 'traditional, accepted': *orthodox methods*.

Of the Orthodox Christian churches of Eastern Europe, Greece and the Middle East.

1. Most of her family is *Eastern Orthodox*.
2. *Eastern Orthodox* Christians often celebrate Easter on a different date.
3. They were married in an *Eastern Orthodox* church in Chicago.

---

### easternmost

**Pronunciation:** /ˈiːstərnmoʊst/ &middot; *EES-turn-mohst*
**Русский:** самый восточный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable — it is already a superlative
**Form:** *eastern* + -most
**Opposite:** *westernmost*

Farthest to the east.

1. Lubec, Maine, is the *easternmost* town in the US.
2. We visited the *easternmost* point of the island.
3. The *easternmost* part of the state is mostly farmland.

---

### eastward

**Pronunciation:** /ˈiːstwərd/ &middot; *EEST-wurd*
**Русский:** направленный на восток
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *east* + -ward
**Opposite:** *westward*
**Contrast:** After a verb it is an adverb: *The storm moved eastward*. *eastwards* with an *s* is more British.

Moving or facing toward the east.

1. The storm continued its *eastward* path.
2. Our *eastward* flight took only four hours.
3. The house has an *eastward* view of the mountains.

---

### easygoing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːziˈɡoʊɪŋ/ &middot; *ee-zee-GOH-ing*
**Русский:** покладистый, спокойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more easygoing*, *most easygoing*
**Form:** compound — *easy* + *going*; one word in American spelling
**Opposite:** *uptight*

Relaxed and calm, not easily upset or worried.

1. My new roommate is really *easygoing*.
2. He has an *easygoing* attitude about money.
3. She's much *more easygoing* than her sister.

---

### ebullient

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈbʊljənt/ &middot; *ih-BUUL-yuhnt*
**Русский:** восторженный, кипучий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ebullient*, *most ebullient*

Very cheerful, excited and full of energy.

1. The coach was in an *ebullient* mood after the win.
2. She's an *ebullient* host who makes everyone feel welcome.
3. He was even *more ebullient* than usual at the reunion.

---

### eccentric

**Pronunciation:** /ɪkˈsɛntrɪk/ &middot; *ik-SEN-trik*
**Русский:** эксцентричный, чудаковатый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eccentric*, *most eccentric*
**Contrast:** *an eccentric* is also a noun: *He's a lovable eccentric*.

Behaving or looking strange and unusual, often in a harmless or amusing way.

1. My *eccentric* uncle keeps goats in his backyard.
2. Her fashion sense is a little *eccentric*.
3. He got *more eccentric* as he got older.

---

### eclectic

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈklɛktɪk/ &middot; *ih-KLEK-tik*
**Русский:** разнообразный, эклектичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eclectic*, *most eclectic*

Made up of many different styles, types or ideas.

1. He has *eclectic* taste in music, from jazz to heavy metal.
2. The café is decorated in an *eclectic* mix of styles.
3. This year's lineup is *more eclectic* than ever.

---

### eco-friendly

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːkoʊˈfrɛndli/ &middot; *ee-koh-FREND-lee*
**Русский:** экологичный, экологически чистый
**Type:** compound — eco- + friendly
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eco-friendly*, *most eco-friendly*
**Form:** compound — *eco-* + *friendly*, like *user-friendly*
**Contrast:** *friendly* here means *not harmful to*, not *kind*. *Environmentally friendly* is the more formal version; *green* is the everyday one.

Not harmful to the environment.

1. We switched to *eco-friendly* cleaning products.
2. Bamboo is more *eco-friendly* than plastic.
3. The new office was designed to be *eco-friendly*.

---

### ecological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːkəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *ee-kuh-LAH-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** экологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *ecology*
**Contrast:** False friend: Russian *экологичный* or *экологически чистый* is *eco-friendly*, *green* or *environmentally friendly* in English: *eco-friendly packaging*, not *ecological packaging*. *ecological* means 'relating to nature and the environment': *an ecological disaster*.

Relating to the relationships between living things and their environment.

1. The oil spill was an *ecological* disaster.
2. Scientists are studying the *ecological* impact of the new dam.
3. Wetlands have enormous *ecological* value.

---

### Ecuadorian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛkwəˈdɔːriən/ &middot; *ek-wuh-DOR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** эквадорский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Ecuador*

Of or from Ecuador, its people or its culture.

1. We tried *Ecuadorian* ceviche in Queens.
2. Her father is *Ecuadorian* and her mother is Peruvian.
3. The *Ecuadorian* coast is famous for its bananas and shrimp.

---

### ecumenical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛkjəˈmɛnɪkəl/ &middot; *ek-yuh-MEN-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** экуменический; разносторонний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — the religious sense mostly before a noun: *an ecumenical service*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more ecumenical* in the 'broad' sense

Ecumenical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Bringing together different Christian churches or different religions.** (экуменический, межконфессиональный)

1. The town held an *ecumenical* prayer service after the tragedy.
2. Leaders from several churches joined the *ecumenical* council.

**2. Including or accepting many different kinds of things.** (разносторонний, всеохватный)

3. The festival takes an *ecumenical* approach to music.
4. Her reading habits are wonderfully *ecumenical*.

---

### edgy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛdʒi/ &middot; *EJ-ee*
**Русский:** нервный; дерзкий, провокационный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *edgier*, *edgiest*
**Form:** -y from *edge*

Edgy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Nervous, tense and easily annoyed.** (нервный, взвинченный)

1. Everyone was *edgy* while we waited for the results.
2. Too much coffee makes me *edgy*.

**2. Daring, modern and a little shocking.** (дерзкий, смелый, провокационный)

3. The comedian is known for his *edgy* jokes.
4. The band's new album is *edgier* than their last one.

---

### edible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛdəbəl/ &middot; *ED-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** съедобный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *barely edible*, *more edible* when it means 'good enough to eat'
**Opposite:** *inedible*
**Contrast:** *edibles* is a plural noun, in American English often 'food containing cannabis'.

Edible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Safe to eat.** (съедобный)

1. Not all wild mushrooms are *edible*.
2. The flowers on the cake are *edible*.
3. We learned to find *edible* plants in the woods.

**2. Good enough to eat, though not very good.** (съедобный, есть можно)

4. The cafeteria food was barely *edible*.
5. With some salt, the soup was at least *edible*.

---

### editorial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛdɪˈtɔːriəl/ &middot; *ed-i-TOR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** редакционный, редакторский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ial from *editor*
**Contrast:** *an editorial* is a noun: an article that gives a newspaper's opinion.

Editorial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to editing or to the people who prepare a book, newspaper or magazine.** (редакционный, редакторский)

1. She joined the magazine's *editorial* staff.
2. The paper insists on its *editorial* independence.

**2. Giving an opinion rather than just reporting facts.** (редакционный, выражающий мнение)

3. The *editorial* page supported the mayor.
4. He draws *editorial* cartoons for a local paper.

---

### educated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛdʒəkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *EJ-uh-kay-tid*
**Русский:** образованный; обоснованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more educated*, *most educated*; also *better educated*, *well-educated*
**Form:** -ed from *educate*
**Opposite:** *uneducated*

Educated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having had a good education and knowing a lot.** (образованный)

1. She's an *educated* woman who speaks three languages.
2. Voters in the suburbs tend to be *more educated*.
3. He was *educated* at a small college in Ohio.

**2. Of a guess or decision: based on some knowledge, so likely to be right.** (обоснованный, основанный на знании)

4. I don't know the exact number, but I can make an *educated* guess.
5. Read the reviews so you can make an *educated* choice.

---

### educational

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛdʒəˈkeɪʃənəl/ &middot; *ej-uh-KAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** образовательный; познавательный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — the 'relating to education' sense only before a noun: *the educational system*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more educational*, *most educational*, in the 'teaches you something' sense
**Form:** -al from *education*

Educational has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to education and schools.** (образовательный, учебный)

1. The *educational* system needs more funding.
2. He works for an *educational* nonprofit.

**2. Teaching you something useful or interesting.** (познавательный, поучительный)

3. The trip to the museum was really *educational*.
4. We try to buy *educational* toys for the kids.
5. The documentary was *more educational* than the textbook.

---

### Edwardian

**Pronunciation:** /ɛdˈwɔːrdiən/ &middot; *ed-WOR-dee-uhn*
**Русский:** эдвардианский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Edward*

From the time of King Edward VII of Britain, 1901 to 1910, or in its style.

1. They restored a beautiful *Edwardian* house.
2. The movie is set in *Edwardian* England.
3. The costumes in the play were *Edwardian*.

---

### eerie

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɪri/ &middot; *IR-ee*
**Русский:** жуткий, зловещий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *eerier*, *eeriest*

Strange and frightening in a mysterious way.

1. An *eerie* silence fell over the empty school.
2. It was *eerie* how much he looked like his late father.
3. The abandoned mall is *eerier* at night.

---

### effeminate

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈfɛmɪnət/ &middot; *ih-FEM-i-nuht*
**Русский:** женоподобный, изнеженный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more effeminate*, *most effeminate*
**Contrast:** Used about men and usually disapproving or offensive. It is not a neutral word for *feminine*.

Of a man: looking or behaving in a way thought to be typical of women.

1. He was bullied at school for being *effeminate*.
2. Critics called the singer's style *effeminate*.
3. In the remake, the character is less *effeminate* and more serious.

---

### effervescent

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛfərˈvɛsənt/ &middot; *ef-ur-VES-uhnt*
**Русский:** шипучий; искрящийся, жизнерадостный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more effervescent*, *most effervescent*

Effervescent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a person: lively, cheerful and full of energy.** (искрящийся, жизнерадостный)

1. She has an *effervescent* personality.
2. The *most effervescent* guest at the party was my grandmother.

**2. Of a liquid: producing small bubbles of gas.** (шипучий, газированный)

3. Drop the *effervescent* tablet into a glass of water.
4. The wine is light and slightly *effervescent*.

---

### effete

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈfiːt/ &middot; *ih-FEET*
**Русский:** изнеженный, слабый, выродившийся
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more effete*, *most effete*
**Contrast:** A disapproving, bookish word; in American political talk it often mocks educated elites: *effete snobs*.

Weak, too refined and lacking energy or strength.

1. The novel mocks the *effete* aristocrats of old Europe.
2. He dismissed his critics as *effete* intellectuals.
3. The new version sounds *more effete* than the original.

---

### effortless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛfərtləs/ &middot; *EF-urt-luhs*
**Русский:** лёгкий, непринуждённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more effortless*, *most effortless*
**Form:** -less from *effort*

Done so well that it seems to need no effort.

1. She made the difficult dance look *effortless*.
2. He has an *effortless* style of writing.
3. Her piano playing grew *more effortless* every year.

---

### effusive

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈfjuːsɪv/ &middot; *ih-FYOO-siv*
**Русский:** восторженный, бурный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more effusive*, *most effusive*
**Takes:** *in* + noun: *effusive in her praise*

Showing strong feelings of praise, thanks or welcome, sometimes too much.

1. The reviews of the play were *effusive*.
2. He gave me an *effusive* welcome at the door.
3. She was *more effusive* in her thanks than I expected.

---

### egalitarian

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˌɡælɪˈtɛriən/ &middot; *ih-gal-i-TAIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** эгалитарный, основанный на равенстве
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more egalitarian*, *most egalitarian*

Believing that all people are equal and should have the same rights and chances.

1. They share the housework in a very *egalitarian* way.
2. The company has an *egalitarian* culture with no private offices.
3. The Nordic countries are among the *most egalitarian* in the world.

---

### egocentric

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːɡoʊˈsɛntrɪk/ &middot; *ee-goh-SEN-trik*
**Русский:** эгоцентричный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more egocentric*, *most egocentric*
**Form:** compound — *ego* + *centric*

Thinking only about yourself and not about other people.

1. Toddlers are naturally *egocentric*.
2. He's an *egocentric* boss who never listens.
3. She became *more egocentric* after she got famous.

---

### egotistical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌiːɡəˈtɪstɪkəl/ &middot; *ee-guh-TIS-ti-kuhl*
**Русский:** самовлюблённый, эгоистичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more egotistical*, *most egotistical*
**Form:** -ical from *egotist*
**Opposite:** *modest*
**Contrast:** *egotistical* means 'thinking you are very important and talking about yourself'; *selfish* means 'caring only about your own needs'. Russian *эгоистичный* is usually *selfish*.

Thinking you are better or more important than other people.

1. He's so *egotistical* that he only talks about himself.
2. The *egotistical* star refused to share the stage.
3. She's the *most egotistical* person I've ever worked with.

---

### egregious

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈɡriːdʒəs/ &middot; *ih-GREE-juhs*
**Русский:** вопиющий, возмутительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more egregious*, *most egregious*

Extremely bad and very obvious.

1. It was an *egregious* error by the referee.
2. The company was fined for *egregious* safety violations.
3. This is the *most egregious* case of fraud the court has seen.

---

### Egyptian

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈdʒɪpʃən/ &middot; *ih-JIP-shuhn*
**Русский:** египетский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Egypt*

Of or from Egypt, ancient or modern, its people or its culture.

1. The museum has a large collection of ancient *Egyptian* art.
2. My doctor is *Egyptian*.
3. *Egyptian* cotton sheets are soft and expensive.

---

### elaborate

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlæbərət/ &middot; *ih-LAB-ur-uht*
**Русский:** сложный, продуманный, замысловатый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elaborate*, *most elaborate*
**Opposite:** *simple*
**Contrast:** The adjective ends in /ərət/; the verb *to elaborate* (give more details) ends in /əreɪt/: *Can you ih-LAB-uh-rayt?*

Having many carefully planned parts or details.

1. They planned an *elaborate* surprise party.
2. The dress had *elaborate* embroidery on the sleeves.
3. Each year the holiday decorations get *more elaborate*.

---

### elastic

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlæstɪk/ &middot; *ih-LAS-tik*
**Русский:** эластичный, упругий; гибкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elastic*, *most elastic*
**Contrast:** *elastic* is also a noun, the stretchy material: *The elastic in these shorts is worn out*.

Elastic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to stretch and then go back to its original shape.** (эластичный, упругий, растяжимый)

1. The pants have an *elastic* waistband.
2. Skin becomes less *elastic* with age.
3. This fabric is *more elastic* than cotton.

**2. Able to change or be changed easily.** (гибкий, растяжимый (о понятии, правилах))

4. "Soon" is a very *elastic* word in this office.
5. Demand for gasoline is not very *elastic*.

---

### elated

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈleɪtɪd/ &middot; *ih-LAY-tid*
**Русский:** ликующий, в восторге
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *We were elated*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elated*, *most elated*
**Takes:** *at*, *by* or *about* + noun; *to*-infinitive; *that*-clause

Extremely happy and excited.

1. She was *elated* at the news of her promotion.
2. The *elated* fans ran onto the field.
3. I've never seen him *more elated* than on the day his son was born.

---

### elected

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlɛktɪd/ &middot; *ih-LEK-tid*
**Русский:** избранный, выборный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the passive: *She was elected mayor*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *elect*
**Contrast:** A person who has won but not yet started the job is *-elect*, after the noun: *the president-elect*, *the governor-elect*.

Chosen by voting.

1. *Elected* officials should listen to voters.
2. The city has an *elected* school board.
3. She was the first *elected* female governor of the state.

---

### elective

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlɛktɪv/ &middot; *ih-LEK-tiv*
**Русский:** выборный; факультативный; плановый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from *elect*
**Contrast:** In American schools *an elective* is a noun, 'an optional class': *I'm taking photography as an elective*.

Elective has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Chosen, not required; of medical treatment, planned rather than urgent.** (факультативный, по выбору; плановый (об операции))

1. Hospitals postponed all *elective* surgery.
2. Art is an *elective* course at our high school.
3. Many insurers don't cover *elective* procedures.

**2. Relating to an election or filled by an election.** (выборный)

4. She has never held *elective* office.
5. The governor is the highest *elective* position in the state.

---

### electoral

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlɛktərəl/ &middot; *ih-LEK-tur-uhl*
**Русский:** избирательный, выборный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *elector*

Relating to elections or voting.

1. The candidate needs 270 votes in the *Electoral* College.
2. Some states are changing their *electoral* maps.
3. There was no evidence of *electoral* fraud.

---

### electrifying

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlɛktrɪfaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *ih-LEK-tri-fy-ing*
**Русский:** захватывающий, потрясающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more electrifying*, *most electrifying*
**Form:** -ing from *electrify*

Very exciting.

1. The band gave an *electrifying* performance.
2. The atmosphere in the stadium was *electrifying*.
3. It was the *most electrifying* finish in years.

---

### electromagnetic

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˌlɛktroʊmæɡˈnɛtɪk/ &middot; *ih-lek-troh-mag-NET-ik*
**Русский:** электромагнитный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *electro-* + *magnetic*

Relating to electricity and magnetism together.

1. Light is a form of *electromagnetic* radiation.
2. The device creates a strong *electromagnetic* field.
3. Radio waves are part of the *electromagnetic* spectrum.

---

### electronic

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˌlɛkˈtrɑːnɪk/ &middot; *ih-lek-TRAH-nik*
**Русский:** электронный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *electron*
**Contrast:** *electronic* devices use microchips and circuits (a phone, a computer); *electric* things simply run on electricity (*an electric fan*, *an electric car*); *electrical* describes the system or work (*electrical wiring*, *an electrical engineer*).

Electronic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Using microchips, transistors or other parts that control electric current.** (электронный)

1. Please turn off all *electronic* devices during takeoff.
2. The store sells *electronic* equipment and appliances.

**2. Done, sent or stored using computers.** (электронный, цифровой)

3. We accept *electronic* payments only.
4. Doctors now keep *electronic* medical records.
5. The music is fully *electronic*, with no live instruments.

---

### elegant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛlɪɡənt/ &middot; *EL-i-guhnt*
**Русский:** элегантный, изящный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elegant*, *most elegant*
**Opposite:** *inelegant*

Elegant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Graceful, attractive and stylish.** (элегантный, изящный)

1. She wore an *elegant* black dress.
2. The hotel lobby is simple but *elegant*.
3. The new design is *more elegant* than the old one.

**2. Of an idea or solution: clever and simple.** (изящный, остроумный (о решении))

4. It's an *elegant* solution to a difficult problem.
5. The proof is short and *elegant*.

---

### elemental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛlɪˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *el-i-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** стихийный, первобытный; элементарный (в химии)
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — the chemistry sense only before a noun: *elemental carbon*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elemental*, *most elemental*
**Contrast:** Not the same as *elementary* (basic, simple): *an elementary mistake*, but *elemental forces of nature*.

Elemental has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Basic and powerful, like the forces of nature.** (стихийный, первобытный, глубинный)

1. The storm showed the *elemental* power of the sea.
2. Fear of the dark is one of the *most elemental* human fears.

**2. In chemistry: in the form of a pure chemical element.** (элементарный, в чистом виде (о веществе))

3. Diamonds are made of *elemental* carbon.
4. *Elemental* sodium reacts violently with water.

---

### elementary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛlɪˈmɛntəri/ &middot; *el-i-MEN-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** элементарный, простой; начальный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — the school sense only before a noun: *elementary school*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elementary*, *most elementary*, in the 'basic' sense
**Form:** -ary from *element*
**Opposite:** *advanced*

Elementary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Basic and simple.** (элементарный, простой, базовый)

1. He made an *elementary* mistake in the math.
2. The questions were fairly *elementary*.
3. Let's start with the *most elementary* rules of grammar.

**2. Relating to the first stage of school, for children aged about 5 to 11.** (начальный (о школе))

4. My daughter is in *elementary* school.
5. He's been an *elementary* teacher for twenty years.

---

### elevated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛlɪveɪtɪd/ &middot; *EL-i-vay-tid*
**Русский:** повышенный; поднятый; возвышенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elevated*, *most elevated*
**Form:** -ed from *elevate*

Elevated has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Higher than normal in level or amount.** (повышенный)

1. The test showed *elevated* levels of lead in the water.
2. Her blood pressure was slightly *elevated*.

**2. Raised above the ground.** (поднятый, надземный)

3. Chicago's *elevated* trains are called the L.
4. The house sits on an *elevated* site above the river.

**3. Formal, serious or high in rank or style.** (возвышенный, высокий)

5. The speech was written in *elevated* language.
6. Her tone became *more elevated* when the cameras were on.

---

### elite

**Pronunciation:** /eɪˈliːt/ &middot; *ay-LEET*
**Русский:** элитный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *an elite school*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elite*, *most elite*
**Contrast:** *the elite* is a noun, the most powerful or talented group. *elitist* is the disapproving word for a person or attitude that looks down on ordinary people.

Belonging to the best, most skilled or most powerful group.

1. She trained with *elite* athletes before the Olympics.
2. He was a member of an *elite* military unit.
3. It's one of the *most elite* colleges in the country.

---

### Elizabethan

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˌlɪzəˈbiːθən/ &middot; *ih-liz-uh-BEE-thuhn*
**Русский:** елизаветинский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Elizabeth*
**Contrast:** It refers to Queen Elizabeth I (1558–1603), the age of Shakespeare, not to Elizabeth II.

From the time of Queen Elizabeth I of England, or in its style.

1. We're studying *Elizabethan* drama this semester.
2. The actors wore *Elizabethan* costumes.
3. The manor house is *Elizabethan*, built in 1580.

---

### elliptical

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlɪptɪkəl/ &middot; *ih-LIP-ti-kuhl*
**Русский:** эллиптический, овальный; недосказанный
**Type:** shape; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elliptical* (the 'unclear' sense); the shape sense is not gradable
**Contrast:** *an elliptical* (noun) is an exercise machine at the gym.

Elliptical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shaped like a flattened circle; oval.** (эллиптический, овальный)

1. The earth moves around the sun in an *elliptical* orbit.
2. They bought an *elliptical* dining table for the narrow room.

**2. Of speech or writing, leaving out words or details, so that it is hard to understand.** (недосказанный, туманный)

3. His answer was short and *elliptical*.
4. Her later poems are even more *elliptical* than her early ones.

---

### elongated

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈlɔːŋɡeɪtɪd/ &middot; *ih-LAWNG-gay-tid*
**Русский:** удлинённый, вытянутый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elongated*, *most elongated*
**Form:** -ed from *elongate*

Longer and thinner than usual.

1. The figures in his paintings have *elongated* faces.
2. The shadows grew *elongated* as the sun went down.
3. The new model has a more *elongated* shape than the old one.

---

### eloquent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛləkwənt/ &middot; *EL-uh-kwuhnt*
**Русский:** красноречивый; выразительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eloquent*, *most eloquent*
**Opposite:** *inarticulate*

Eloquent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Speaking or writing well, in a way that is clear and moving.** (красноречивый)

1. She gave an *eloquent* speech at her father's funeral.
2. He is one of the most *eloquent* writers of his generation.
3. The senator was *eloquent* on the subject of health care.

**2. Showing a feeling or meaning clearly without words.** (выразительный, красноречивый)

4. Her silence was an *eloquent* answer.
5. The empty seats were *eloquent* proof that the show had failed.

---

### elusive

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈluːsɪv/ &middot; *ih-LOO-siv*
**Русский:** неуловимый, труднодостижимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more elusive*, *most elusive*
**Form:** -ive from *elude*

Hard to find, catch, achieve or describe.

1. The team is still chasing that *elusive* first championship.
2. Snow leopards are among the most *elusive* animals in the world.
3. A good night's sleep has been *elusive* since the baby was born.

---

### emaciated

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈmeɪʃieɪtɪd/ &middot; *ih-MAY-shee-ay-tid*
**Русский:** истощённый, исхудавший
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more emaciated*, *most emaciated*
**Form:** -ed from *emaciate*

Extremely thin and weak because of illness or lack of food.

1. Rescuers found an *emaciated* dog tied up behind the house.
2. After months in the hospital, he looked pale and *emaciated*.
3. The horses were more *emaciated* than anyone had expected.

---

### embattled

**Pronunciation:** /ɛmˈbætəld/ &middot; *em-BAT-uhld*
**Русский:** находящийся под огнём критики; осаждённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *the embattled CEO*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more embattled*, *most embattled*

Embattled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Facing a lot of criticism or serious problems.** (находящийся под огнём критики, в трудном положении)

1. The *embattled* mayor refused to resign.
2. The company is more *embattled* now than it was a year ago.
3. Teachers at the *embattled* school say they need more support.

**2. Of a place or army, surrounded by enemies in a war.** (осаждённый)

4. Supplies were flown into the *embattled* city.
5. The *embattled* troops held out for three weeks.

---

### embedded

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈbɛdɪd/ &middot; *im-BED-id*
**Русский:** вмурованный, застрявший; укоренившийся; встроенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — degree with *deeply* or *firmly*: *deeply embedded*
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *embed*

Embedded has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Fixed firmly inside something solid.** (вмурованный, застрявший)

1. The doctor removed a piece of glass *embedded* in his foot.
2. Fossils are *embedded* in the rock.

**2. Forming a deep, lasting part of a culture, mind or group.** (укоренившийся)

3. Racism was deeply *embedded* in the town's history.
4. These habits are so *embedded* that they are hard to change.

**3. Built into a larger system or device, or placed inside a group to work with it.** (встроенный; прикомандированный)

5. The car's *embedded* software controls the brakes.
6. You can play the *embedded* video right on the page.
7. An *embedded* reporter traveled with the unit.

---

### emblematic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛmbləˈmætɪk/ &middot; *em-bluh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** символичный, характерный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more emblematic*, *most emblematic*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Form:** -ic from *emblem*

Typical of something and serving as a symbol of it.

1. The long lines at the DMV are *emblematic* of the problem.
2. The Golden Gate Bridge is the most *emblematic* sight in San Francisco.
3. His story is *emblematic* of a whole generation of immigrants.

---

### emboldened

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈboʊldənd/ &middot; *im-BOHL-duhnd*
**Русский:** ободрённый, осмелевший
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more emboldened*, *most emboldened*
**Takes:** *by* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** -ed from *embolden* (em- + *bold* + -en)

Feeling braver or more confident, usually because of something that has happened.

1. *Emboldened* by the first win, the team started taking more risks.
2. She felt *emboldened* to ask for a raise.
3. After the vote, the protesters were more *emboldened* than ever.

---

### embossed

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈbɑːst/ &middot; *im-BAHST*
**Русский:** тиснёный, рельефный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun (the design)
**Form:** -ed from *emboss*

Having a raised design or letters pressed into the surface.

1. The wedding invitations were printed on *embossed* paper.
2. The leather notebook is *embossed* with her initials.
3. Run your finger over the *embossed* numbers on the card.

---

### embroiled

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈbrɔɪld/ &middot; *im-BROYLD*
**Русский:** втянутый (в конфликт, скандал)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb or after the noun: *a company embroiled in a lawsuit*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more embroiled*, *most embroiled*
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *embroil*

Deeply involved in an argument, conflict or difficult situation.

1. The senator is *embroiled* in a campaign finance scandal.
2. They became *embroiled* in a bitter fight over the will.
3. The longer the war lasted, the more *embroiled* the country became.

---

### embryonic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛmbriˈɑːnɪk/ &middot; *em-bree-AH-nik*
**Русский:** эмбриональный; зачаточный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — the biology sense only before a noun: *embryonic cells*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *embryo*

Embryonic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. At a very early stage of development.** (зачаточный, находящийся в зачаточном состоянии)

1. The plan is still in its *embryonic* stage.
2. In 1995 the online shopping industry was *embryonic*.

**2. Relating to an embryo.** (эмбриональный)

3. The lab studies *embryonic* stem cells.
4. The scan showed normal *embryonic* growth.

---

### emerging

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈmɜːrdʒɪŋ/ &middot; *ih-MUR-jing*
**Русский:** развивающийся, новый, набирающий силу
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *The truth is emerging* is the verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *emerge*

Starting to exist, develop or become known.

1. The fund invests in *emerging* markets such as India and Brazil.
2. The festival showcases *emerging* artists.
3. Doctors are worried about an *emerging* strain of the virus.

---

### emeritus

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈmɛrɪtəs/ &middot; *ih-MER-i-tuhs*
**Русский:** почётный (в отставке), заслуженный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** after the noun — usually: *professor emeritus*; also before: *emeritus professor*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** It does not simply mean 'honorary': an *emeritus* professor really held the job and has retired, keeping the title.

Retired but allowed to keep a title, especially at a university.

1. He is a professor *emeritus* of history at Yale.
2. The *emeritus* pastor still preaches on holidays.
3. She became president *emeritus* when she stepped down.

---

### eminent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛmɪnənt/ &middot; *EM-i-nuhnt*
**Русский:** выдающийся, видный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eminent*, *most eminent*
**Contrast:** Not *imminent* (about to happen): *an eminent scientist*, but *an imminent storm*.

Famous and respected, especially in a profession.

1. The panel included several *eminent* scientists.
2. She was one of the most *eminent* surgeons in the country.
3. Her grandfather was an *eminent* judge.

---

### emotional

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈmoʊʃənəl/ &middot; *ih-MOH-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** эмоциональный; душевный; трогательный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more emotional*, *most emotional*
**Form:** -al from *emotion*
**Opposite:** *unemotional*

Emotional has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to feelings.** (эмоциональный, душевный)

1. Her friends gave her a lot of *emotional* support.
2. The divorce took an *emotional* toll on the kids.

**2. Showing strong feelings, or easily showing them.** (эмоциональный, взволнованный)

3. He got very *emotional* when he talked about his mother.
4. I'm more *emotional* than my husband.

**3. Causing strong feelings.** (трогательный, волнующий)

5. It was an *emotional* reunion at the airport.
6. Abortion is a highly *emotional* issue.

---

### emotionless

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈmoʊʃənləs/ &middot; *ih-MOH-shuhn-luhs*
**Русский:** бесстрастный, безэмоциональный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more emotionless*, *most emotionless*
**Form:** -less from *emotion*

Showing no feelings.

1. The defendant stared ahead with an *emotionless* face.
2. Her voice was flat and *emotionless*.
3. The robot sounded more *emotionless* than I expected.

---

### emotive

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈmoʊtɪv/ &middot; *ih-MOH-tiv*
**Русский:** эмоционально окрашенный, вызывающий эмоции
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more emotive*, *most emotive*
**Contrast:** *emotive* describes words and issues that stir feelings; a person who shows feelings is *emotional*, not *emotive*.

Causing strong feelings.

1. Gun control is a very *emotive* issue.
2. The ad uses *emotive* language to get donations.
3. Her second speech was even more *emotive* than the first.

---

### empathetic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛmpəˈθɛtɪk/ &middot; *em-puh-THET-ik*
**Русский:** сопереживающий, эмпатичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more empathetic*, *most empathetic*
**Form:** from *empathy*

Able to understand and share other people's feelings.

1. She's a very *empathetic* nurse.
2. Try to be more *empathetic* when your friends are going through a hard time.
3. The manager gave an *empathetic* response to the complaint.

---

### emphatic

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈfætɪk/ &middot; *im-FAT-ik*
**Русский:** решительный, категоричный; убедительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more emphatic*, *most emphatic*
**Takes:** *about* + noun; *that*-clause

Emphatic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Said or done in a strong, definite way.** (решительный, категоричный)

1. Her answer was an *emphatic* no.
2. He was *emphatic* that he had never met the man.
3. The coach was more *emphatic* about defense this season.

**2. Of a win or result, very clear.** (убедительный)

4. The Lakers won an *emphatic* victory, 120 to 89.
5. The vote was an *emphatic* rejection of the plan.

---

### empirical

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈpɪrɪkəl/ &middot; *im-PIR-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** эмпирический, опытный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *theoretical*

Based on observation or experiment rather than theory.

1. There is no *empirical* evidence that the diet works.
2. The study is *empirical*, not theoretical.
3. We need *empirical* data before we change the policy.

---

### employable

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈplɔɪəbəl/ &middot; *im-PLOY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** востребованный на рынке труда, способный найти работу
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more employable*, *most employable*
**Form:** -able from *employ*
**Opposite:** *unemployable*

Having the skills or qualities that employers want.

1. Learning to code made her more *employable*.
2. The program helps young people develop *employable* skills.
3. At 58, he worried that he was no longer *employable*.

---

### employed

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈplɔɪd/ &middot; *im-PLOYD*
**Русский:** работающий, имеющий работу
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* or *at* + employer; *as* + job
**Form:** -ed from *employ*
**Opposite:** *unemployed*

Having a paid job.

1. She is *employed* as a nurse at the county hospital.
2. About 60 percent of *employed* adults work full-time.
3. Are you currently *employed*?

---

### empowered

**Pronunciation:** /ɪmˈpaʊərd/ &middot; *im-POW-urd*
**Русский:** уверенный в своих силах; уполномоченный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more empowered*, *most empowered* (the 'confident' sense)
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive
**Form:** -ed from *empower*

Empowered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Feeling confident and in control of your own life.** (уверенный в своих силах)

1. The class made the women feel *empowered*.
2. Workers feel more *empowered* when they have a voice.

**2. Officially given the power or right to do something.** (уполномоченный)

3. The committee is *empowered* to hire a new director.
4. Only *empowered* officials can sign the contract.

---

### empty-handed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛmptiˈhændɪd/ &middot; *emp-tee-HAN-did*
**Русский:** с пустыми руками
**Type:** compound
**Position:** predicative only — after *come*, *go*, *leave*, *return*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *empty* + *hand* + -ed

Without getting or bringing anything.

1. The robbers fled *empty-handed*.
2. Don't show up at the party *empty-handed*; bring some wine.
3. We went fishing all day and came home *empty-handed*.

---

### empty-headed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛmptiˈhɛdɪd/ &middot; *emp-tee-HED-id*
**Русский:** пустоголовый, глупый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more empty-headed*, *most empty-headed*
**Form:** compound — *empty* + *head* + -ed

Silly and not thinking seriously about anything.

1. The movie is full of *empty-headed* characters.
2. He's not as *empty-headed* as he pretends to be.
3. That was the most *empty-headed* comment of the night.

---

### enamored

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈnæmərd/ &middot; *ih-NAM-urd*
**Русский:** влюблённый, очарованный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** predicative only — *enamored of* or *with* something
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enamored*, *most enamored*
**Takes:** *of* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *enamor*
**Contrast:** American spelling; British is *enamoured*. Often used in the negative: *not enamored with the idea* means you do not like it.

Liking or loving someone or something very much.

1. He was completely *enamored* of his new neighbor.
2. I'm not very *enamored* with the new design.
3. The longer she lived in Chicago, the more *enamored* of it she became.

---

### enchanted

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtʃæntɪd/ &middot; *in-CHAN-tid*
**Русский:** заколдованный, волшебный; очарованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the magic sense usually before a noun: *an enchanted forest*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enchanted* (the 'charmed' sense); the magic sense is not gradable
**Takes:** *by* or *with* + noun (the 'charmed' sense)
**Form:** -ed from *enchant*

Enchanted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Under a magic spell.** (заколдованный, волшебный)

1. In the story, the princess sleeps in an *enchanted* castle.
2. They got lost in an *enchanted* forest.

**2. Charmed and delighted.** (очарованный)

3. The children were *enchanted* by the puppet show.
4. I was even more *enchanted* with the city the second time.

---

### enchanting

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/ &middot; *in-CHAN-ting*
**Русский:** очаровательный, чарующий
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enchanting*, *most enchanting*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling; from *enchant*

Very charming and delightful.

1. We stayed in an *enchanting* little village in Vermont.
2. Her singing voice is *enchanting*.
3. It was the most *enchanting* evening of the trip.

---

### enclosed

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈkloʊzd/ &middot; *in-KLOHZD*
**Русский:** закрытый, огороженный; вложенный, прилагаемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *enclose*

Enclosed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Surrounded by walls, a fence or a roof.** (закрытый, огороженный)

1. The house has an *enclosed* porch.
2. Dogs must stay in the *enclosed* area of the park.
3. The pool is fully *enclosed*, so we can swim in winter.

**2. Sent in the same envelope or package as a letter.** (вложенный, прилагаемый)

4. Please fill out the *enclosed* form.
5. A check for $50 is *enclosed*.

---

### encrusted

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈkrʌstɪd/ &middot; *in-KRUHS-tid*
**Русский:** покрытый коркой; усыпанный, инкрустированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *encrust*

Encrusted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with a hard layer of something, such as dirt or ice.** (покрытый коркой)

1. He left his mud-*encrusted* boots by the door.
2. The car windows were *encrusted* with ice.

**2. Decorated with jewels or other precious things set into the surface.** (усыпанный, инкрустированный)

3. She wore a diamond-*encrusted* watch.
4. The crown is *encrusted* with rubies.

---

### encyclopedic

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˌsaɪkləˈpiːdɪk/ &middot; *in-sy-kluh-PEE-dik*
**Русский:** энциклопедический, всеобъемлющий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more encyclopedic*, *most encyclopedic*
**Form:** -ic from *encyclopedia*

Covering a very wide range of facts about a subject.

1. My uncle has an *encyclopedic* knowledge of baseball.
2. The guidebook is *encyclopedic* but hard to carry.
3. This is the most *encyclopedic* history of jazz ever written.

---

### end-to-end

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛnd tə ˈɛnd/ &middot; *end tuh END*
**Русский:** сквозной, полный (от начала до конца)
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *end-to-end encryption*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + preposition + noun
**Contrast:** Before a noun it takes hyphens; after a verb it is an adverb phrase without them: *Line the tables up end to end*.

Covering a whole process from the beginning to the end.

1. The app uses *end-to-end* encryption, so only you and the other person can read the messages.
2. The company offers an *end-to-end* service, from design to delivery.
3. We ran an *end-to-end* test of the whole system.

---

### endangered

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈdeɪndʒərd/ &middot; *in-DAYN-jurd*
**Русский:** находящийся под угрозой исчезновения
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more endangered*, *most endangered*
**Form:** -ed from *endanger*

At risk of disappearing completely.

1. The Florida panther is an *endangered* species.
2. Many Native American languages are *endangered*.
3. Mountain gorillas are among the most *endangered* animals on earth.

---

### endearing

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈdɪrɪŋ/ &middot; *in-DEER-ing*
**Русский:** милый, располагающий, трогательный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more endearing*, *most endearing*
**Form:** -ing from *endear*

Making people like you or feel affection for you.

1. He has an *endearing* habit of humming while he cooks.
2. Her shyness was actually rather *endearing*.
3. The puppy's most *endearing* quality is its clumsiness.

---

### endemic

**Pronunciation:** /ɛnˈdɛmɪk/ &middot; *en-DEM-ik*
**Русский:** распространённый, характерный; эндемичный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + place or group (disease, problem); *to* + place (plant, animal)
**Preposition patterns:** [endemic to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#endemic-to)
**Contrast:** Not *epidemic*: an *endemic* disease is always present in a place, an *epidemic* is a sudden outbreak.

Endemic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a disease or problem, regularly found in a particular place or group.** (распространённый, характерный)

1. Malaria is *endemic* in parts of Africa.
2. Corruption was *endemic* in the city government.
3. Health officials say the virus has become *endemic*.

**2. Of a plant or animal, found naturally only in one area.** (эндемичный)

4. The Hawaiian goose is *endemic* to Hawaii.
5. The island has dozens of *endemic* bird species.

---

### endless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛndləs/ &middot; *END-luhs*
**Русский:** бесконечный; бесчисленный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *end*

Endless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Seeming to continue forever.** (бесконечный)

1. I spent the whole day in *endless* meetings.
2. The drive through Kansas felt *endless*.
3. She was tired of their *endless* arguing.

**2. Very many or without any limit.** (бесчисленный, неограниченный)

4. With a 3D printer, the possibilities are *endless*.
5. The buffet had an *endless* supply of shrimp.

---

### endowed

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈdaʊd/ &middot; *in-DOWD*
**Русский:** наделённый, одарённый; обеспеченный фондом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — degree with an adverb: *richly endowed*, *generously endowed*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [endowed with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#endowed-with)
**Form:** -ed from *endow*
**Contrast:** *well-endowed* said about a person is a joke about the size of their breasts or genitals; say *talented* or *gifted* for ability.

Endowed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Naturally having a good quality or ability.** (наделённый, одарённый)

1. She is *endowed* with a great sense of humor.
2. The region is richly *endowed* with natural resources.

**2. Of a school or institution, given a large sum of money that provides a steady income.** (обеспеченный фондом, финансируемый за счёт пожертвований)

3. Harvard is a generously *endowed* university.
4. He holds an *endowed* chair in economics.

---

### enduring

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈdʊrɪŋ/ &middot; *in-DUUR-ing*
**Русский:** прочный, долговечный, неизменный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enduring*, *most enduring*
**Form:** -ing from *endure*

Lasting for a long time.

1. They formed an *enduring* friendship in college.
2. Elvis is one of the most *enduring* stars in American music.
3. Her love of the ocean has been *enduring*.

---

### energetic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛnərˈdʒɛtɪk/ &middot; *en-ur-JET-ik*
**Русский:** энергичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more energetic*, *most energetic*
**Form:** -ic from *energy*
**Opposite:** *lethargic*

Full of energy and very active.

1. Our kindergarten teacher is young and *energetic*.
2. I feel more *energetic* when I get up early.
3. The band gave an *energetic* performance.

---

### energizing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛnərdʒaɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *EN-ur-jy-zing*
**Русский:** бодрящий, придающий сил
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more energizing*, *most energizing*
**Form:** -ing from *energize*

Making you feel full of energy.

1. A cold shower in the morning is really *energizing*.
2. The yoga class was more *energizing* than I expected.
3. She gave an *energizing* talk to the sales team.

---

### enforceable

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈfɔːrsəbəl/ &middot; *in-FOR-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** имеющий законную силу; выполнимый (о правиле)
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enforceable*, *most enforceable*
**Preposition patterns:** [enforceable against](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#enforceable-against)
**Form:** -able from *enforce*
**Opposite:** *unenforceable*

Of a law, rule or contract, possible to make people obey or legally valid.

1. A verbal agreement is not always legally *enforceable*.
2. The new rules are simpler and more *enforceable*.
3. The court ruled that the contract was *enforceable*.

---

### engaged

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈɡeɪdʒd/ &middot; *in-GAYJD*
**Русский:** помолвленный; увлечённый, вовлечённый; занятый (чем-то)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more engaged* (the 'involved' sense); *engaged* to marry is not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + person (marriage); *in* + noun or *-ing* (activity)
**Preposition patterns:** [engaged to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#engaged-to)
**Form:** -ed from *engage*
**Contrast:** In American English a phone line or a restroom is *busy* or *occupied*, not *engaged*.

Engaged has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having formally agreed to marry someone.** (помолвленный)

1. My sister just got *engaged* to her college boyfriend.
2. The *engaged* couple are planning a June wedding.

**2. Interested and actively taking part.** (увлечённый, вовлечённый)

3. The students were more *engaged* when they worked in groups.
4. Voters in the county are very *engaged* this year.

**3. Busy doing a particular activity.** (занятый (чем-то))

5. The two sides are *engaged* in peace talks.
6. He was *engaged* in a heated conversation with the manager.

---

### engaging

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/ &middot; *in-GAY-jing*
**Русский:** обаятельный; увлекательный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more engaging*, *most engaging*
**Form:** -ing from *engage*

Pleasant, interesting and holding your attention.

1. She has an *engaging* smile.
2. The documentary is informative and *engaging*.
3. He's the most *engaging* speaker I've ever heard.

---

### English-speaking

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɪŋɡlɪʃˈspiːkɪŋ/ &middot; *ing-glish SPEE-king*
**Русский:** англоязычный
**Type:** compound; also origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *English-speaking countries*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle

Using English as the main language.

1. Canada has both *English-speaking* and French-speaking provinces.
2. The hotel has *English-speaking* staff.
3. Most of the island is *English-speaking*.

---

### engraved

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈɡreɪvd/ &middot; *in-GRAYVD*
**Русский:** выгравированный; врезавшийся (в память)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + words or design; *on* or *in* + surface or memory
**Form:** -ed from *engrave*

Engraved has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With words or a design cut into the surface.** (выгравированный, гравированный)

1. He gave her an *engraved* silver bracelet.
2. The trophy is *engraved* with the names of past winners.

**2. Fixed firmly in your memory.** (врезавшийся (в память))

3. That day is *engraved* in my memory.
4. Her face was *engraved* on his mind.

---

### engrossed

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈɡroʊst/ &middot; *in-GROHST*
**Русский:** поглощённый, увлечённый
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *engrossed in a book*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more engrossed*, *most engrossed*
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how you feel; from *engross*

So interested in something that you do not notice anything else.

1. She was so *engrossed* in her book that she missed her stop.
2. The kids sat *engrossed* in the cartoon.
3. The more *engrossed* he got in the game, the less he ate.

---

### engrossing

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/ &middot; *in-GROH-sing*
**Русский:** увлекательный, захватывающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more engrossing*, *most engrossing*
**Form:** -ing — what holds your attention; from *engross*

So interesting that it holds all your attention.

1. It's an *engrossing* novel about a family in 1930s Texas.
2. The trial was long but *engrossing*.
3. This was the most *engrossing* series I watched all year.

---

### enhanced

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈhænst/ &middot; *in-HANST*
**Русский:** улучшенный, усиленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *enhance*

Improved, increased or made stronger.

1. The airport has introduced *enhanced* security checks.
2. The photos were digitally *enhanced*.
3. Some states now issue an *enhanced* driver's license.

---

### enigmatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛnɪɡˈmætɪk/ &middot; *en-ig-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** загадочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enigmatic*, *most enigmatic*
**Form:** -ic from *enigma*

Mysterious and hard to understand.

1. The Mona Lisa is famous for her *enigmatic* smile.
2. He left an *enigmatic* note on the kitchen table.
3. She is the most *enigmatic* character in the novel.

---

### enjoyable

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈdʒɔɪəbəl/ &middot; *in-JOY-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** приятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enjoyable*, *most enjoyable*
**Form:** -able from *enjoy*

Giving pleasure.

1. Thanks for a very *enjoyable* evening.
2. The hike was more *enjoyable* once the rain stopped.
3. Cooking is *enjoyable* when you're not in a hurry.

---

### enlarged

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈlɑːrdʒd/ &middot; *in-LAHRJD*
**Русский:** увеличенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *enlarge*

Bigger than before or bigger than normal.

1. The X-ray showed an *enlarged* heart.
2. We hung an *enlarged* copy of the photo in the hall.
3. His glands were swollen and *enlarged*.

---

### enlightened

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈlaɪtənd/ &middot; *in-LY-tuhnd*
**Русский:** просвещённый, прогрессивный; просветлённый
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enlightened*, *most enlightened*
**Form:** -ed from *enlighten*
**Opposite:** *unenlightened*

Enlightened has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having modern, sensible and fair ideas.** (просвещённый, прогрессивный)

1. The company has an *enlightened* policy on parental leave.
2. We like to think we live in more *enlightened* times.

**2. In religion, especially Buddhism, having reached spiritual understanding.** (просветлённый)

3. The Buddha is described as an *enlightened* being.
4. The monk seemed calm and *enlightened*.

---

### enlightening

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈlaɪtənɪŋ/ &middot; *in-LY-tuh-ning*
**Русский:** поучительный, познавательный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enlightening*, *most enlightening*
**Form:** -ing from *enlighten*

Helping you understand something better.

1. The museum tour was really *enlightening*.
2. We had an *enlightening* conversation about her work.
3. His second book is more *enlightening* than the first.

---

### enmeshed

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈmɛʃt/ &middot; *in-MESHT*
**Русский:** втянутый, запутавшийся; слишком тесно связанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enmeshed*, *most enmeshed*
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *enmesh*

Enmeshed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Caught up in a difficult or complicated situation.** (втянутый, запутавшийся)

1. The firm became *enmeshed* in a long legal battle.
2. The more *enmeshed* he got in the family business, the harder it was to leave.

**2. Of family members or partners, too closely involved in each other's lives, without healthy limits.** (слишком тесно связанный, без личных границ)

3. The therapist said they had an *enmeshed* relationship.
4. Children from *enmeshed* families may struggle to make their own decisions.

---

### enraged

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈreɪdʒd/ &middot; *in-RAYJD*
**Русский:** разъярённый
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enraged*, *most enraged*
**Takes:** *by* or *at* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *enrage*

Extremely angry.

1. *Enraged* fans threw bottles onto the field.
2. She was *enraged* by the court's decision.
3. The more he explained, the more *enraged* his boss became.

---

### ensuing

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈsuːɪŋ/ &middot; *in-SOO-ing*
**Русский:** последующий, последовавший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *ensue*

Happening after something, often as a result of it.

1. Two people were hurt in the *ensuing* fight.
2. The storm and the *ensuing* floods destroyed hundreds of homes.
3. Over the *ensuing* weeks, the story spread across the country.

---

### entangled

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtæŋɡəld/ &middot; *in-TANG-guhld*
**Русский:** запутавшийся; втянутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more entangled*, *most entangled*
**Takes:** *in* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *entangle*

Entangled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Twisted together with or caught in something such as rope or wire.** (запутавшийся)

1. A whale was found *entangled* in fishing nets.
2. The cords behind the TV were all *entangled*.

**2. Involved in a complicated or difficult situation or relationship.** (втянутый, замешанный)

3. He got *entangled* with a group of con artists.
4. The country became more *entangled* in the conflict each year.

---

### enterprising

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛntərpraɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *EN-tur-pry-zing*
**Русский:** предприимчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enterprising*, *most enterprising*
**Form:** -ing from *enterprise*

Good at thinking of new ideas and ready to take risks to make money or get things done.

1. An *enterprising* teenager set up a lemonade stand outside the stadium.
2. The more *enterprising* students started their own businesses.
3. She's smart and *enterprising*.

---

### entertained

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛntərˈteɪnd/ &middot; *en-tur-TAYND*
**Русский:** развлечённый, занятый (чем-то интересным)
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after *keep*, *stay* or *be*: *keep the kids entertained*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more entertained*, *most entertained*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how you feel; from *entertain*
**Contrast:** You are *entertained*; the show is *entertaining*.

Enjoying yourself because something interesting or amusing is holding your attention.

1. It's hard to keep the kids *entertained* on a long flight.
2. The audience was clearly *entertained* by his jokes.
3. I was more *entertained* by the commercials than by the game.

---

### entertaining

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛntərˈteɪnɪŋ/ &middot; *en-tur-TAY-ning*
**Русский:** занимательный, развлекательный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more entertaining*, *most entertaining*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling; from *entertain*
**Contrast:** *She does a lot of entertaining* is a noun: having guests at home.

Amusing and enjoyable.

1. It wasn't a great movie, but it was *entertaining*.
2. He's one of the most *entertaining* speakers on the circuit.
3. She told us an *entertaining* story about her trip.

---

### enthralled

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈθrɔːld/ &middot; *in-THRAWLD*
**Русский:** очарованный, заворожённый
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enthralled*, *most enthralled*
**Takes:** *by* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how you feel; from *enthrall*

So interested or delighted that you give all your attention.

1. The children sat *enthralled* by the magician.
2. The *enthralled* audience didn't make a sound.
3. She was even more *enthralled* with the city at night.

---

### enthralling

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈθrɔːlɪŋ/ &middot; *in-THRAW-ling*
**Русский:** захватывающий, завораживающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enthralling*, *most enthralling*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling; from *enthrall*

So exciting or interesting that it holds all your attention.

1. The final set was *enthralling*.
2. It's an *enthralling* story of survival in Alaska.
3. That was the most *enthralling* game of the season.

---

### enthusiastic

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˌθuːziˈæstɪk/ &middot; *in-thoo-zee-AS-tik*
**Русский:** полный энтузиазма, восторженный
**Type:** emotion; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enthusiastic*, *most enthusiastic*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [enthusiastic about](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#enthusiastic-about)
**Form:** -ic from *enthusiasm*
**Opposite:** *unenthusiastic*

Showing a lot of interest and excitement.

1. The kids are really *enthusiastic* about the science fair.
2. The band got an *enthusiastic* welcome.
3. My husband was more *enthusiastic* about the trip than I was.

---

### enticing

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtaɪsɪŋ/ &middot; *in-TY-sing*
**Русский:** заманчивый, соблазнительный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enticing*, *most enticing*
**Form:** -ing from *entice*

Attractive and making you want to have or do something.

1. An *enticing* smell was coming from the kitchen.
2. The job offer was very *enticing*.
3. A bonus made the deal even more *enticing*.

---

### entitled

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtaɪtəld/ &middot; *in-TY-tuhld*
**Русский:** имеющий право; избалованный, считающий, что ему все должны; озаглавленный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both — the 'right' sense only after a verb; the 'title' sense after the noun: *a book entitled Home*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more entitled*, *most entitled* (the 'spoiled' sense); the other senses are not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun; *to*-infinitive (the 'right' sense)
**Preposition patterns:** [entitled to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#entitled-to)
**Form:** -ed from *entitle*
**Contrast:** *entitled to* something means you have a right to it; *entitled* alone about a person is an insult.

Entitled has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having the right to have or do something.** (имеющий право)

1. Full-time employees are *entitled* to two weeks of paid vacation.
2. You're *entitled* to your opinion.

**2. Believing that you deserve special treatment without earning it.** (избалованный, считающий, что ему все должны)

3. The customers were rude and *entitled*.
4. Some say this generation is more *entitled* than the last.

**3. Having a particular title.** (озаглавленный, под названием)

5. She wrote a memoir *entitled* "Coming Home."
6. The report, *entitled* "Hunger in America," came out in May.

---

### entrancing

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtrænsɪŋ/ &middot; *in-TRAN-sing*
**Русский:** чарующий, завораживающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more entrancing*, *most entrancing*
**Form:** -ing from the verb *entrance* (in-TRANS)
**Contrast:** The stress moves: the noun *entrance* (a door) is EN-truhns; the verb and *entrancing* are stressed on -TRAN-.

So beautiful or interesting that you cannot stop watching or listening.

1. The northern lights were *entrancing*.
2. She has an *entrancing* voice.
3. It's the most *entrancing* dance performance I've seen.

---

### entrenched

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˈtrɛntʃt/ &middot; *in-TRENCHT*
**Русский:** укоренившийся, прочно утвердившийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more entrenched*, *most entrenched*
**Form:** -ed from *entrench*

Firmly established and very difficult to change.

1. It's hard to change deeply *entrenched* attitudes.
2. The problem is more *entrenched* than we thought.
3. Powerful *entrenched* interests blocked the reform.

---

### entrepreneurial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɑːntrəprəˈnɜːriəl/ &middot; *ahn-truh-pruh-NUR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** предпринимательский
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more entrepreneurial*, *most entrepreneurial*
**Form:** -ial from *entrepreneur*

Relating to starting businesses, or having the qualities needed to start them.

1. She has a strong *entrepreneurial* spirit.
2. Austin has become one of the most *entrepreneurial* cities in the country.
3. The course teaches basic *entrepreneurial* skills.

---

### enviable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛnviəbəl/ &middot; *EN-vee-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** завидный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more enviable*, *most enviable*
**Form:** -able from *envy*

So good that other people would like to have it.

1. The team has an *enviable* record at home.
2. Her position was hardly *enviable*.
3. He has one of the most *enviable* jobs in Hollywood.

---

### environmental

**Pronunciation:** /ɪnˌvaɪrənˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *in-vy-ruhn-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** экологический; связанный с окружающей средой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *environment*
**Contrast:** Russian *экологический* is usually *environmental* in English: *environmental problems*, not *ecological problems*.

Environmental has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the natural world and how people affect it.** (экологический)

1. Plastic waste is a serious *environmental* problem.
2. She works for an *environmental* group in Seattle.
3. The project needs an *environmental* review before it starts.

**2. Relating to the conditions a person lives in, as opposed to their genes.** (связанный со средой, внешний)

4. Both genetic and *environmental* factors affect weight.
5. Doctors think the cause is *environmental*.

---

### ephemeral

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈfɛmərəl/ &middot; *ih-FEM-uh-ruhl*
**Русский:** мимолётный, недолговечный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ephemeral*, *most ephemeral*
**Opposite:** *lasting*

Lasting only a very short time.

1. Fame on social media is often *ephemeral*.
2. The artist makes *ephemeral* sculptures out of ice.
3. Some fashions are more *ephemeral* than others.

---

### epic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛpɪk/ &middot; *EP-ik*
**Русский:** эпический; грандиозный; потрясающий (разг.)
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more epic*, *most epic*
**Contrast:** *an epic* (noun) is a long poem, book or movie.

Epic has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of a story, poem or movie, long and telling of heroic deeds.** (эпический)

1. The Odyssey is an *epic* poem.
2. It's a three-hour *epic* Western.

**2. Very large, long or impressive in scale.** (грандиозный, масштабный)

3. They set off on an *epic* road trip across the country.
4. The traffic jam reached *epic* proportions.

**3. Informal: extremely good, impressive or bad.** (потрясающий, эпичный (разг.))

5. That concert was *epic*!
6. It was the most *epic* fail of the whole show.

---

### epidemiological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛpɪˌdiːmiəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *ep-i-dee-mee-uh-LAH-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** эпидемиологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *epidemiology*

Relating to the study of how diseases spread in populations.

1. *Epidemiological* studies link smoking to lung cancer.
2. The CDC collects *epidemiological* data from every state.
3. The *epidemiological* evidence was strong.

---

### epileptic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛpɪˈlɛptɪk/ &middot; *ep-i-LEP-tik*
**Русский:** эпилептический; страдающий эпилепсией
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *epilepsy*
**Contrast:** The noun *an epileptic* can sound offensive; say *a person with epilepsy*.

Relating to epilepsy, or having epilepsy.

1. He had an *epileptic* seizure at school.
2. Flashing lights can be dangerous for *epileptic* patients.
3. Her son is *epileptic*, so she carries his medication everywhere.

---

### Episcopalian

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˌpɪskəˈpeɪliən/ &middot; *ih-pis-kuh-PAY-lee-uhn*
**Русский:** епископальный (о церкви в США)
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The church itself is *the Episcopal Church*; *an Episcopalian* (noun) is a member.

Belonging to or relating to the Episcopal Church, the American branch of the Anglican Church.

1. They were married in an *Episcopalian* ceremony.
2. Her family is *Episcopalian*.
3. He went to an *Episcopalian* school in Virginia.

---

### episodic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛpəˈsɑːdɪk/ &middot; *ep-uh-SAH-dik*
**Русский:** эпизодический, периодический; состоящий из эпизодов
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more episodic*, *most episodic*
**Form:** -ic from *episode*

Episodic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happening only now and then, not regularly.** (эпизодический, периодический)

1. He suffers from *episodic* migraines.
2. Violence in the area has been *episodic*.

**2. Of a story or show, made up of separate episodes.** (состоящий из эпизодов, серийный)

3. The novel is *episodic*, with each chapter set in a different year.
4. The second season felt more *episodic* than the first.

---

### eponymous

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈpɑːnɪməs/ &middot; *ih-PAH-ni-muhs*
**Русский:** одноимённый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having the same name as the person or group it is named after.

1. The band released its *eponymous* debut album in 1994.
2. Martha Stewart started her *eponymous* magazine in 1990.
3. The *eponymous* hero of the novel is a young orphan.

---

### equatorial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɛkwəˈtɔːriəl/ &middot; *ek-wuh-TOR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** экваториальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ial from *equator*

Near the equator or typical of the areas near it.

1. The Amazon has an *equatorial* climate.
2. They studied the plants of *equatorial* Africa.
3. The heat in Singapore is *equatorial*.

---

### equestrian

**Pronunciation:** /ɪˈkwɛstriən/ &middot; *ih-KWES-tree-uhn*
**Русский:** конный, конноспортивный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
