Exuberant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of energy, excitement and happiness.** (жизнерадостный, полный энергии)

1. The *exuberant* fans poured onto the field.
2. The kids were *exuberant* on the last day of school.
3. Her *exuberant* laugh filled the room.

**2. Growing thickly and strongly, or bright and full of life in style.** (буйный, пышный; яркий)

4. The garden was a mass of *exuberant* green.
5. The painting uses *exuberant* reds and yellows.

---

### eye-catching

**Pronunciation:** /ˈaɪˌkætʃɪŋ/ &middot; *Y-kach-ing*
**Русский:** броский, привлекающий внимание
**Type:** compound — noun + -ing participle
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eye-catching*, *most eye-catching*
**Form:** compound — *eye* + *catching*

Attractive or unusual enough to make people notice it.

1. The store needs an *eye-catching* sign.
2. Her red coat was very *eye-catching*.
3. Which of these two designs is *more eye-catching*?

---

### eye-popping

**Pronunciation:** /ˈaɪˌpɑːpɪŋ/ &middot; *Y-pah-ping*
**Русский:** поразительный, ошеломляющий
**Type:** compound — noun + -ing participle
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more eye-popping*, *most eye-popping*
**Form:** compound — *eye* + *popping*

So large, surprising or striking that it makes you stare — informal.

1. The house sold for an *eye-popping* $4 million.
2. The special effects were *eye-popping*.
3. The team put up *eye-popping* numbers this season.

---

### fabled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪbəld/ &middot; *FAY-buhld*
**Русский:** легендарный, знаменитый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun, after a verb
**Form:** -ed from *fable*

Famous and talked about a lot, often in stories that may not be completely true.

1. We finally drove the *fabled* Route 66.
2. The team's *fabled* 1927 lineup is still talked about today.
3. The city is *fabled* for its jazz clubs.

---

### fabricated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæbrɪˌkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *FAB-ri-kay-tid*
**Русский:** сфабрикованный, выдуманный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *fabricate*

Invented in order to deceive people; not true.

1. The reporter was fired for publishing *fabricated* quotes.
2. His lawyers say the evidence was *fabricated*.
3. She told a completely *fabricated* story about her past.

---

### fabulous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæbjələs/ &middot; *FAB-yuh-luhs*
**Русский:** потрясающий, великолепный; баснословный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fabulous*, *most fabulous*

Fabulous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely good; wonderful — informal.** (потрясающий, великолепный)

1. You look *fabulous* in that dress!
2. We had a *fabulous* time in New Orleans.
3. The view from the top was *fabulous*.

**2. Unusually great in amount or size.** (баснословный)

4. He made a *fabulous* fortune in oil.
5. The family lived in *fabulous* wealth.

---

### face-saving

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪsˌseɪvɪŋ/ &middot; *FAYS-say-ving*
**Русский:** позволяющий сохранить лицо
**Type:** compound — noun + -ing participle
**Position:** attributive only — *a face-saving deal*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the phrase *save face*

Helping someone avoid looking foolish or losing other people's respect.

1. The two sides agreed on a *face-saving* compromise.
2. Resigning was a *face-saving* move for the senator.
3. They needed a *face-saving* way to end the strike.

---

### face-to-face

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfeɪs tə ˈfeɪs/ &middot; *fays-tuh-FAYS*
**Русский:** личный, очный
**Type:** compound — noun + preposition + noun
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *a face-to-face meeting*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *face* + *to* + *face*
**Contrast:** After a verb it is often the adverb, usually written open: *We finally met face to face*.

With the people physically together in the same place, not by phone or online.

1. I'd rather have a *face-to-face* conversation about this.
2. Classes will be *face-to-face* again this fall.
3. Nothing replaces *face-to-face* contact with customers.

---

### faceless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪsləs/ &middot; *FAYS-luhs*
**Русский:** безликий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more faceless*, *most faceless*
**Form:** -less from *face*

Having no individual character; impersonal and unknown.

1. I'm tired of dealing with *faceless* bureaucrats.
2. The *faceless* office towers all looked the same.
3. Big corporations can seem *faceless* and cold.

---

### facetious

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈsiːʃəs/ &middot; *fuh-SEE-shuhs*
**Русский:** шутливый (некстати), несерьёзный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more facetious*, *most facetious*

Joking about something serious, or not meaning what you say to be taken seriously.

1. Don't be *facetious* — this is important.
2. I was being *facetious* when I said I'd quit.
3. He made a *facetious* comment about the funeral.

---

### facial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪʃəl/ &middot; *FAY-shuhl*
**Русский:** лицевой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *facial hair*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *face*
**Contrast:** *A facial* is also a noun: a beauty treatment for the face.

Of or on the face.

1. His *facial* expression didn't change.
2. The phone uses *facial* recognition to unlock.
3. He has a lot of *facial* hair.

---

### facile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæsəl/ &middot; *FAS-uhl*
**Русский:** поверхностный, упрощённый; лёгкий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more facile*, *most facile*
**Contrast:** Usually not a compliment: it does not simply mean 'easy' (лёгкий). *A facile answer* is too simple to be taken seriously.

Facile has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too simple; not showing careful thought.** (поверхностный, упрощённый)

1. That's a *facile* explanation for a complicated problem.
2. Critics called the book's conclusions *facile*.
3. There are no *facile* solutions to poverty.

**2. Done with little effort; smooth and fluent, sometimes too much so.** (лёгкий, беглый)

4. The Yankees won a *facile* victory.
5. He's a *facile* writer who can turn out a column in an hour.

---

### fact-finding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæktˌfaɪndɪŋ/ &middot; *FAKT-fyn-ding*
**Русский:** по установлению фактов, ознакомительный
**Type:** compound — noun + -ing participle
**Position:** attributive only — *a fact-finding mission*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *fact* + *finding*

Done to find out the facts about a situation.

1. The senator went on a *fact-finding* trip to the border.
2. A *fact-finding* committee will look into the accident.
3. The first meeting is a *fact-finding* session.

---

### factory-made

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæktəriˌmeɪd/ &middot; *FAK-tuh-ree-mayd*
**Русский:** фабричный, заводской
**Type:** compound — noun + past participle
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *factory* + *made*
**Opposite:** *handmade*; *homemade*

Made by machines in a factory, not by hand or at home.

1. *Factory-made* furniture is cheaper but doesn't last as long.
2. Are these cookies homemade or *factory-made*?
3. Almost all the bread in the store is *factory-made*.

---

### factual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæktʃuəl/ &middot; *FAK-choo-uhl*
**Русский:** основанный на фактах, фактологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more factual*, *most factual*
**Form:** -al from *fact*
**Contrast:** Russian *фактический* often means *actual* or *real*: *фактическая стоимость* is *the actual cost*. English *factual* means only 'based on facts' or 'about facts': *a factual report*, *a factual error*.

Based on facts or containing facts, not opinions.

1. The report contains several *factual* errors.
2. Please keep your answers short and *factual*.
3. The second article was *more factual* and less emotional.

---

### faded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪdɪd/ &middot; *FAY-did*
**Русский:** выцветший, поблёкший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more faded*, *most faded*
**Form:** -ed from *fade*

Faded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having lost its color or brightness.** (выцветший, полинявший)

1. He wore an old pair of *faded* jeans.
2. The sign on the barn was *faded* and hard to read.
3. The photo was *more faded* than I remembered.

**2. No longer strong, famous or clear.** (поблёкший, угасший)

4. She's a *faded* movie star living in Palm Springs.
5. I have only *faded* memories of that house.

---

### Fahrenheit

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛrənˌhaɪt/ &middot; *FAIR-uhn-hyt*
**Русский:** по Фаренгейту
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** after the noun — after a number of degrees: *72 degrees Fahrenheit*; also before a noun: *the Fahrenheit scale*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Americans use *Fahrenheit* for weather, cooking and body temperature: 32°F is freezing and 98.6°F is normal body temperature. Always with a capital F.

Measured on the temperature scale on which water freezes at 32 degrees and boils at 212.

1. It was 95 degrees *Fahrenheit* in Phoenix today.
2. Bake at 350 degrees *Fahrenheit* for 25 minutes.
3. Most Americans only know the *Fahrenheit* scale.

---

### fail-safe

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪlˌseɪf/ &middot; *FAYL-sayf*
**Русский:** надёжный, безотказный
**Type:** compound — verb + adjective
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *fail* + *safe*

Designed to stay safe if something goes wrong; certain to work.

1. The elevator has a *fail-safe* braking system.
2. There's no *fail-safe* way to pick stocks.
3. The system is supposed to be *fail-safe*.

---

### failing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪlɪŋ/ &middot; *FAY-ling*
**Русский:** слабеющий; терпящий неудачу; неудовлетворительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *failing health*; after *be* it is usually the verb: *His health is failing*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *fail*
**Contrast:** *A failing* is also a noun, meaning a weakness: *We all have our failings*.

Failing has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Becoming weaker or worse.** (слабеющий, ухудшающийся)

1. She moved in with her daughter because of *failing* health.
2. In the *failing* light we couldn't see the trail.
3. Grandpa's *failing* eyesight means he can't drive anymore.

**2. Not successful; losing money, customers or support.** (терпящий неудачу, убыточный)

4. He took over a *failing* restaurant and turned it around.
5. The state stepped in to fix *failing* schools.

**3. Of a grade or score: too low to pass.** (неудовлетворительный)

6. He got a *failing* grade in chemistry.
7. Anything below 60 counts as a *failing* score.

---

### faint

**Pronunciation:** /feɪnt/ &middot; *FAYNT*
**Русский:** слабый, едва заметный; близкий к обмороку
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — in the 'about to pass out' sense only after a verb: *I feel faint*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fainter*, *faintest*
**Contrast:** *I don't have the faintest idea* means 'I have no idea at all'. *Faint* is also a verb, 'to lose consciousness' (*She fainted*). *Feint* (a fake move) sounds the same.

Faint has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Hard to see, hear or smell; not strong.** (слабый, едва различимый)

1. There was a *faint* smell of smoke in the hall.
2. The music grew *fainter* as we walked away.
3. I could see a *faint* light in the distance.

**2. Very small; slight.** (слабый, малейший)

4. There's still a *faint* hope that they'll find survivors.
5. I don't have the *faintest* idea where he went.
6. She gave a *faint* smile.

**3. Feeling weak and dizzy, as if you are about to lose consciousness.** (близкий к обмороку)

7. I felt *faint* from the heat.
8. She was *faint* with hunger after the long hike.

---

### faint-hearted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfeɪntˈhɑːrtɪd/ &middot; *faynt-HAR-tid*
**Русский:** робкий, малодушный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more faint-hearted*, *most faint-hearted*
**Form:** compound — *faint* + *hearted*
**Opposite:** *bold*
**Contrast:** Very often in the fixed phrase *not for the faint-hearted* = not for people who scare easily.

Easily frightened and lacking the nerve to act.

1. The hike along the ridge is not for the *faint-hearted*.
2. He was too *faint-hearted* to ask for a raise.
3. *Faint-hearted* investors sold everything that week.

---

### fainthearted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfeɪntˈhɑːrtɪd/ &middot; *faynt-HAHR-tid*
**Русский:** малодушный, робкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fainthearted*, *most fainthearted*
**Form:** compound — *faint* + *heart* + -ed; also spelled *faint-hearted*
**Contrast:** Most often met in *not for the fainthearted* (not for people who scare easily), where *the fainthearted* is a noun.

Lacking courage; easily frightened or discouraged.

1. *Fainthearted* investors sold everything when prices fell.
2. Congress has been too *fainthearted* to raise taxes.
3. Don't be so *fainthearted* — just ask her out.

---

### fair

**Pronunciation:** /fɛr/ &middot; *FAIR*
**Русский:** справедливый; разумный; светлый; ясный
**Type:** evaluative; also color
**Position:** both — in the 'fairly large' sense only before a noun: *a fair number*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fairer*, *fairest*
**Takes:** *to* + person: *It isn't fair to her*; *to*-infinitive in *it's fair to say*
**Opposite:** *unfair*
**Contrast:** Russian *светлый* about hair and skin is *fair* in a slightly formal style; everyday American English more often says *blond* hair and *light* or *pale* skin. *A fair* is a noun (*the county fair*), and in *play fair* the word is an adverb.

Fair has 6 distinct senses.

**1. Treating people equally and according to the rules; just.** (справедливый, честный)

1. It's not *fair* that she gets more money for the same job.
2. The judge was strict but *fair*.
3. Everyone deserves a *fair* trial.

**2. Reasonable and acceptable in the circumstances.** (разумный, приемлемый)

4. We got a *fair* price for the car.
5. That's a *fair* question.
6. It's *fair* to say the plan didn't work.

**3. Fairly large in amount, number or distance.** (изрядный, немалый)

7. It's a *fair* distance from here to the lake.
8. A *fair* number of people left early.

**4. Neither very good nor very bad; average.** (удовлетворительный, средний)

9. His condition is only *fair* after the surgery.
10. The car is in *fair* shape for its age.

**5. Of hair or skin: light in color.** (светлый)

11. People with *fair* skin burn easily.
12. She has *fairer* hair than her sister.
13. The baby had *fair* curls.

**6. Of weather: pleasant and dry, without rain.** (ясный, хороший (о погоде))

14. The forecast is *fair* and sunny for the weekend.
15. We sailed out under *fair* skies.

---

### fair-haired

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɛrˈhɛrd/ &middot; *fair-HAIRD*
**Русский:** светловолосый; любимчик
**Type:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed
**Position:** both — in the 'favorite' sense only before a noun: *the boss's fair-haired boy*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *fair* + *hair* + -ed

Fair-haired has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having light-colored hair.** (светловолосый)

1. A *fair-haired* girl answered the door.
2. Both boys are *fair-haired* like their dad.

**2. Favored by someone in power — in *fair-haired boy*, informal American.** (любимчик, фаворит)

3. He's the boss's *fair-haired* boy and gets every promotion.
4. For a few years she was the network's *fair-haired* girl.

---

### fair-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɛrˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *fair-MYN-did*
**Русский:** беспристрастный, справедливый
**Type:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fair-minded*, *most fair-minded*
**Form:** compound — *fair* + *mind* + -ed

Judging people and situations fairly, without prejudice.

1. We need a *fair-minded* person to settle this.
2. Most *fair-minded* voters would agree.
3. He's *more fair-minded* than most of his colleagues.

---

### faithful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪθfəl/ &middot; *FAYTH-fuhl*
**Русский:** верный, преданный; точный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more faithful*, *most faithful*
**Takes:** *to* + person, belief or original: *faithful to his wife*, *faithful to the book*
**Form:** -ful from *faith*
**Opposite:** *unfaithful*
**Contrast:** About a husband, wife or partner, *faithful* means not having sex with anyone else, and *unfaithful* means cheating.

Faithful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Loyal; staying with a person, belief or group.** (верный, преданный)

1. Max was a *faithful* dog who followed her everywhere.
2. She's been a *faithful* customer for twenty years.
3. He stayed *faithful* to his wife for fifty years.

**2. Accurate; true to the original.** (точный)

4. The movie is *faithful* to the novel.
5. It's the *most faithful* translation I've read.

---

### fake

**Pronunciation:** /feɪk/ &middot; *FAYK*
**Русский:** поддельный, фальшивый; притворный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but about insincere people *so fake* and *more fake* are common in speech
**Opposite:** *real*; *genuine*
**Contrast:** *Fake* vs *false*: *fake* is for copies made to fool people (*fake money*, *a fake ID*); *false* is for things that are not true (*a false statement*) and a few replacements (*false teeth*). *Fake* is also a verb (*He faked an injury*) and a noun (*The painting is a fake*).

Fake has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made to look like something real, to trick people or to cost less.** (поддельный, искусственный)

1. The police arrested him for using a *fake* ID.
2. Is that fur real or *fake*?
3. The site is full of *fake* reviews.

**2. Pretended; not sincere.** (притворный, фальшивый)

4. She gave me a big *fake* smile.
5. I can't stand him — he's so *fake*.
6. His Southern accent sounds *fake*.

---

### fallacious

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈleɪʃəs/ &middot; *fuh-LAY-shuhs*
**Русский:** ошибочный, ложный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *fallacy*

Based on a false idea or bad reasoning — formal.

1. The argument is *fallacious* because it assumes what it tries to prove.
2. The ad makes several *fallacious* claims.
3. Critics called the report's reasoning *fallacious*.

---

### fallen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːlən/ &middot; *FAW-luhn*
**Русский:** упавший, опавший; павший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *fallen leaves*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *fall*
**Contrast:** *The fallen* is also a noun: soldiers killed in war.

Fallen has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Lying on the ground after falling.** (упавший, опавший)

1. The yard was covered in *fallen* leaves.
2. A *fallen* tree blocked the road.

**2. Killed in a war.** (павший)

3. Memorial Day honors *fallen* soldiers.
4. The town built a monument to its *fallen* heroes.

**3. Having lost power, status or a good moral reputation.** (свергнутый, падший)

5. The movie is about a *fallen* star trying to make a comeback.
6. The *fallen* dictator fled the country.

---

### fallible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæləbəl/ &middot; *FAL-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** склонный ошибаться, небезупречный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fallible*, *most fallible*
**Opposite:** *infallible*

Able to make mistakes or be wrong.

1. Doctors are human and *fallible* like everyone else.
2. Memory is *fallible*, so write it down.
3. Eyewitness testimony is *more fallible* than most juries think.

---

### falling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːlɪŋ/ &middot; *FAW-ling*
**Русский:** падающий; снижающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after *be* it is the verb: *Prices are falling*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *fall*

Falling has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Coming down toward the ground.** (падающий)

1. Watch out for *falling* rocks.
2. He was hit by a *falling* branch.
3. A *falling* star streaked across the sky.

**2. Going down in amount or level.** (снижающийся, падающий)

4. *Falling* prices are good news for shoppers.
5. The company is struggling with *falling* sales.

---

### fallow

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæloʊ/ &middot; *FAL-oh*
**Русский:** под паром; непродуктивный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — often in *lie fallow* and *leave fallow*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Fallow has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of farmland: left without crops for a season so the soil can recover.** (под паром, незасеянный)

1. The farmer leaves one field *fallow* every year.
2. Weeds grew over the *fallow* land.

**2. Of a period of time: when little is done or produced.** (непродуктивный, застойный)

3. After his first hit, he went through a long *fallow* period.
4. The *fallow* years between albums were hard for the band.

---

### false

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːls/ &middot; *FAWLS*
**Русский:** ложный, неверный; искусственный; притворный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — in the 'artificial' sense only before a noun: *false teeth*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *true*
**Contrast:** *False* vs *fake*: *a false statement* is not true; *a fake Rolex* is a copy made to fool you. Russian *фальшивые деньги* are *counterfeit* or *fake money*, not *false money*.

False has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Not true or correct.** (ложный, неверный)

1. The rumor turned out to be *false*.
2. True or *false*: the sun is a star.
3. He gave the police a *false* name.

**2. Based on a mistake; wrong.** (ошибочный, напрасный)

4. It was a *false* alarm — there was no fire.
5. Don't give her *false* hope.
6. I had a *false* impression of him.

**3. Not natural; artificial.** (искусственный, накладной)

7. My grandfather has *false* teeth.
8. She wore *false* eyelashes to the party.

**4. Not sincere; pretended.** (притворный, фальшивый)

9. Spare me the *false* modesty.
10. Her cheerfulness sounded *false*.

---

### faltering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːltərɪŋ/ &middot; *FAWL-tur-ing*
**Русский:** слабеющий; неуверенный, запинающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after *be* it is usually the verb: *The economy is faltering*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *falter*

Faltering has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Weak or unsteady and likely to stop or fail.** (слабеющий, неустойчивый)

1. The tax cuts were meant to boost the *faltering* economy.
2. The coach tried to save his *faltering* career.
3. She took a few *faltering* steps.

**2. Of speech: hesitant and full of pauses.** (неуверенный, запинающийся)

4. He answered in *faltering* English.
5. In a *faltering* voice, she thanked the crowd.

---

### famed

**Pronunciation:** /feɪmd/ &middot; *FAYMD*
**Русский:** знаменитый, прославленный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun: *famed for its beaches*
**Form:** -ed from the noun *fame*

Famous — used mostly in writing.

1. We ate at the *famed* diner on Main Street.
2. The town is *famed* for its maple syrup.
3. She studied under the *famed* architect Frank Gehry.

---

### familial

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈmɪliəl/ &middot; *fuh-MIL-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** семейный; наследственный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *familial ties*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *family*
**Contrast:** Formal and medical; in everyday speech use *family* before a noun: *family ties*, *family history*. Don't confuse it with *familiar* (знакомый).

Relating to a family, or passed down through families.

1. The disease has a strong *familial* link.
2. She felt a strong sense of *familial* duty.
3. The study looked at *familial* patterns of heart disease.

---

### famished

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæmɪʃt/ &middot; *FAM-isht*
**Русский:** страшно голодный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — usually after a verb: *I'm famished*
**Comparison:** not gradable — an extreme adjective: *absolutely famished*

Extremely hungry — informal.

1. I'm *famished* — let's get something to eat.
2. The kids came home from practice *famished*.
3. A couple of *famished* hikers wandered into the diner.

---

### fanatical

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈnætɪkəl/ &middot; *fuh-NAT-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** фанатичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fanatical*, *most fanatical*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -al from *fanatic*

Extremely enthusiastic about something, often too much.

1. He's *fanatical* about keeping his car clean.
2. The band has a *fanatical* following.
3. She became even *more fanatical* about fitness after college.

---

### fanciful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfænsɪfəl/ &middot; *FAN-si-fuhl*
**Русский:** надуманный, нереальный; причудливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fanciful*, *most fanciful*
**Form:** -ful from *fancy*

Fanciful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Based on imagination rather than facts; not realistic.** (надуманный, нереальный)

1. The idea that we'll finish by Friday is a little *fanciful*.
2. His story sounded *fanciful*, but it was true.

**2. Decorated in an unusual, imaginative way.** (причудливый)

3. The gates were covered in *fanciful* ironwork.
4. The book has *fanciful* drawings of dragons and castles.

---

### fancy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfænsi/ &middot; *FAN-see*
**Русский:** шикарный, дорогой; нарядный; замысловатый
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fancier*, *fanciest*
**Opposite:** *plain*; *simple*
**Contrast:** Only the adjective is common in American English; *I fancy you* (I like you) and *Fancy a drink?* are British. *Nothing fancy* means simple and ordinary.

Fancy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Expensive and stylish, for rich or important people.** (шикарный, дорогой)

1. They took us to a *fancy* restaurant downtown.
2. We stayed at the *fanciest* hotel in town.
3. Don't get too dressed up — it's not a *fancy* party.

**2. Decorated; not plain.** (нарядный, с украшениями)

4. The cake had *fancy* decorations on top.
5. I just want a plain T-shirt, nothing *fancy*.

**3. Complicated, clever or technically advanced.** (замысловатый, мудрёный)

6. You don't need a *fancy* camera to take good photos.
7. The coach doesn't like *fancy* moves — just pass the ball.
8. He uses a lot of *fancy* words to sound smart.

---

### fantastical

**Pronunciation:** /fænˈtæstɪkəl/ &middot; *fan-TAS-ti-kuhl*
**Русский:** фантастический, сказочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fantastical*, *most fantastical*
**Form:** -al from *fantastic*
**Contrast:** In everyday speech *fantastic* usually means 'wonderful'; *fantastical* keeps the older meaning 'strange and imaginary'.

Strange and imaginary, like something from a dream or a fairy tale.

1. The film is set in a *fantastical* world of talking animals.
2. Her paintings are full of *fantastical* creatures.
3. The costumes were even *more fantastical* than the sets.

---

### far left

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɑːr ˈlɛft/ &middot; *far LEFT*
**Русский:** крайне левый, ультралевый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *a far-left group*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *far right*
**Contrast:** Hyphenated before a noun (*a far-left candidate*), two words after a verb (*Her views are far left*). *The far left* is a noun — the people themselves.

Holding political views at the extreme left end of the scale.

1. The party expelled its *far-left* wing.
2. Her economics professor is *far left*.
3. *Far-left* and far-right groups both showed up that day.

---

### far-flung

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɑːrˈflʌŋ/ &middot; *fahr-FLUHNG*
**Русский:** отдалённый; разбросанный, обширный
**Type:** compound — adverb + past participle
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more far-flung*, *most far-flung*
**Form:** compound — *far* + *flung*, past participle of *fling*

Far-flung has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very far away.** (отдалённый, далёкий)

1. She's traveled to *far-flung* places like Mongolia and Chile.
2. The company has offices in *far-flung* corners of the world.

**2. Spread out over a large area.** (разбросанный, обширный)

3. My family is so *far-flung* that we rarely all meet.
4. The empire was huge and *far-flung*.

---

### far-left

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɑːrˈlɛft/ &middot; *fahr-LEFT*
**Русский:** ультралевый
**Type:** compound — adverb + adjective
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *far-left groups*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *far* + *left*
**Opposite:** *far-right*
**Contrast:** *The far left*, with no hyphen, is the noun: the people with the most extreme left-wing views.

Holding very extreme left-wing political views.

1. The *far-left* candidate won only 3 percent of the vote.
2. Some voters see his views as *far-left*.
3. The protest was organized by several *far-left* groups.

---

### far-off

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɑːrˈɔːf/ &middot; *fahr-AWF*
**Русский:** далёкий
**Type:** compound — adverb + adverb
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb use *far off* or *far away*: *The day is not far off*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *far* + *off*

Distant in space or time.

1. The story takes place in a *far-off* land.
2. We could hear *far-off* thunder.
3. Retirement still seems like a *far-off* dream.

---

### far-right

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɑːrˈraɪt/ &middot; *fahr-RYT*
**Русский:** ультраправый
**Type:** compound — adverb + adjective
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *far-right parties*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *far* + *right*
**Opposite:** *far-left*
**Contrast:** *The far right*, with no hyphen, is the noun: the people with the most extreme right-wing views.

Holding very extreme right-wing political views.

1. The *far-right* party gained seats in parliament.
2. Critics called the website *far-right*.
3. Police are watching several *far-right* groups.

---

### faraway

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːrəˌweɪ/ &middot; *FAHR-uh-way*
**Русский:** далёкий; отсутствующий (о взгляде)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb use two words: *The town is far away*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *far* + *away*

Faraway has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Distant.** (далёкий)

1. She dreamed of *faraway* places.
2. We could see the lights of a *faraway* town.

**2. Of a look or voice: dreamy, as if thinking about something else.** (отсутствующий, мечтательный)

3. He had a *faraway* look in his eyes.
4. She answered in a *faraway* voice.

---

### farcical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːrsɪkəl/ &middot; *FAHR-si-kuhl*
**Русский:** нелепый, фарсовый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more farcical*, *most farcical*
**Form:** -al from *farce*

So badly organized or silly that it is ridiculous.

1. The trial was a *farcical* waste of time.
2. The meeting became *farcical* when nobody could find the agenda.
3. Each excuse was *more farcical* than the last.

---

### farfetched

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɑːrˈfɛtʃt/ &middot; *fahr-FECHT*
**Русский:** надуманный, неправдоподобный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more farfetched*, *most farfetched*
**Form:** compound — *far* + *fetched*; also spelled *far-fetched*

Very unlikely to be true or to happen.

1. His excuse sounded pretty *farfetched*.
2. The plot of the movie is *farfetched* but fun.
3. It's not as *farfetched* as it sounds.

---

### farsighted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːrˌsaɪtɪd/ &middot; *FAHR-sy-tid*
**Русский:** дальнозоркий; дальновидный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more farsighted*, *most farsighted*
**Form:** compound — *far* + *sight* + -ed
**Opposite:** *nearsighted* (eyes); *shortsighted* (planning)
**Contrast:** In American English the eye words are *farsighted* (дальнозоркий) and *nearsighted* (близорукий); British English says *long-sighted* and *short-sighted*.

Farsighted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to see things far away clearly, but not things close to you.** (дальнозоркий)

1. I'm *farsighted*, so I need glasses to read.
2. Many people become *farsighted* after forty.

**2. Thinking carefully about the future and planning for it.** (дальновидный)

3. Buying land there in the 1970s was a *farsighted* decision.
4. The city's *farsighted* leaders built the subway early.

---

### fascist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæʃɪst/ &middot; *FASH-ist*
**Русский:** фашистский
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — mostly in the insulting sense: *more fascist*
**Form:** -ist from *fascism*
**Contrast:** *Fascist* is the general word for this kind of politics; *Nazi* is specific to Hitler's Germany. *A fascist* is also a noun.

Fascist has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to fascism: rule by a dictator, with extreme nationalism and no opposition allowed.** (фашистский)

1. Mussolini led Italy's *fascist* government.
2. The group was banned for spreading *fascist* ideas.

**2. Very controlling and intolerant — used as an insult.** (диктаторский, авторитарный)

3. My old boss was totally *fascist* about the dress code.
4. Protesters called the new law *fascist*.

---

### fashionable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæʃənəbəl/ &middot; *FASH-uh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** модный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fashionable*, *most fashionable*
**Form:** -able from *fashion*
**Opposite:** *unfashionable*

Popular at a particular time, or used by people who follow the latest style.

1. She always wears *fashionable* clothes.
2. They live in a *fashionable* neighborhood in Brooklyn.
3. Small weddings have become *more fashionable* lately.

---

### fast

**Pronunciation:** /fæst/ &middot; *FAST*
**Русский:** быстрый; спешащий (о часах); верный, прочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — in the 'clock' sense only after a verb: *My watch is fast*; in *fast friends* only before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *faster*, *fastest*
**Opposite:** *slow*
**Contrast:** *Fast* is also the adverb: *drive fast*, never *fastly*. *Fast* is mostly about speed (*a fast car*); *quick* is about taking a short time (*a quick question*), though they often overlap. In *fast asleep* it means 'firmly', and the verb *to fast* means to go without food.

Fast has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Moving or able to move at high speed.** (быстрый, скоростной)

1. He drives a really *fast* car.
2. She's the *fastest* runner on the team.
3. The *fast* lane is on the left.

**2. Doing something or happening in a short time.** (быстрый, скорый)

4. He's a *fast* learner.
5. The internet here is much *faster* than at home.
6. We need a *fast* decision.

**3. Of a clock or watch: showing a time later than the real time.** (спешащий)

7. My watch is five minutes *fast*.
8. The clock in the car is *fast*, so don't panic.

**4. Firm and lasting — in *fast friends*, and of dye that does not wash out.** (верный, прочный; нелиняющий)

9. The two girls became *fast* friends at camp.
10. Wash it in cold water — the dye isn't *fast*.

---

### fast asleep

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfæst əˈsliːp/ &middot; *fast uh-SLEEP*
**Русский:** крепко спящий
**Type:** quality — a fixed phrase
**Position:** predicative only — *The baby is fast asleep*, not *a fast asleep baby*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *fast* 'firmly' + *asleep*
**Contrast:** Here *fast* does not mean 'quick': *fast asleep* means sleeping deeply, not falling asleep quickly.

Sleeping deeply.

1. By nine o'clock the kids were *fast asleep*.
2. I found him *fast asleep* on the couch.
3. She was *fast asleep* before the movie ended.

---

### fast-growing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfæstˈɡroʊɪŋ/ &middot; *fast-GROH-ing*
**Русский:** быстрорастущий
**Type:** compound — adjective + -ing participle
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *faster-growing*, *fastest-growing*
**Form:** compound — *fast* + *growing*

Growing or getting bigger quickly.

1. Austin is one of the *fastest-growing* cities in the country.
2. Bamboo is a *fast-growing* plant.
3. The company is small but *fast-growing*.

---

### fast-paced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfæstˈpeɪst/ &middot; *fast-PAYST*
**Русский:** динамичный, стремительный
**Type:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *faster-paced*, *more fast-paced*
**Form:** compound — *fast* + *pace* + -ed

Happening or moving quickly, with a lot going on.

1. She works in a *fast-paced* newsroom.
2. The movie is *fast-paced* and full of action.
3. Life in New York is *faster-paced* than in Ohio.

---

### fastidious

**Pronunciation:** /fæˈstɪdiəs/ &middot; *fa-STID-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** придирчивый, педантичный, привередливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fastidious*, *most fastidious*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*

Very careful about details, cleanliness or order, sometimes too careful.

1. He's *fastidious* about keeping his desk clean.
2. The restaurant is run by a *fastidious* chef.
3. When it comes to grammar, she's *more fastidious* than anyone I know.

---

### fat-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfætˈfriː/ &middot; *fat-FREE*
**Русский:** обезжиренный
**Type:** compound — noun + adjective
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *fat* + *-free* 'without'
**Contrast:** *Fat-free* means with no fat at all; *low-fat* means with a little. *Fat-free* food can still be high in sugar and calories.

Containing no fat.

1. I'll have a *fat-free* latte, please.
2. This yogurt is *fat-free* but full of sugar.
3. The store only sells *fat-free* milk.

---

### fatal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪtəl/ &middot; *FAY-tuhl*
**Русский:** смертельный; роковой, губительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + person or thing: *fatal to her campaign*
**Form:** -al from *fate*
**Contrast:** Russian *фатальный* often means 'fateful' or 'inevitable'; English *fatal* means 'causing death' or 'causing ruin'. *A fatal accident* is one in which someone died. For 'deciding the future', use *fateful*.

Fatal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Causing death.** (смертельный)

1. There was a *fatal* accident on the highway last night.
2. The disease is almost always *fatal*.
3. He suffered a *fatal* heart attack.

**2. Causing failure or very bad results.** (роковой, губительный)

4. Trusting him was a *fatal* mistake.
5. The plan has one *fatal* flaw.
6. The scandal proved *fatal* to her campaign.

---

### fatalistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfeɪtəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *fay-tuh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** фаталистический, фаталистичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fatalistic*, *most fatalistic*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** -istic from *fatalism*

Believing that events are decided by fate and cannot be changed.

1. He's *fatalistic* about his health — he says what will be will be.
2. She has a very *fatalistic* view of politics.
3. Older voters were *more fatalistic* about the economy.

---

### fated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪtɪd/ &middot; *FAY-tid*
**Русский:** обречённый; предначертанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — usually after a verb with a *to*-infinitive: *fated to fail*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive
**Form:** -ed from *fate*

Certain to happen or to do something, as if fate has decided it.

1. Their marriage seemed *fated* to fail from the start.
2. Do you think we were *fated* to meet?
3. The novel tells the story of two *fated* lovers.

---

### fateful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪtfəl/ &middot; *FAYT-fuhl*
**Русский:** роковой, судьбоносный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — usually before a noun: *that fateful day*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ful from *fate*
**Contrast:** *Fateful* means having a very important, often bad, effect on the future; *fatal* means causing death or ruin. *A fateful decision* changed everything; *a fatal decision* led to disaster.

Having an important, often bad, effect on the future.

1. On that *fateful* morning, she decided to take the train.
2. It was a *fateful* decision that changed his life.
3. The *fateful* meeting took place in 1962.

---

### fatherless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːðərləs/ &middot; *FAH-thur-luhs*
**Русский:** оставшийся без отца
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *father*

Without a father, because he has died or is not part of the child's life.

1. She grew up *fatherless* after her dad died in the war.
2. The charity helps *fatherless* children.
3. Millions of kids are growing up *fatherless*.

---

### fatigued

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈtiːɡd/ &middot; *fuh-TEEGD*
**Русский:** утомлённый, уставший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fatigued*, *most fatigued*
**Form:** -ed from *fatigue*
**Contrast:** *Fatigued* is formal or medical; in conversation say *tired* or *exhausted*.

Very tired — formal.

1. Patients may feel *fatigued* for several weeks.
2. The *fatigued* soldiers slept wherever they could.
3. I felt *more fatigued* after the second dose.

---

### fattening

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfætənɪŋ/ &middot; *FAT-uh-ning*
**Русский:** калорийный, от которого толстеют
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fattening*, *most fattening*
**Form:** -ing from *fatten* — what causes weight gain

Likely to make you gain weight.

1. I love fries, but they're so *fattening*.
2. Try to avoid *fattening* snacks late at night.
3. Is beer *more fattening* than wine?

---

### fatty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfæti/ &middot; *FAT-ee*
**Русский:** жирный; жировой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fattier*, *fattiest*
**Form:** -y from *fat*
**Contrast:** *Fatty* describes food and body tissue; a person is *fat* or, more politely, *overweight*. As a noun, *fatty* is a rude name for a fat person.

Fatty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Containing a lot of fat.** (жирный)

1. Cut down on *fatty* foods like burgers and fries.
2. This cut of meat is *fattier* than chicken breast.
3. The soup was too *fatty* for me.

**2. Made of fat or relating to fat in the body — in medicine and science.** (жировой)

4. Doctors found *fatty* deposits in his arteries.
5. Omega-3 is a type of *fatty* acid.

---

### faultless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːltləs/ &middot; *FAWLT-luhs*
**Русский:** безупречный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *fault*

Without any mistakes; perfect.

1. Her English is *faultless*.
2. The pianist gave a *faultless* performance.
3. His driving record is *faultless*.

---

### faulty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːlti/ &middot; *FAWL-tee*
**Русский:** неисправный, бракованный; ошибочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -y from *fault*

Faulty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not working correctly, or not made correctly.** (неисправный, бракованный)

1. The fire was caused by *faulty* wiring.
2. The company recalled thousands of *faulty* airbags.
3. If the charger is *faulty*, the store will replace it.

**2. Of reasoning or information: containing mistakes.** (ошибочный)

4. The study was based on *faulty* data.
5. That's *faulty* logic.

---

### Faustian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfaʊstiən/ &middot; *FOW-stee-uhn*
**Русский:** фаустовский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a Faustian bargain*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Faust*, the man in the legend who sold his soul to the devil
**Contrast:** Almost always in *a Faustian bargain* or *a Faustian deal*: giving up your values or your future for power, money or success now. Always with a capital F.

Giving up something morally important in exchange for power, money or success.

1. He made a *Faustian* bargain with the donors who paid for his campaign.
2. Taking the money was a *Faustian* deal she later regretted.
3. The novel explores the *Faustian* temptations of fame.

---

### faux

**Pronunciation:** /foʊ/ &middot; *FOH*
**Русский:** искусственный; притворный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *faux fur*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Pronounced FOH: the x is silent, as in French. In stores *faux* is the polite word, so *faux leather* sounds better than *fake leather*.

Faux has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made to look like a real material; artificial.** (искусственный)

1. She bought a *faux* fur coat.
2. The chairs are covered in *faux* leather.
3. The kitchen has *faux* marble countertops.

**2. Pretended; not sincere — often humorous.** (притворный, показной)

4. He answered with *faux* innocence.
5. She let out a gasp of *faux* surprise.

---

### favorable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪvərəbəl/ &middot; *FAY-vur-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** положительный, благосклонный; благоприятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more favorable*, *most favorable*
**Takes:** *to* or *toward* + an idea or person (approving of it); *for* + what it helps
**Form:** -able from *favor*
**Opposite:** *unfavorable*

Favorable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Showing approval; positive.** (положительный, благосклонный)

1. The movie got *favorable* reviews.
2. Most voters have a *favorable* opinion of her.
3. The board was *favorable* to the idea.

**2. Good for someone or something; likely to help success.** (благоприятный, выгодный)

4. Conditions are *favorable* for the launch.
5. We got a loan on *favorable* terms.
6. Interest rates are *more favorable* now than last year.

---

### favored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪvərd/ &middot; *FAY-vurd*
**Русский:** предпочитаемый, излюбленный; являющийся фаворитом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more favored*, *most favored*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive in the 'expected to win' sense: *favored to win*
**Form:** -ed from *favor*

Favored has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Preferred; liked more than others.** (предпочитаемый, излюбленный)

1. Bikes are the *favored* way to get around campus.
2. It's a *favored* vacation spot for wealthy New Yorkers.
3. This was the *most favored* option in the survey.

**2. Expected to win.** (являющийся фаворитом)

4. The Lakers are heavily *favored* to win tonight.
5. She's the *favored* candidate in the race.

---

### favorite

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfeɪvərɪt/ &middot; *FAY-vur-it*
**Русский:** любимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *my favorite song*; after a verb it is the noun: *This one is my favorite*
**Comparison:** not gradable — *most favorite* is heard but avoided in careful English
**Contrast:** *Favorite* already means 'liked most', so *my most favorite* is redundant. In sports, *the favorite* (a noun) is the team or player expected to win.

Liked more than all others of the same kind.

1. What's your *favorite* movie?
2. Pizza is my *favorite* food.
3. We went back to our *favorite* restaurant for our anniversary.

---

### fawning

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːnɪŋ/ &middot; *FAW-ning*
**Русский:** угодливый, подобострастный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fawning*, *most fawning*
**Form:** -ing from the verb *fawn* (*over* someone), to flatter
**Contrast:** The verb *fawn* has nothing to do with *a fawn*, a baby deer (оленёнок).

Praising someone too much in order to win their approval.

1. The singer was surrounded by *fawning* fans.
2. The article was a *fawning* profile of the CEO.
3. Critics said the interview was far too *fawning*.

---

### fazed

**Pronunciation:** /feɪzd/ &middot; *FAYZD*
**Русский:** смущённый, выбитый из колеи
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *She wasn't fazed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *faze*
**Contrast:** Almost always with a negative: *not fazed*, *didn't seem fazed*. It sounds the same as *phased* (*phased out*), which is a different word.

Worried, confused or upset by something — usually with *not*.

1. She wasn't *fazed* by the tough questions.
2. The pilot didn't seem *fazed* at all.
3. The kids weren't *fazed* by the long drive.

---

### fearful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪrfəl/ &middot; *FEER-fuhl*
**Русский:** боящийся, опасающийся; испуганный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fearful*, *most fearful*
**Takes:** *of* + noun or *-ing*; *that*-clause; *for* + the person you are worried about
**Form:** -ful from *fear*
**Opposite:** *fearless*
**Contrast:** *Fearful* is more formal than *afraid* or *scared* and describes the person who feels fear; *fearsome* describes what causes it.

Afraid, or worried that something bad will happen.

1. Many residents are *fearful* of another flood.
2. The dog gave a *fearful* look and ran away.
3. Parents are *more fearful* for their kids' safety than they used to be.

---

### fearless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪrləs/ &middot; *FEER-luhs*
**Русский:** бесстрашный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fearless*, *most fearless*
**Form:** -less from *fear*
**Opposite:** *fearful*

Not afraid of anything.

1. She's a *fearless* reporter who will ask anyone anything.
2. Little kids can be totally *fearless* around water.
3. He grew *more fearless* with every climb.

---

### fearsome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪrsəm/ &middot; *FEER-suhm*
**Русский:** грозный, устрашающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fearsome*, *most fearsome*
**Form:** -some from *fear* — causing fear

Frightening, or so powerful that people are afraid of it.

1. The team has a *fearsome* defense.
2. The grizzly bear is a *fearsome* animal.
3. The storm looked *fearsome* on the radar.

---

### feasible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːzəbəl/ &middot; *FEE-zuh-buhl*
**Русский:** осуществимый, выполнимый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more feasible*, *most feasible*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive, usually in *it is feasible to*
**Opposite:** *impractical*; *not feasible*

Possible to do and likely to work.

1. Is it *feasible* to finish the project by June?
2. We need a *feasible* plan, not a dream.
3. Solar power is becoming *more feasible* every year.

---

### feathered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛðərd/ &middot; *FETH-urd*
**Русский:** пернатый, с перьями
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *feather*

Feathered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having or covered with feathers.** (пернатый, с перьями)

1. Some dinosaurs were *feathered*.
2. She wore a *feathered* hat to the Kentucky Derby.
3. Our *feathered* friends need extra food in winter.

**2. Of hair: cut in soft layers that are brushed back from the face.** (уложенный «перьями» (о стрижке))

4. *Feathered* hair was hugely popular in the 1970s.
5. He still has the *feathered* haircut he had in high school.

---

### feathery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛðəri/ &middot; *FETH-uh-ree*
**Русский:** перистый, лёгкий как перо
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more feathery*, *most feathery*
**Form:** -y from *feather*

Soft, light or thin, like a feather.

1. Dill has *feathery* green leaves.
2. *Feathery* clouds drifted across the sky.
3. The snow was light and *feathery*.

---

### featured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːtʃərd/ &middot; *FEE-churd*
**Русский:** главный, представленный особо
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *the featured speaker*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *feature*

Given special attention as an important part of an event, show or page.

1. Tonight's *featured* speaker is the governor.
2. Check out the *featured* products on our home page.
3. The album's *featured* artists include several big names.

---

### fecal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːkəl/ &middot; *FEE-kuhl*
**Русский:** фекальный, каловый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *fecal matter*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *feces*

Relating to feces, the solid waste from the body — medical and technical.

1. The beach was closed because of *fecal* bacteria in the water.
2. The doctor ordered a *fecal* test.
3. *Fecal* matter can contaminate drinking water.

---

### feckless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛkləs/ &middot; *FEK-luhs*
**Русский:** никчёмный, безответственный, беспомощный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more feckless*, *most feckless*

Lacking the ability, effort or will to do anything useful; irresponsible.

1. Her *feckless* brother keeps asking to borrow money.
2. Critics called the city council *feckless*.
3. The leadership has never looked *more feckless*.

---

### fed up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɛd ˈʌp/ &middot; *fed UHP*
**Русский:** сытый по горло, доведённый до предела
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** predicative only — *I'm fed up*, not *a fed up person*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fed up*, *most fed up*
**Takes:** *with* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [fed up with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#fed-up-with)
**Contrast:** Americans say *fed up with*; *fed up of* is heard in British speech but is avoided in careful English.

Annoyed and tired of something that has gone on too long.

1. I'm *fed up* with this traffic.
2. Voters are *fed up* with both parties.
3. She finally got *fed up* and quit.

---

### federal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛdərəl/ &middot; *FED-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** федеральный; федеративный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a federal agency*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In the US, *federal* means the national government in Washington, as opposed to the state governments: *federal taxes* vs *state taxes*, *a federal crime*. Russian *федеративное государство* is *a federal state*.

Federal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the US national government, not the government of a state.** (федеральный)

1. Kidnapping is a *federal* crime.
2. He works for a *federal* agency in Washington.
3. *Federal* taxes are due in April.

**2. Of a country or system: made of states or regions that share power with a central government.** (федеративный)

4. The United States and Germany both have *federal* systems of government.
5. Canada is a *federal* country with ten provinces.

---

### feeble

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːbəl/ &middot; *FEE-buhl*
**Русский:** слабый, немощный; жалкий, неубедительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *feebler*, *feeblest*

Feeble has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Physically very weak.** (слабый, немощный)

1. Her grandmother was too *feeble* to walk alone.
2. He spoke in a *feeble* voice.

**2. Not effective or convincing.** (жалкий, неубедительный)

3. That's a *feeble* excuse.
4. The team made a *feeble* attempt to come back.
5. That was the *feeblest* joke I've ever heard.

---

### feigned

**Pronunciation:** /feɪnd/ &middot; *FAYND*
**Русский:** притворный, напускной
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *feigned surprise*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *feign*, to pretend
**Contrast:** The g is silent: FAYND.

Pretended; not real.

1. She listened with *feigned* interest.
2. He looked up in *feigned* surprise.
3. Her *feigned* indifference didn't fool anyone.

---

### feisty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfaɪsti/ &middot; *FY-stee*
**Русский:** бойкий, задиристый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *feistier*, *feistiest*

Full of energy and determination, and ready to argue or fight — often said with approval.

1. My *feisty* 90-year-old grandma still argues with everyone.
2. The little dog is *feistier* than the big one.
3. She gave a *feisty* speech against the plan.

---

### feline

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːlaɪn/ &middot; *FEE-lyn*
**Русский:** кошачий
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A feline* is also a noun, meaning a cat.

Feline has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to cats or the cat family.** (кошачий)

1. *Feline* diseases spread quickly in shelters.
2. Lions and tigers are *feline* predators.

**2. Like a cat, especially graceful, quiet or sly.** (кошачий, грациозный)

3. She moved with *feline* grace.
4. His eyes had a *feline* look.

---

### female

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːmeɪl/ &middot; *FEE-mayl*
**Русский:** женский; женского пола
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *male*
**Contrast:** *Female* is about sex, used for animals, forms and statistics (*female workers*, *a female cat*); *feminine* describes qualities traditionally linked to women. As a noun, *a female* for a woman sounds clinical or rude, so say *a woman*.

Female has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to the sex that can have babies or lay eggs.** (женский, женского пола)

1. Only *female* mosquitoes bite.
2. The company has only two *female* executives.
3. Is your kitten male or *female*?

**2. Of a plug or part: with a hole that another part fits into.** (с гнездом, «мама» (о разъёме))

4. You need a cable with a *female* connector on one end.
5. Screw the pipe into the *female* fitting.

---

### feminine

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛmənɪn/ &middot; *FEM-uh-nin*
**Русский:** женственный; женского рода
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more feminine*, *most feminine*
**Opposite:** *masculine*

Feminine has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having qualities traditionally thought typical of women.** (женственный)

1. She likes soft, *feminine* colors like pink and lavender.
2. The dress is very *feminine*.
3. Her new haircut looks *more feminine*.

**2. In grammar: belonging to the gender class that includes most words for women.** (женского рода)

4. In Spanish, *casa* is a *feminine* noun.
5. Russian adjectives change their endings to agree with *feminine* nouns.

---

### feminist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛmənɪst/ &middot; *FEM-uh-nist*
**Русский:** феминистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more feminist*, *most feminist*
**Form:** -ist from *feminine*

Supporting equal rights and opportunities for women.

1. She wrote several *feminist* books in the 1970s.
2. The movie has a strong *feminist* message.
3. Is the new version *more feminist* than the original?

---

### feral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛrəl/ &middot; *FER-uhl*
**Русский:** одичавший; дикий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more feral*, *most feral*

Feral has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of an animal: living wild after being tame, or born to such animals.** (одичавший, бродячий)

1. The neighborhood has a lot of *feral* cats.
2. *Feral* hogs are destroying farms in Texas.

**2. Wild and fierce, like a wild animal.** (дикий, звериный)

3. The crowd let out a *feral* roar.
4. After a week in the woods, the boys looked *feral*.

---

### ferocious

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈroʊʃəs/ &middot; *fuh-ROH-shuhs*
**Русский:** свирепый; яростный, жестокий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ferocious*, *most ferocious*

Ferocious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very fierce and violent.** (свирепый)

1. The guard dog looked *ferocious*.
2. Bears can be *ferocious* when they protect their cubs.

**2. Very strong or intense.** (яростный, жестокий, сильнейший)

3. The *ferocious* heat kept everyone inside.
4. The bill faced *ferocious* opposition in the Senate.
5. The competition is *more ferocious* than ever.

---

### fertile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːrtəl/ &middot; *FUR-tuhl*
**Русский:** плодородный; способный иметь детей; богатый (о воображении)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fertile*, *most fertile*
**Opposite:** *infertile*; *barren* (land)
**Contrast:** American English says FUR-tuhl; British English says FUR-tyl.

Fertile has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of land or soil: able to produce good crops.** (плодородный)

1. The valley has rich, *fertile* soil.
2. The Midwest has some of the *most fertile* farmland in the world.

**2. Of people or animals: able to have babies.** (способный иметь детей, фертильный)

3. Doctors ran tests to see if he was *fertile*.
4. Women are most *fertile* a few days each month.

**3. Of the mind or a situation: producing many ideas or good results.** (богатый; благодатный)

5. The writer has a *fertile* imagination.
6. The city was *fertile* ground for new bands.

---

### fervent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːrvənt/ &middot; *FUR-vuhnt*
**Русский:** пылкий, горячий, ярый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — usually before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fervent*, *most fervent*

Having or showing very strong and sincere feelings about something.

1. She's a *fervent* supporter of animal rights.
2. It is my *fervent* hope that they find a cure.
3. His fans are *more fervent* than ever.

---

### festive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛstɪv/ &middot; *FES-tiv*
**Русский:** праздничный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more festive*, *most festive*
**Contrast:** *The festive season* is mostly British; Americans usually say *the holidays* or *the holiday season*.

Cheerful and colorful in a way that suits a holiday or celebration.

1. The streets looked *festive* with all the lights.
2. Everyone was in a *festive* mood.
3. Let's make the table a little *more festive*.

---

### fetal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːtəl/ &middot; *FEE-tuhl*
**Русский:** внутриутробный, плодный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *fetal development*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *fetus*
**Contrast:** *The fetal position* is lying curled up on your side with your knees near your chest.

Relating to a baby that is still developing inside its mother.

1. The doctor checked the *fetal* heartbeat.
2. Drinking during pregnancy can harm *fetal* development.
3. He lay curled up in the *fetal* position.

---

### fetching

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɛtʃɪŋ/ &middot; *FECH-ing*
**Русский:** привлекательный, миловидный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fetching*, *most fetching*
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with the everyday verb *fetch* (сходить и принести); the adjective means 'attractive'.

Attractive and pleasant to look at.

1. She looked *fetching* in her new red coat.
2. What a *fetching* hat!
3. That's the most *fetching* photo of you I've ever seen.

---

### feudal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfjuːdəl/ &middot; *FYOO-duhl*
**Русский:** феодальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Connected with the medieval system in which people worked land owned by a lord; also used of anything run in that old-fashioned, top-down way.

1. Medieval Europe had a *feudal* system of lords and peasants.
2. The *feudal* lords owned most of the land.
3. The way that company treats its workers is almost *feudal*.

---

### feverish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːvərɪʃ/ &middot; *FEE-vuh-rish*
**Русский:** лихорадочный; температурящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more feverish*, *most feverish*
**Form:** -ish from *fever*

Feverish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a fever.** (с температурой, в жару)

1. The baby felt *feverish* last night.
2. If you're *feverish*, stay home from work.

**2. Very fast, excited and nervous.** (лихорадочный)

3. They worked at a *feverish* pace to finish on time.
4. The excitement grew more *feverish* as midnight approached.

---

### fiber-optic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfaɪbər ˈɑːptɪk/ &middot; *fy-bur-AHP-tik*
**Русский:** оптоволоконный
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *fiber* + *optic*

Using thin glass threads that carry information as light.

1. Our town finally got *fiber-optic* internet.
2. Workers were laying *fiber-optic* cable along the highway.
3. *Fiber-optic* lines carry data much faster than copper wires.

---

### fibrous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfaɪbrəs/ &middot; *FY-bruhs*
**Русский:** волокнистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fibrous*, *most fibrous*
**Form:** -ous from *fiber*

Made of or full of fibers.

1. Celery is a crunchy, *fibrous* vegetable.
2. The scar was made of tough, *fibrous* tissue.
3. Older asparagus stalks are more *fibrous* and harder to chew.

---

### fickle

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪkəl/ &middot; *FIK-uhl*
**Русский:** непостоянный, переменчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fickle*, *most fickle*

Fickle has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Often changing what one likes or who one is loyal to.** (непостоянный, ветреный)

1. Voters can be *fickle*.
2. He's a *fickle* friend who disappears when you need him.

**2. Of weather, luck or fame, changing suddenly and unpredictably.** (переменчивый)

3. The weather in April is *fickle*.
4. Fame is the most *fickle* thing in show business.

---

### fictional

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪkʃənəl/ &middot; *FIK-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** вымышленный, художественный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *fiction*
**Opposite:** *real*
**Contrast:** *Fictional* is about stories: *a fictional character*. *Fictitious* is about something false, made up to fool people: *a fictitious name*.

Existing only in a story, not in real life.

1. Sherlock Holmes is a *fictional* detective.
2. The town in the movie is *fictional*.
3. All the characters in this book are *fictional*.

---

### fictitious

**Pronunciation:** /fɪkˈtɪʃəs/ &middot; *fik-TISH-uhs*
**Русский:** фиктивный, ложный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Fictitious* means false and made up, often to deceive: *a fictitious address*. For characters in a story, *fictional* is the usual word.

Invented and not true, often to fool people.

1. He checked into the hotel under a *fictitious* name.
2. The company created *fictitious* invoices to hide the money.
3. The story he told the police turned out to be completely *fictitious*.

---

### fidgety

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪdʒɪti/ &middot; *FIJ-i-tee*
**Русский:** неусидчивый, беспокойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fidgety*, *most fidgety*
**Form:** -y from *fidget*

Unable to keep still, always moving the hands or body.

1. The kids got *fidgety* during the long ceremony.
2. He's a *fidgety* boy who can't sit still in class.
3. I get more *fidgety* when I drink too much coffee.

---

### fiduciary

**Pronunciation:** /fɪˈduːʃiˌɛri/ &middot; *fi-DOO-shee-air-ee*
**Русский:** фидуциарный, доверительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — almost always before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

In law and finance, involving a duty to manage money or property for someone else's benefit.

1. Financial advisers have a *fiduciary* duty to act in their clients' best interest.
2. The board was accused of breaching its *fiduciary* responsibilities.
3. The relationship between a trustee and a beneficiary is *fiduciary*.

---

### fiendish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːndɪʃ/ &middot; *FEEN-dish*
**Русский:** дьявольский; чертовски трудный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fiendish*, *most fiendish*
**Form:** -ish from *fiend*

Fiendish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cruel, evil and cunning.** (дьявольский, злодейский)

1. The villain came up with a *fiendish* plan to rob the bank.
2. He gave a *fiendish* laugh.

**2. Extremely difficult or cleverly complicated.** (чертовски трудный, заковыристый)

3. This week's crossword was *fiendish*.
4. It's the most *fiendish* puzzle I've ever tried.

---

### fierce

**Pronunciation:** /fɪrs/ &middot; *FEERS*
**Русский:** свирепый; ожесточённый, сильный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fiercer*, *fiercest*

Fierce has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Angry, aggressive and ready to attack.** (свирепый, яростный)

1. The dog looked *fierce*, but it was actually friendly.
2. The tribe was known for its *fierce* warriors.

**2. Very strong or intense.** (ожесточённый, сильный)

3. Competition for the job was *fiercer* than we expected.
4. A *fierce* storm knocked out power across the state.
5. She is a *fierce* defender of free speech.

---

### fiery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfaɪəri/ &middot; *FY-uh-ree*
**Русский:** огненный; пылкий, вспыльчивый; жгучий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fiery*, *most fiery*
**Form:** -y from *fire*

Fiery has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Full of fire, or bright red or orange like fire.** (огненный)

1. The plane went down in a *fiery* crash.
2. She has *fiery* red hair.

**2. Full of strong emotion, especially anger or passion.** (пылкий, вспыльчивый)

3. The senator gave a *fiery* speech.
4. Her brother has an even more *fiery* temper than she does.

**3. Of food, very hot and spicy.** (жгучий, острый)

5. This salsa is *fiery*, so go easy on it.
6. He likes his wings with a *fiery* chili sauce.

---

### fifty-fifty

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɪfti ˈfɪfti/ &middot; *fif-tee-FIF-tee*
**Русский:** равный, пятьдесят на пятьдесят
**Type:** quantity; also compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also an adverb: *We split the bill fifty-fifty*.

Divided equally between two; with an equal chance either way.

1. There's a *fifty-fifty* chance of rain tomorrow.
2. The two brothers have a *fifty-fifty* partnership.
3. The odds of winning are about *fifty-fifty*.

---

### figurative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪɡjərətɪv/ &middot; *FIG-yur-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** переносный, образный; фигуративный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *literal*; in art, *abstract*

Figurative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of words, used in a way different from their basic meaning to create an image.** (переносный, образный)

1. 'Break a leg' is a *figurative* expression.
2. I didn't mean it literally — it was *figurative*.
3. The poem is full of *figurative* language.

**2. Of art, showing people and things as they really look.** (фигуративный, изобразительный)

4. The museum has a large collection of *figurative* paintings.
5. Her early work was *figurative*, but she later turned to abstract art.

---

### Fijian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfiːdʒiən/ &middot; *FEE-jee-uhn*
**Русский:** фиджийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *A Fijian*, *Fijians* are nouns for the people.

From or connected with Fiji.

1. The *Fijian* rugby team is famous for its speed.
2. Our tour guide was *Fijian*.
3. We tried a *Fijian* raw fish dish called kokoda.

---

### Filipino

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɪləˈpiːnoʊ/ &middot; *fil-uh-PEE-noh*
**Русский:** филиппинский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The country is *the Philippines* (with *Ph*), but the adjective and the people are *Filipino* (with *F*); *Filipina* is also used for a woman.

From or connected with the Philippines.

1. There's a great *Filipino* restaurant downtown.
2. My grandmother is *Filipino*.
3. Adobo is a classic *Filipino* dish.

---

### financial

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈnænʃəl/ &middot; *fuh-NAN-shuhl*
**Русский:** финансовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *finance*

Connected with money and how it is managed.

1. She works in the *financial* industry.
2. We're having some *financial* problems this year.
3. The reasons for closing the store were purely *financial*.

---

### fine

**Pronunciation:** /faɪn/ &middot; *FYN*
**Русский:** нормальный; прекрасный; тонкий; мелкий
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both — the 'all right' sense mostly after a verb: *I'm fine*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *finer*, *finest*
**Contrast:** *I'm fine* means 'all right, nothing wrong', not 'wonderful'. *A fine* is a noun (штраф: *a parking fine*), and in speech *fine* is also an adverb: *The printer works fine*.

Fine has 5 distinct senses.

**1. All right, acceptable, or in good health.** (нормальный, в порядке)

1. "How are you?" "I'm *fine*, thanks."
2. Tuesday is *fine* with me.
3. Don't worry — the car is *fine*.

**2. Of very high quality.** (прекрасный, превосходный)

4. The restaurant serves *fine* wines.
5. This is the *finest* chocolate in the country.
6. He did a *fine* job on the kitchen.

**3. Very thin or narrow.** (тонкий)

7. Her hair is very *fine* and hard to style.
8. Draw a *fine* line along the edge with a pencil.

**4. Made of very small pieces.** (мелкий)

9. The beach is covered in *fine* white sand.
10. Use the *finer* side of the grater for the cheese.

**5. Small and subtle, so hard to notice or understand.** (тонкий, едва уловимый)

11. There's a *fine* line between confidence and arrogance.
12. The coach explained the *finer* points of the game.

---

### fine-grained

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfaɪn ˈɡreɪnd/ &middot; *fyn-GRAYND*
**Русский:** мелкозернистый; детальный
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fine-grained*, *most fine-grained*
**Form:** compound — *fine* + *grained*

Fine-grained has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of wood, stone or other material, having a smooth texture made of very small grains.** (мелкозернистый)

1. Maple is a hard, *fine-grained* wood.
2. The sculptor chose a *fine-grained* white marble.

**2. Very detailed, dealing with small parts separately.** (детальный, подробный)

3. The new app gives you more *fine-grained* control over your privacy settings.
4. We need a *fine-grained* analysis of the sales data.

---

### finicky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪnɪki/ &middot; *FIN-i-kee*
**Русский:** привередливый; капризный, мудрёный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more finicky*, *most finicky*
**Takes:** *about* + noun — fussy about something

Finicky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very hard to please, especially about small details.** (привередливый, разборчивый)

1. My cat is a *finicky* eater.
2. He's *finicky* about how his coffee is made.

**2. Of a task or machine, needing a lot of care and attention to work.** (капризный, мудрёный)

3. The old printer is *finicky* — you have to feed the paper by hand.
4. This is the most *finicky* recipe I've ever tried.

---

### finished

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪnɪʃt/ &middot; *FIN-isht*
**Русский:** законченный, готовый; конченый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the 'ruined' sense only after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun — done using or dealing with something
**Form:** -ed from *finish*
**Opposite:** *unfinished*

Finished has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Completed, or done with an activity.** (законченный, готовый)

1. Are you *finished* with the scissors?
2. The *finished* house looks amazing.
3. Once the paint dries, the table will be *finished*.

**2. Ruined, with no hope of success or of continuing.** (конченый, погибший)

4. After the scandal, his political career was *finished*.
5. If the bank says no, we're *finished*.

---

### finite

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfaɪnaɪt/ &middot; *FY-nyt*
**Русский:** конечный, ограниченный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *infinite*
**Contrast:** The opposite is pronounced differently: *finite* /ˈfaɪnaɪt/ FY-nyt, but *infinite* /ˈɪnfɪnət/ IN-fi-nuht.

Having a limit or an end.

1. The earth has a *finite* supply of oil.
2. Our time together is *finite*, so let's make the most of it.
3. There's only a *finite* number of tickets.

---

### Finnish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪnɪʃ/ &middot; *FIN-ish*
**Русский:** финский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *Finnish* is also the language (*She speaks Finnish*); the people are *Finns*. Do not confuse with *finish* (заканчивать).

From or connected with Finland.

1. Saunas are a big part of *Finnish* culture.
2. My coworker is *Finnish*.
3. She spent a winter in a *Finnish* village above the Arctic Circle.

---

### fire-resistant

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfaɪər rɪˈzɪstənt/ &middot; *fy-ur-ri-ZIS-tuhnt*
**Русский:** огнестойкий
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fire-resistant*, *most fire-resistant*
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective
**Contrast:** *Fire-resistant* means hard to burn or slow to burn; *fireproof* means fire cannot damage it at all.

Not burning easily, or protecting against fire for some time.

1. Firefighters wear *fire-resistant* suits.
2. Brick is more *fire-resistant* than wood.
3. The new curtains in the school auditorium are *fire-resistant*.

---

### fire-retardant

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfaɪər rɪˈtɑːrdənt/ &middot; *fy-ur-ri-TAR-duhnt*
**Русский:** огнезащитный
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective
**Contrast:** Same meaning as *flame-retardant*. Without the hyphen it is often a noun: *Planes dropped fire retardant on the hills*.

Treated with or made of a substance that slows the spread of fire.

1. The theater curtains are *fire-retardant*.
2. Workers sprayed a *fire-retardant* coating on the wooden beams.
3. Crews used *fire-retardant* gel to protect homes near the wildfire.

---

### fireproof

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfaɪərpruːf/ &middot; *FY-ur-proof*
**Русский:** огнеупорный, несгораемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *fire* + -proof ('not damaged by')

Not able to be damaged by fire.

1. Keep your passports in a *fireproof* safe.
2. The walls between the apartments are *fireproof*.
3. She bought a small *fireproof* box for important documents.

---

### firm

**Pronunciation:** /fɜːrm/ &middot; *FURM*
**Русский:** твёрдый; крепкий; строгий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *firmer*, *firmest*
**Takes:** *with* + person — strict with someone
**Contrast:** *A firm* is also a noun — a company: *a law firm*.

Firm has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Fairly hard and not soft when pressed.** (твёрдый, упругий)

1. I sleep better on a *firm* mattress.
2. Bake the cake until the top is *firm*.
3. Choose the *firmest* peaches for the pie.

**2. Strong and steady.** (крепкий)

4. He has a *firm* handshake.
5. Keep a *firm* grip on the rope.

**3. Strict and showing that you are in control.** (строгий, твёрдый)

6. You need to be *firm* with the kids about bedtime.
7. She's *firm* but fair with her students.

**4. Definite and not likely to change.** (твёрдый, окончательный)

8. We don't have a *firm* date for the wedding yet.
9. She's a *firm* believer in hard work.
10. Is that a *firm* offer?

---

### first-rate

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɜːrst ˈreɪt/ &middot; *furst-RAYT*
**Русский:** первоклассный
**Type:** evaluative; also compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *first* + *rate*
**Opposite:** *second-rate*

Excellent, of the best quality.

1. The hotel has a *first-rate* restaurant.
2. The service was *first-rate*.
3. She's a *first-rate* lawyer.

---

### first-string

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɜːrst ˈstrɪŋ/ &middot; *furst-STRING*
**Русский:** основной (о составе команды)
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *first* + *string*
**Opposite:** *second-string*
