In sports, belonging to the main team of best players, not the backups.

1. He's the *first-string* quarterback this season.
2. The coach rested his *first-string* players in the fourth quarter.
3. She finally earned a *first-string* spot on the varsity team.

---

### first-year

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɜːrst ˈjɪr/ &middot; *furst-YEER*
**Русский:** первого года, первокурсный
**Type:** classifying; also compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *first* + *year*
**Contrast:** At American schools and colleges *freshman* is the traditional word; many colleges now prefer the neutral *first-year*: *a first-year student*.

In the first year of study, work or operation.

1. All *first-year* students live on campus.
2. She's a *first-year* teacher at a public school.
3. The *first-year* costs were higher than we expected.

---

### firstborn

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːrstbɔːrn/ &middot; *FURST-born*
**Русский:** первородный, старший (о ребёнке)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *first* + *born*
**Contrast:** Often a noun: *Their firstborn is starting college* (первенец).

Born first in a family.

1. Their *firstborn* son was born in May.
2. Emma was their *firstborn* child.
3. *Firstborn* children are often expected to be more responsible.

---

### firsthand

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɜːrstˈhænd/ &middot; *furst-HAND*
**Русский:** из первых рук, личный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *first* + *hand*
**Contrast:** After a verb it is an adverb: *I saw it firsthand* (своими глазами).

Coming from your own experience or from seeing something yourself.

1. She has *firsthand* experience of running a small business.
2. We heard a *firsthand* account of the flood.
3. There's no substitute for *firsthand* knowledge.

---

### fiscal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪskəl/ &middot; *FIS-kuhl*
**Русский:** фискальный, бюджетный, финансовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Fiscal* is about government money and taxes, or an organization's accounting year. For a person's money, use *financial*: *financial problems*, not *fiscal problems*.

Connected with government money, taxes and spending, or with an organization's financial year.

1. The company's *fiscal* year ends in June.
2. Congress is debating *fiscal* policy.
3. The city is facing a *fiscal* crisis.

---

### fishy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪʃi/ &middot; *FISH-ee*
**Русский:** подозрительный; рыбный
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fishier*, *fishiest*
**Form:** -y from *fish*

Fishy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Informal: seeming dishonest or suspicious.** (подозрительный, сомнительный)

1. Something *fishy* is going on here.
2. His story sounds *fishy* to me.
3. The deal got even *fishier* when he refused to show us the contract.

**2. Tasting or smelling of fish.** (рыбный, с рыбным запахом)

4. The kitchen had a *fishy* smell.
5. This salmon tastes a little *fishy*.

---

### fitful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪtfəl/ &middot; *FIT-fuhl*
**Русский:** прерывистый, беспокойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fitful*, *most fitful*
**Form:** -ful from *fit* ('a short burst')

Starting and stopping, not steady or continuous.

1. I had a *fitful* night's sleep before the exam.
2. Progress on the project has been *fitful*.
3. His sleep was even more *fitful* after the surgery.

---

### fitted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪtɪd/ &middot; *FIT-id*
**Русский:** приталенный, облегающий; на резинке
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fitted*, *most fitted*
**Form:** -ed from *fit*

Made to follow the shape of the body or of the thing it covers.

1. She wore a *fitted* black blazer.
2. Put the *fitted* sheet on first, then the flat one.
3. This shirt is more *fitted* at the waist.

---

### fitting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *FIT-ing*
**Русский:** подходящий, уместный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fitting*, *most fitting*
**Takes:** *that*-clause or *to*-infinitive after *it is*; *for* + noun
**Contrast:** *A fitting* is a noun: примерка (*a dress fitting*) or a small part (*pipe fittings*).

Right or suitable for the situation.

1. It was a *fitting* end to a great season.
2. It's *fitting* that she won the award in her hometown.
3. I can't think of a more *fitting* tribute to him.

---

### fixed

**Pronunciation:** /fɪkst/ &middot; *FIKST*
**Русский:** фиксированный; починенный; неподвижный; подстроенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the 'repaired' sense only after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *fix*

Fixed has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Not changing; set at a particular amount or level.** (фиксированный, постоянный)

1. We pay a *fixed* rate on our mortgage.
2. The prices are *fixed*, so don't try to bargain.
3. Retirees on *fixed* incomes were hit hardest by inflation.

**2. Repaired and working again.** (починенный)

4. Is the dishwasher *fixed* yet?
5. Once the brakes are *fixed*, you can drive it again.

**3. Attached in one place so that it cannot move.** (закреплённый, неподвижный)

6. The camera stays in a *fixed* position.
7. The benches in the park are *fixed*, so you can't move them.

**4. Informal: arranged dishonestly so the result is decided in advance.** (подстроенный)

8. Fans claimed the fight was *fixed*.
9. Nobody believed it was anything but a *fixed* election.

---

### fizzy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɪzi/ &middot; *FIZ-ee*
**Русский:** газированный, шипучий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fizzier*, *fizziest*
**Form:** -y from *fizz*
**Opposite:** *flat*; for water, *still*

Of a drink, full of small bubbles of gas.

1. Kids love sweet *fizzy* drinks.
2. Do you want *fizzy* water or still?
3. This soda is *fizzier* than the one we had yesterday.

---

### flabbergasted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflæbərˌɡæstɪd/ &middot; *FLAB-ur-gas-tid*
**Русский:** ошеломлённый, потрясённый
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both — usually after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable — an extreme adjective: *absolutely flabbergasted*

Extremely surprised and shocked.

1. I was *flabbergasted* when she said yes.
2. He looked absolutely *flabbergasted* by the news.
3. The *flabbergasted* crowd went silent.

---

### flabby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflæbi/ &middot; *FLAB-ee*
**Русский:** дряблый, обрюзгший; вялый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *flabbier*, *flabbiest*
**Form:** -y from *flab*

Flabby has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the body, soft and loose, with too much fat.** (дряблый, обрюзгший)

1. He's gotten *flabby* since he stopped running.
2. My stomach is *flabbier* than it used to be.
3. She does push-ups to tone her *flabby* arms.

**2. Weak and not effective, with too much that is not needed.** (вялый, рыхлый)

4. The essay is *flabby* and needs serious cutting.
5. The movie's *flabby* middle hour really drags.

---

### flaccid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflæsɪd/ &middot; *FLAS-id*
**Русский:** вялый, дряблый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flaccid*, *most flaccid*
**Contrast:** Two pronunciations are heard in American English: FLAS-id /ˈflæsɪd/ and FLAK-sid /ˈflæksɪd/.

Soft, weak and hanging loosely; also, lacking force or energy.

1. The plants were *flaccid* from lack of water.
2. After a week of rest, his muscles felt *flaccid*.
3. Critics called the sequel even more *flaccid* than the original.

---

### flagging

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflæɡɪŋ/ &middot; *FLAG-ing*
**Русский:** ослабевающий, падающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from the verb *flag* ('to become weak or tired')
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with *a flag* (флаг). After *be* it is the verb: *Our energy was flagging*.

Becoming weaker, less active or less successful.

1. The company is trying to revive its *flagging* sales.
2. A cup of coffee helped my *flagging* energy.
3. The ad campaign boosted the senator's *flagging* popularity.

---

### flagrant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfleɪɡrənt/ &middot; *FLAY-gruhnt*
**Русский:** вопиющий, явный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flagrant*, *most flagrant*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *fragrant* (ароматный) — one letter apart.

Of something bad, done openly and shockingly, without trying to hide it.

1. It was a *flagrant* violation of the rules.
2. The referee called a *flagrant* foul.
3. It's one of the most *flagrant* cases of fraud I've ever seen.

---

### flaky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfleɪki/ &middot; *FLAY-kee*
**Русский:** слоистый, шелушащийся; ненадёжный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *flakier*, *flakiest*
**Form:** -y from *flake*

Flaky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Breaking easily into small thin pieces.** (слоистый, рассыпчатый; шелушащийся)

1. The croissants were warm and *flaky*.
2. Winter makes my skin dry and *flaky*.

**2. Informal: not reliable — forgetting plans, or working only some of the time.** (ненадёжный)

3. She's the *flakiest* person I know — she always cancels at the last minute.
4. Don't count on him; he's too *flaky*.
5. The Wi-Fi in this hotel is really *flaky*.

---

### flamboyant

**Pronunciation:** /flæmˈbɔɪənt/ &middot; *flam-BOY-uhnt*
**Русский:** яркий, эффектный, броский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flamboyant*, *most flamboyant*

Very confident, colorful and eager to be noticed.

1. He's known for his *flamboyant* style.
2. The singer wore a *flamboyant* gold suit.
3. Her brother is even more *flamboyant* than she is.

---

### flame-retardant

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfleɪm rɪˈtɑːrdənt/ &middot; *flaym-ri-TAR-duhnt*
**Русский:** огнезащитный, замедляющий горение
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective

Made of or treated with a substance that makes it burn slowly.

1. Children's pajamas must be made of *flame-retardant* fabric.
2. Some *flame-retardant* chemicals have been linked to health problems.
3. The seats on the plane are *flame-retardant*.

---

### flaming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfleɪmɪŋ/ &middot; *FLAY-ming*
**Русский:** пылающий; огненно-яркий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *flame*

Flaming has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Burning with flames.** (пылающий, горящий)

1. The chef poured brandy over the *flaming* pan.
2. Rioters threw *flaming* bottles at the police.

**2. Very bright red or orange, like fire.** (огненный, ярко-рыжий)

3. She has *flaming* red hair.
4. We watched the *flaming* orange sunset from the pier.

---

### flammable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflæməbəl/ &middot; *FLAM-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** легковоспламеняющийся, горючий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flammable*, *most flammable*
**Form:** -able from *flame*
**Opposite:** *nonflammable*
**Contrast:** *Flammable* and *inflammable* mean the same thing — easy to burn; the *in-* is not 'not'. Americans use *flammable* on warning labels.

Burning easily.

1. Gasoline is highly *flammable*.
2. Keep *flammable* liquids away from the stove.
3. Some fabrics are more *flammable* than others.

---

### flashy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflæʃi/ &middot; *FLASH-ee*
**Русский:** кричащий, броский, показной
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *flashier*, *flashiest*
**Form:** -y from *flash*

Bright, expensive-looking and designed to impress people.

1. He drives a *flashy* red sports car.
2. Her outfit was a bit too *flashy* for the office.
3. The new phone is *flashier*, but the old one works better.

---

### flat-footed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌflæt ˈfʊtɪd/ &middot; *flat-FUUT-id*
**Русский:** застигнутый врасплох; плоскостопый; неуклюжий
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both — the 'unprepared' sense mostly in *catch someone flat-footed*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flat-footed*, *most flat-footed*
**Form:** compound — adjective + *footed*

Flat-footed has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not ready for something that happens suddenly.** (застигнутый врасплох)

1. The quick pass caught the defense *flat-footed*.
2. Officials were *flat-footed* when the storm hit.

**2. Having feet with a very low arch.** (плоскостопый)

3. He was *flat-footed* as a child and wore special shoes.
4. *Flat-footed* runners may need arch supports.

**3. Clumsy and without skill or imagination.** (неуклюжий, топорный)

5. The dialogue in the movie is clumsy and *flat-footed*.
6. It was the most *flat-footed* speech of the whole campaign.

---

### flattened

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflætənd/ &middot; *FLAT-uhnd*
**Русский:** сплющенный, расплющенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *flatten*

Pressed or made flat.

1. We found a *flattened* soda can in the street.
2. The storm left the cornfields *flattened*.
3. The kids slid down the hill on a *flattened* cardboard box.

---

### flattering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflætərɪŋ/ &middot; *FLAT-ur-ing*
**Русский:** лестный; выгодный, идущий (об одежде)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flattering*, *most flattering*
**Form:** -ing from *flatter* — what causes the feeling
**Opposite:** *unflattering*
**Contrast:** *Flattering* describes the words or the clothes; *flattered* is how you feel: *I was flattered by her comments*.

Flattering has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Praising someone, often more than is true.** (лестный)

1. The article about her was very *flattering*.
2. It's *flattering* to be asked to give the speech.

**2. Making someone look more attractive.** (выгодный, идущий)

3. That color is really *flattering* on you.
4. This haircut is much more *flattering* than your old one.

---

### flavorful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfleɪvərfəl/ &middot; *FLAY-vur-fuhl*
**Русский:** ароматный, вкусный, насыщенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flavorful*, *most flavorful*
**Form:** -ful from *flavor*
**Opposite:** *flavorless*; *bland*

Having a strong, pleasant taste.

1. The soup was rich and *flavorful*.
2. Fresh herbs make the sauce more *flavorful*.
3. Heirloom tomatoes are the most *flavorful* ones at the market.

---

### flavorless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfleɪvərləs/ &middot; *FLAY-vur-luhs*
**Русский:** безвкусный, пресный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flavorless*, *most flavorless*
**Form:** -less from *flavor*
**Opposite:** *flavorful*

Having little or no taste.

1. The hospital food was bland and *flavorless*.
2. Supermarket tomatoes in winter are often *flavorless*.
3. The reheated rice was even more *flavorless* than before.

---

### flawed

**Pronunciation:** /flɔːd/ &middot; *FLAWD*
**Русский:** несовершенный, с изъяном, ошибочный
**Type:** evaluative; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flawed*, *most flawed*
**Form:** -ed from *flaw*
**Opposite:** *flawless*

Having a mistake or weakness that makes it less good or less correct.

1. The study was *flawed* from the start.
2. It's a *flawed* but interesting movie.
3. His second argument was even more *flawed* than the first.

---

### flawless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɔːləs/ &middot; *FLAW-luhs*
**Русский:** безупречный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — an absolute adjective: *absolutely flawless*, *almost flawless*
**Form:** -less from *flaw*
**Opposite:** *flawed*

Perfect, with no mistakes or faults.

1. Her performance was *flawless*.
2. He speaks *flawless* English.
3. The diamond is almost *flawless*.

---

### fledgling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɛdʒlɪŋ/ &middot; *FLEJ-ling*
**Русский:** начинающий, молодой, только что появившийся
**Type:** age; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — a young bird learning to fly (птенец). As an adjective it goes only before a noun: *a fledgling business*.

New and not yet experienced or fully developed.

1. The *fledgling* company has only five employees.
2. After years of war, it was a *fledgling* democracy.
3. She runs a program that helps *fledgling* writers find agents.

---

### fleeting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfliːtɪŋ/ &middot; *FLEE-ting*
**Русский:** мимолётный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fleeting*, *most fleeting*

Lasting only a very short time.

1. I caught a *fleeting* glimpse of him in the crowd.
2. Fame can be *fleeting*.
3. The good moods were getting more *fleeting* every week.

---

### Flemish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɛmɪʃ/ &middot; *FLEM-ish*
**Русский:** фламандский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *Flemish* is also the name of the Dutch spoken in northern Belgium.

From or connected with Flanders, the northern part of Belgium.

1. The museum has a whole room of *Flemish* paintings.
2. Antwerp is the largest *Flemish* city.
3. My neighbor's family is *Flemish*.

---

### flesh-eating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɛʃ ˌiːtɪŋ/ &middot; *FLESH-ee-ting*
**Русский:** плотоядный; пожирающий плоть
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle

Eating or destroying flesh.

1. Piranhas have a reputation as *flesh-eating* fish.
2. Doctors treated him for a rare *flesh-eating* infection.
3. The movie is about a town overrun by *flesh-eating* zombies.

---

### fleshy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɛʃi/ &middot; *FLESH-ee*
**Русский:** мясистый; полный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fleshier*, *fleshiest*
**Form:** -y from *flesh*

Fleshy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of plants and fruit, thick and soft.** (мясистый, сочный)

1. Aloe has thick, *fleshy* leaves.
2. Peaches and plums are soft, *fleshy* fruits.

**2. Of a person or body part, having a lot of flesh; slightly fat.** (мясистый, полный)

3. He had a round, *fleshy* face.
4. The nurse gave the shot in the *fleshiest* part of my arm.

---

### flexible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɛksəbəl/ &middot; *FLEK-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** гибкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flexible*, *most flexible*
**Takes:** *about* + noun — willing to change something
**Form:** -ible from *flex*
**Opposite:** *inflexible*; *rigid*

Flexible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to bend easily without breaking.** (гибкий, эластичный)

1. The tube is made of *flexible* plastic.
2. Yoga keeps your body *flexible*.

**2. Able or willing to change to suit new situations.** (гибкий, свободный)

3. My job has *flexible* hours.
4. We're *flexible* about the date of the party.
5. Try to be more *flexible* with your plans.

---

### flickering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɪkərɪŋ/ &middot; *FLIK-ur-ing*
**Русский:** мерцающий, мигающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *flicker*
**Contrast:** After *be* it is the verb: *The lights are flickering*.

Shining unsteadily, getting brighter and weaker again and again.

1. We talked by the *flickering* candlelight.
2. The *flickering* light in the hallway gave me a headache.
3. A *flickering* hope remained that they would find him alive.

---

### flightless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflaɪtləs/ &middot; *FLYT-luhs*
**Русский:** нелетающий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *flight*

Of birds and insects, not able to fly.

1. Penguins are *flightless* birds.
2. The ostrich is the largest *flightless* bird in the world.
3. Over time, some island beetles became *flightless*.

---

### flighty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflaɪti/ &middot; *FLY-tee*
**Русский:** ветреный, легкомысленный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *flightier*, *flightiest*; *more flighty*
**Form:** -y from *flight*

Changing plans or interests often; not serious or reliable.

1. Her parents thought she was too *flighty* to hold a steady job.
2. He's charming but *flighty*.
3. She was the *flightiest* of the three sisters.

---

### flimsy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɪmzi/ &middot; *FLIM-zee*
**Русский:** хлипкий, непрочный; неубедительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *flimsier*, *flimsiest*

Flimsy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Thin, light and easily broken or damaged.** (хлипкий, непрочный, тонкий)

1. The tent was too *flimsy* for the wind.
2. She was shivering in a *flimsy* cotton dress.
3. Paper plates are *flimsier* than plastic ones.

**2. Of an excuse or evidence, weak and hard to believe.** (неубедительный, слабый)

4. He gave a *flimsy* excuse for being late.
5. The evidence against her is *flimsy*.

---

### flippant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɪpənt/ &middot; *FLIP-uhnt*
**Русский:** легкомысленный, несерьёзный, развязный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flippant*, *most flippant*

Not serious enough about something that deserves respect.

1. Don't be *flippant* — this is serious.
2. He made a *flippant* remark about the accident.
3. Her tone was more *flippant* than she meant it to be.

---

### flirtatious

**Pronunciation:** /flɜːrˈteɪʃəs/ &middot; *flur-TAY-shuhs*
**Русский:** кокетливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flirtatious*, *most flirtatious*
**Form:** -atious from *flirt*

Behaving in a playful way that shows romantic interest.

1. She gave him a *flirtatious* smile.
2. He's *flirtatious* with everyone at the office.
3. Their texts got more *flirtatious* over time.

---

### floating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfloʊtɪŋ/ &middot; *FLOH-ting*
**Русский:** плавучий, плавающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *float*
**Contrast:** After *be* it is the verb: *A leaf was floating on the water*.

Floating has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Resting on water or moving in the air.** (плавучий, плавающий)

1. We had dinner at a *floating* restaurant on the river.
2. The lake was full of *floating* ice.

**2. Not fixed; able to change or move.** (плавающий, нефиксированный)

3. Our company gives everyone one *floating* holiday a year.
4. The country switched to a *floating* exchange rate.

---

### flooded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflʌdɪd/ &middot; *FLUHD-id*
**Русский:** затопленный; заваленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun — having too much of something
**Form:** -ed from *flood*

Flooded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with water.** (затопленный)

1. Drivers should stay off *flooded* roads.
2. Our basement was *flooded* after the storm.

**2. Full of more things or people than can be dealt with.** (заваленный, переполненный)

3. The market is *flooded* with cheap imports.
4. Her inbox was *flooded* with messages after the video went viral.

---

### floppy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɑːpi/ &middot; *FLAH-pee*
**Русский:** мягкий, обвисший, болтающийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *floppier*, *floppiest*
**Form:** -y from *flop*

Soft and hanging loosely.

1. The puppy has big *floppy* ears.
2. She wore a *floppy* straw hat to the beach.
3. The lettuce gets *floppier* if you leave it out of the fridge.

---

### floral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɔːrəl/ &middot; *FLOR-uhl*
**Русский:** цветочный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Made of flowers, decorated with flowers, or smelling like flowers.

1. She wore a *floral* print dress.
2. The perfume has a light *floral* scent.
3. This white wine is fruity and *floral*.

---

### Florentine

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɔːrəntiːn/ &middot; *FLOR-uhn-teen*
**Русский:** флорентийский; по-флорентийски (со шпинатом)
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — in cooking after the noun: *eggs Florentine*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. In dish names it comes after the noun.

Florentine has 2 distinct senses.

**1. From or connected with Florence, Italy.** (флорентийский)

1. We spent the day admiring *Florentine* architecture.
2. The painter was *Florentine* by birth.

**2. Of a dish, made with spinach.** (по-флорентийски, со шпинатом)

3. I ordered eggs *Florentine* for brunch.
4. Chicken *Florentine* is served in a creamy spinach sauce.

---

### florid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɔːrɪd/ &middot; *FLOR-id*
**Русский:** витиеватый, цветистый; багровый (о лице)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more florid*, *most florid*

Florid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too full of decoration or fancy words.** (витиеватый, цветистый)

1. The book is written in a *florid* style.
2. His speeches grew more *florid* with age.

**2. Of a face or skin, red.** (багровый, красный)

3. He had a *florid* face from years of drinking.
4. The old sea captain had a *florid* complexion.

---

### flourishing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflɜːrɪʃɪŋ/ &middot; *FLUR-i-shing*
**Русский:** процветающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flourishing*, *most flourishing*
**Form:** -ing from *flourish*
**Contrast:** After *be* it is usually the verb: *Business is flourishing*.

Growing well, successful and active.

1. The town has a *flourishing* arts scene.
2. They built a *flourishing* business from nothing.
3. Downtown seems more *flourishing* now than it was ten years ago.

---

### flowered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflaʊərd/ &middot; *FLOW-urd*
**Русский:** в цветочек, с цветочным узором
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *flower*

Decorated with a pattern of flowers.

1. She wore a *flowered* dress to the picnic.
2. The old couch had faded *flowered* cushions.
3. The wallpaper in the guest room is *flowered*.

---

### flowering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflaʊərɪŋ/ &middot; *FLOW-ur-ing*
**Русский:** цветущий, цветковый
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *flower*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *the flowering of jazz* (расцвет).

Of a plant, producing flowers.

1. We planted *flowering* shrubs along the fence.
2. Dogwoods are popular *flowering* trees in the South.
3. Bees help pollinate *flowering* plants.

---

### flowery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflaʊəri/ &middot; *FLOW-uh-ree*
**Русский:** цветочный; цветистый, витиеватый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flowery*, *most flowery*
**Form:** -y from *flower*

Flowery has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of flowers, or smelling or looking like flowers.** (цветочный)

1. The tea has a sweet, *flowery* aroma.
2. She wore a *flowery* summer dress.

**2. Of language, using too many fancy words.** (цветистый, витиеватый)

3. Skip the *flowery* language and get to the point.
4. The second draft was even more *flowery* than the first.

---

### flowing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfloʊɪŋ/ &middot; *FLOH-ing*
**Русский:** струящийся, ниспадающий; плавный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flowing*, *most flowing*
**Form:** -ing from *flow*

Flowing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of hair or clothes, long and hanging loosely.** (струящийся, ниспадающий)

1. She had long, *flowing* hair.
2. The bride wore a *flowing* white gown.

**2. Smooth, graceful and continuous.** (плавный)

3. He writes in a smooth, *flowing* style.
4. The dancer's movements were graceful and *flowing*.
5. Her handwriting became more *flowing* over the years.

---

### fluctuating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflʌktʃuˌeɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *FLUHK-choo-ay-ting*
**Русский:** колеблющийся, нестабильный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *fluctuate*
**Contrast:** After *be* it is the verb: *Prices have been fluctuating*.

Going up and down or changing often.

1. *Fluctuating* gas prices make it hard to plan a budget.
2. As a freelancer, she has a *fluctuating* income.
3. Patients with *fluctuating* blood pressure need close monitoring.

---

### fluent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfluːənt/ &middot; *FLOO-uhnt*
**Русский:** свободно владеющий (языком); беглый, плавный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fluent*, *most fluent*
**Takes:** *in* + language
**Contrast:** Say *fluent in English* or *speaks fluent English*. The adverb is *fluently*: *She speaks English fluently*.

Fluent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to speak a language easily and well.** (свободно владеющий, свободный)

1. She's *fluent* in Russian and English.
2. He speaks *fluent* Japanese.
3. After a year abroad, my sister is more *fluent* than I am.

**2. Smooth and skillful, without stopping or making mistakes.** (беглый, плавный)

4. By second grade, most kids are *fluent* readers.
5. He gave a *fluent*, confident presentation.

---

### fluffy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflʌfi/ &middot; *FLUHF-ee*
**Русский:** пушистый; воздушный; несерьёзный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fluffier*, *fluffiest*
**Form:** -y from *fluff*

Fluffy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Soft and light, covered with fur, hair or soft threads.** (пушистый, мягкий)

1. The kitten is so soft and *fluffy*.
2. We bought big *fluffy* towels for the guest bathroom.

**2. Of food, light and full of air.** (воздушный, пышный)

3. Beat the eggs until they're light and *fluffy*.
4. These are the *fluffiest* pancakes I've ever had.

**3. Informal: light and not serious or important.** (несерьёзный, лёгкий)

5. The article was a *fluffy* piece about celebrity pets.
6. It's a fun but *fluffy* movie.

---

### fluid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfluːɪd/ &middot; *FLOO-id*
**Русский:** изменчивый; плавный; жидкий, текучий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fluid*, *most fluid*
**Contrast:** Very often a noun: *Drink plenty of fluids* (жидкость).

Fluid has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of a situation or plan, not settled and likely to change.** (изменчивый, неустойчивый)

1. The situation is still *fluid*, and we'll update you as we learn more.
2. Our plans for the trip remain *fluid*.

**2. Smooth and graceful.** (плавный)

3. The dancer's movements were *fluid* and graceful.
4. His golf swing is more *fluid* than it was last season.

**3. Able to flow like a liquid.** (жидкий, текучий)

5. The lava stayed hot and *fluid* for hours.
6. Add a little water to keep the batter *fluid*.

---

### fluorescent

**Pronunciation:** /flʊˈrɛsənt/ &middot; *fluu-RES-uhnt*
**Русский:** люминесцентный, флуоресцентный; кислотный (о цвете)
**Type:** classifying; also color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Spelling trap: *fluor-*, not *flour-*.

Fluorescent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a light, produced by a glowing gas in a long tube.** (люминесцентный)

1. The office had harsh *fluorescent* lighting.
2. One of the *fluorescent* tubes in the garage keeps flickering.

**2. Of a color, extremely bright and seeming to glow.** (флуоресцентный, кислотный, неоновый)

3. Road workers wear *fluorescent* orange vests.
4. She painted her nails *fluorescent* pink.

---

### flush

**Pronunciation:** /flʌʃ/ &middot; *FLUHSH*
**Русский:** вровень; при деньгах
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun — level with a surface, or having plenty of money
**Contrast:** Usually a verb or noun: *flush the toilet* (смывать), *a flush of anger* (прилив).

Flush has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Exactly level with another surface.** (вровень, заподлицо)

1. Make sure the shelf is *flush* with the wall.
2. The new cabinets are *flush* against the ceiling.

**2. Informal: having a lot of money.** (при деньгах)

3. After selling the house, they were *flush* with cash.
4. Tech companies were *flush* that year and hiring fast.

---

### flushed

**Pronunciation:** /flʌʃt/ &middot; *FLUHSHT*
**Русский:** раскрасневшийся; опьянённый (успехом)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flushed*, *most flushed*
**Takes:** *with* + noun — excited by something
**Form:** -ed from *flush*

Flushed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the face, red, from heat, illness, embarrassment or effort.** (раскрасневшийся)

1. Her face was *flushed* from the heat.
2. You look *flushed* — do you have a fever?
3. His face grew more *flushed* as the argument went on.

**2. Very excited and proud because of something.** (опьянённый, воодушевлённый)

4. *Flushed* with victory, the team celebrated all night.
5. The candidate, *flushed* with success, thanked her supporters.

---

### flustered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflʌstərd/ &middot; *FLUH-sturd*
**Русский:** взволнованный, растерянный
**Type:** emotion; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more flustered*, *most flustered*
**Form:** -ed from *fluster*

Nervous and confused, often because of being rushed or embarrassed.

1. She got *flustered* when the teacher called on her.
2. A *flustered* waiter dropped our plates.
3. He was more *flustered* than angry.

---

### fly-by-night

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflaɪ baɪ ˌnaɪt/ &middot; *FLY-by-nyt*
**Русский:** ненадёжный, сомнительный (о фирме), однодневка
**Type:** evaluative; also compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — phrase used as an adjective

Of a business, dishonest or unreliable and likely to disappear quickly.

1. Don't hire some *fly-by-night* contractor.
2. It turned out to be a *fly-by-night* operation.
3. Many of those online colleges were *fly-by-night*.

---

### flying

**Pronunciation:** /ˈflaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *FLY-ing*
**Русский:** летающий, летящий; стремительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *fly*
**Contrast:** *Flying* is also a noun: *I hate flying* (полёты). *Pass with flying colors* means 'succeed easily'.

Flying has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to fly, or moving through the air.** (летающий, летящий)

1. Mosquitoes and other *flying* insects come out at dusk.
2. Several people were hurt by *flying* glass.

**2. Fast and energetic, especially at the start.** (стремительный, удачный)

3. The team got off to a *flying* start.
4. He took a *flying* leap into the pool.

---

### foamy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfoʊmi/ &middot; *FOH-mee*
**Русский:** пенистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *foamier*, *foamiest*
**Form:** -y from *foam*

Covered with or full of small bubbles.

1. I like my latte hot and *foamy*.
2. The waves left *foamy* lines on the sand.
3. This soap is *foamier* than the old one.

---

### focal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfoʊkəl/ &middot; *FOH-kuhl*
**Русский:** центральный; фокусный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *focus*

Focal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Central and most important.** (центральный, главный)

1. The fireplace is the *focal* point of the room.
2. Health care became the *focal* issue of the campaign.

**2. In optics, connected with the point where light rays meet.** (фокусный)

3. A lens with a longer *focal* length makes distant objects look closer.
4. Check the camera's *focal* range before you buy it.

---

### focused

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfoʊkəst/ &middot; *FOH-kuhst*
**Русский:** сосредоточенный; целенаправленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more focused*, *most focused*
**Takes:** *on* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ed from *focus*
**Opposite:** *unfocused*; *distracted*
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *s*: *focused*, *focusing*.

Focused has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Giving all your attention to one thing.** (сосредоточенный)

1. Stay *focused* — the test is almost over.
2. She's completely *focused* on her career right now.
3. I'm more *focused* in the morning than at night.

**2. Aimed clearly at a particular goal or area.** (целенаправленный, узконаправленный)

4. We need a short, *focused* meeting, not a two-hour discussion.
5. The company is *focused* on small businesses.

---

### foggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːɡi/ &middot; *FAH-gee*
**Русский:** туманный; смутный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *foggier*, *foggiest*
**Form:** -y from *fog*
**Contrast:** *I don't have the foggiest (idea)* means 'I have no idea at all'.

Foggy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With a lot of fog.** (туманный)

1. It was a cold, *foggy* morning.
2. San Francisco is *foggier* in summer than in fall.

**2. Unclear or confused.** (смутный, затуманенный)

3. My memory of that night is a little *foggy*.
4. I always feel *foggy* after taking cold medicine.
5. I don't have the *foggiest* idea where he went.

---

### foldable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfoʊldəbəl/ &middot; *FOHL-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** складной, раскладной
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *fold*
**Contrast:** For furniture the usual word is *folding*: *a folding chair*. *Foldable* is common for phones and gadgets, and after a verb: *The table is foldable*.

Able to be folded to make it smaller.

1. He just bought a *foldable* phone.
2. The table is *foldable*, so it fits in the closet.
3. A *foldable* bike fits easily in the trunk.

---

### folding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfoʊldɪŋ/ &middot; *FOHL-ding*
**Русский:** складной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *fold*

Made so that it can be folded flat or into a smaller shape.

1. Grab a few *folding* chairs from the garage.
2. We set up a *folding* table in the backyard.
3. He always carries a small *folding* knife.

---

### folksy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfoʊksi/ &middot; *FOHK-see*
**Русский:** простецкий, по-домашнему простой
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *folksier*, *folksiest*
**Form:** -y from *folks*

Friendly, simple and informal, in the style of ordinary country people.

1. The governor is known for his *folksy* charm.
2. The café has a warm, *folksy* feel.
3. His speeches got *folksier* as the election got closer.

---

### following

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːloʊɪŋ/ &middot; *FAH-loh-ing*
**Русский:** следующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *follow*
**Contrast:** *Next week* counts from now; *the following week* counts from another time: *We arrived on Monday and left the following week*. *Following* is also a preposition meaning 'after': *following the meeting*.

Following has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Coming next in time, after the one just mentioned.** (следующий)

1. We met again the *following* week.
2. She moved to Denver the *following* year.

**2. About to be listed or shown.** (следующий, нижеприведённый)

3. Please answer the *following* questions.
4. You'll need the *following* documents to apply.

---

### foolhardy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfuːlˌhɑːrdi/ &middot; *FOOL-har-dee*
**Русский:** безрассудный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more foolhardy*, *most foolhardy*
**Form:** compound — *fool* + *hardy* ('bold')

Taking stupid and unnecessary risks.

1. It would be *foolhardy* to hike alone in this weather.
2. He made a *foolhardy* attempt to swim across the river.
3. Nothing was more *foolhardy* than driving through the flood.

---

### foolproof

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfuːlpruːf/ &middot; *FOOL-proof*
**Русский:** надёжный, защищённый от ошибок
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *fool* + *-proof*, as in *waterproof*

So simple or well designed that it cannot go wrong or be used wrongly.

1. There's no *foolproof* way to pick the right stock.
2. The instructions are so clear they're practically *foolproof*.
3. She has a *foolproof* recipe for chocolate chip cookies.

---

### footloose

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʊtluːs/ &middot; *FUUT-loos*
**Русский:** свободный, ничем не связанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — most often after a verb or in *footloose and fancy-free*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more footloose*, *most footloose*
**Form:** compound — *foot* + *loose*

Free to go where you like and do what you want, with no responsibilities.

1. After college he spent a *footloose* year traveling through Asia.
2. She's single and *footloose* and can move anywhere for work.
3. They were young, *footloose* and fancy-free.

---

### forbidden

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈbɪdən/ &middot; *fur-BID-uhn*
**Русский:** запрещённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -en participle of *forbid*
**Opposite:** *allowed*, *permitted*
**Contrast:** Not the same as *forbidding*, which means unfriendly or threatening-looking: *a forbidden room* is one you may not enter; *a forbidding room* looks cold and unwelcoming.

Not allowed by a rule, a law or custom.

1. Smoking is *forbidden* anywhere on campus.
2. As kids we were fascinated by the *forbidden* room in the basement.
3. Money was a *forbidden* topic at our dinner table.

---

### forbidding

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈbɪdɪŋ/ &middot; *fur-BID-ing*
**Русский:** мрачный, неприветливый, угрожающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forbidding*, *most forbidding*
**Form:** -ing from *forbid*
**Opposite:** *inviting*
**Contrast:** Not the same as *forbidden* (not allowed): *a forbidding place* looks unfriendly or dangerous; *a forbidden place* is one you may not enter.

Looking unfriendly, threatening or difficult, so that you do not want to go near.

1. The old prison was a gray, *forbidding* building.
2. The mountains looked even more *forbidding* in the fog.
3. His boss had a *forbidding* expression that kept people from asking questions.

---

### forced

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːrst/ &middot; *FORST*
**Русский:** вынужденный, принудительный; натянутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — the 'unnatural' sense takes *more/most*: *more forced*
**Form:** -ed from *force*

Forced has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Done because you have to or are made to, not because you choose.** (вынужденный, принудительный)

1. The plane made a *forced* landing in a cornfield.
2. Millions of people were sent to *forced* labor camps.
3. The company announced a round of *forced* retirements.

**2. Not sincere or natural; done with obvious effort.** (натянутый, неестественный)

4. She gave a *forced* smile and said she was fine.
5. The jokes in the script felt *forced*.
6. His cheerfulness seemed even more *forced* after the news.

---

### forceful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrsfəl/ &middot; *FORS-fuhl*
**Русский:** сильный, напористый, убедительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forceful*, *most forceful*
**Form:** -ful from *force*
**Contrast:** *forceful* is about a strong, confident manner: *a forceful argument*. *forcible* is about physical force: *forcible entry*.

Strong and confident in expressing opinions or taking action.

1. She's a *forceful* speaker who holds the room.
2. The mayor made a *forceful* case for the new tax.
3. He needs to be more *forceful* in meetings.

---

### forcible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrsəbəl/ &middot; *FOR-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** насильственный, принудительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *forcible entry*, *forcible removal*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ible from *force*
**Contrast:** *forcible* means using physical force; *forceful* means strong and confident: *a forceful speaker*.

Done by using physical force.

1. Police found no signs of *forcible* entry.
2. The court ruled against the *forcible* removal of the protesters.
3. They opposed the *forcible* relocation of families from the area.

---

### foreboding

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːrˈboʊdɪŋ/ &middot; *for-BOH-ding*
**Русский:** зловещий, предвещающий беду
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more foreboding*, *most foreboding*
**Form:** -ing from *forebode* (*fore-* + *bode*)
**Contrast:** More often a noun: *a sense of foreboding*, *full of foreboding*.

Making you feel that something bad is going to happen.

1. *Foreboding* clouds gathered over the lake.
2. The music grew dark and *foreboding*.
3. The empty house looked even more *foreboding* at night.

---

### foregoing

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːrˈɡoʊɪŋ/ &middot; *for-GOH-ing*
**Русский:** вышеупомянутый, предшествующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — or as a noun: *the foregoing*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *fore-* 'before' + *going*
**Contrast:** Formal writing only. Do not confuse with *forgoing*, from *forgo* (to give something up).

Just mentioned, or coming before this in a text; used in formal writing.

1. The *foregoing* discussion shows the limits of the study.
2. In light of the *foregoing* facts, the motion is denied.
3. All of the *foregoing* examples come from court records.

---

### foregone

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrɡɔːn/ &middot; *FOR-gawn*
**Русский:** предрешённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — almost always in *a foregone conclusion*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *fore-* 'before' + *gone*

Certain even before it happens, so there is no real doubt about it.

1. The outcome of the election was a *foregone* conclusion.
2. Don't treat a win as a *foregone* conclusion — we still have to play.
3. Most fans saw the trade as a *foregone* conclusion.

---

### foreign

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrən/ &middot; *FOR-uhn*
**Русский:** иностранный, зарубежный; внешний; чуждый
**Type:** origin; also classifying
**Position:** both — the 'other countries' and 'foreign object' senses only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — the 'unfamiliar' sense takes *more/most*: *more foreign*
**Takes:** *to* + person, in the 'unfamiliar' sense
**Opposite:** *domestic*
**Contrast:** Use *foreign* for things: *foreign cars*, *foreign languages*. For people, *foreigner* can sound unfriendly; Americans often say *international students* or *visitors from abroad* instead.

Foreign has 4 distinct senses.

**1. From, in or typical of a country that is not your own.** (иностранный, зарубежный)

1. She speaks three *foreign* languages.
2. Most of the cars in the lot were *foreign*.
3. The city attracts millions of *foreign* tourists every year.

**2. Dealing with or involving other countries.** (внешний (о политике))

4. The president's *foreign* policy was unpopular.
5. She reports on *foreign* affairs for a Chicago paper.

**3. Strange and unfamiliar to someone.** (чуждый, незнакомый)

6. The idea of saving money was completely *foreign* to him.
7. Life on a farm felt *foreign* at first.

**4. Coming from outside and not belonging where it is found.** (инородный)

8. The X-ray showed a *foreign* object in his stomach.
9. The doctor removed a *foreign* body from the child's ear.

---

### foreign-born

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɔːrənˈbɔːrn/ &middot; *for-uhn-BORN*
**Русский:** родившийся за границей
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *foreign* + *born*
**Opposite:** *native-born*

Born in another country and now living in this one.

1. About a third of the city's residents are *foreign-born*.
2. The study tracked *foreign-born* workers in the tech industry.
3. Her *foreign-born* parents never lost their accents.

---

### foremost

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrmoʊst/ &middot; *FOR-mohst*
**Русский:** ведущий, выдающийся; главный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — it already means 'most important'
**Form:** *fore-* + *-most*, a superlative in itself
**Contrast:** *first and foremost* is an adverb phrase meaning 'most importantly': *First and foremost, we need a plan.*

Most important, or best known in a particular field.

1. She is one of the country's *foremost* experts on climate.
2. Safety should be *foremost* in everyone's mind.
3. The museum holds the region's *foremost* collection of Native American art.

---

### forensic

**Pronunciation:** /fəˈrɛnsɪk/ &middot; *fuh-REN-sik*
**Русский:** судебный, криминалистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Using scientific methods to investigate crimes or to provide evidence in court.

1. The *forensic* team collected fingerprints from the door.
2. *Forensic* evidence linked him to the scene.
3. She works as a *forensic* accountant for the FBI.

---

### foreseeable

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːrˈsiːəbəl/ &middot; *for-SEE-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** предсказуемый; обозримый (о будущем)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *foresee*
**Opposite:** *unforeseeable*

Foreseeable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Possible to know about or expect in advance.** (предсказуемый, предвидимый)

1. The accident was entirely *foreseeable*.
2. The bridge collapse was a *foreseeable* result of years of neglect.

**2. In *the foreseeable future*: for as far ahead as anyone can reasonably plan.** (обозримый)

3. We don't plan to hire anyone for the *foreseeable* future.
4. Prices will stay high in the *foreseeable* future.

---

### forested

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrɪstɪd/ &middot; *FOR-uh-stid*
**Русский:** лесистый, покрытый лесом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — use *heavily* or *densely forested*
**Form:** -ed from *forest*

Covered with trees.

1. The trail winds through *forested* hills.
2. Much of northern Maine is heavily *forested*.
3. They bought ten acres of *forested* land.

---

### forged

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːrdʒd/ &middot; *FORJD*
**Русский:** поддельный; кованый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *forge*

Forged has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Copied illegally so that it looks real.** (поддельный, фальшивый)

1. He was arrested for using a *forged* passport.
2. The signature on the check was *forged*.
3. Police found boxes of *forged* documents.

**2. Shaped by heating metal and hammering it.** (кованый)

4. The gate is made of *forged* iron.
5. These knives are *forged*, not stamped.

---

### forgetful

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈɡɛtfəl/ &middot; *fur-GET-fuhl*
**Русский:** забывчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forgetful*, *most forgetful*
**Form:** -ful from *forget*

Often forgetting things.

1. My grandmother is getting more *forgetful*.
2. I'm so *forgetful* — I left my keys in the door again.
3. Stress and lack of sleep can make you *forgetful*.

---

### forgettable

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈɡɛtəbəl/ &middot; *fur-GET-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** непримечательный, незапоминающийся
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forgettable*, *most forgettable*
**Form:** -able from *forget*, with the *t* doubled
**Opposite:** *unforgettable*

Not interesting or special enough to remember.

1. The movie was entertaining but *forgettable*.
2. He had a *forgettable* season after his injury.
3. It was one of the most *forgettable* meals I've ever eaten.

---

### forgivable

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈɡɪvəbəl/ &middot; *fur-GIV-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** простительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forgivable*, *most forgivable*
**Form:** -able from *forgive*
**Opposite:** *unforgivable*

Not so bad that it cannot be forgiven.

1. Being late once is *forgivable*; being late every day isn't.
2. It was an easy, *forgivable* mistake.
3. Lying to protect a friend seemed more *forgivable* to her.

---

### forgiving

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈɡɪvɪŋ/ &middot; *fur-GIV-ing*
**Русский:** снисходительный, незлопамятный; щадящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forgiving*, *most forgiving*
**Form:** -ing from *forgive*
**Opposite:** *unforgiving*

Forgiving has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Willing to forgive people for what they have done wrong.** (снисходительный, незлопамятный)

1. My mom was always more *forgiving* than my dad.
2. Voters are not always *forgiving* of scandals.

**2. Allowing for mistakes or rough use without causing problems.** (щадящий, прощающий ошибки)

3. This racket is very *forgiving* for beginners.
4. Stretchy fabric is *forgiving* if you gain a few pounds.

---

### forgotten

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈɡɑːtən/ &middot; *fur-GAH-tuhn*
**Русский:** забытый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -en participle of *forget*

No longer remembered, noticed or cared about.

1. We found a *forgotten* box of letters in the attic.
2. The small towns along the old highway feel *forgotten*.
3. By the 1980s the singer was largely *forgotten*.

---

### forked

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːrkt/ &middot; *FORKT*
**Русский:** раздвоенный, разветвлённый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *fork*

Divided into two or more parts at one end.

1. Snakes have *forked* tongues.
2. A *forked* branch makes a good slingshot.
3. *Forked* lightning lit up the sky.

---

### forlorn

**Pronunciation:** /fərˈlɔːrn/ &middot; *fur-LORN*
**Русский:** покинутый, унылый; тщетный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — the 'hopeless' sense only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forlorn*, *most forlorn*

Forlorn has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sad and lonely, or looking empty and abandoned.** (покинутый, унылый)

1. A *forlorn* puppy sat alone in the rain.
2. The empty mall looked *forlorn*.
3. She looked more *forlorn* than ever.

**2. Very unlikely to succeed.** (тщетный, безнадёжный)

4. They made a *forlorn* attempt to save the factory.
5. Peace now seems a *forlorn* hope.

---

### formal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrməl/ &middot; *FOR-muhl*
**Русский:** официальный; строгий (о стиле)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the 'official' and 'education' senses usually before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more formal*, *most formal*
**Opposite:** *informal*
**Contrast:** False friend. Russian *формальный подход* (done only for show) is *perfunctory* or *box-ticking* in English, not *formal*. English *formal* means serious and official in style.

Formal has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Serious and correct in style, following the rules for official occasions, clothes or language.** (официальный, строгий, торжественный)

1. The wedding invitation says *formal* attire.
2. Use *formal* language in a cover letter.
3. He's more *formal* than his brother and always calls me Ms. Chen.

**2. Official and done according to the proper rules.** (официальный)

4. She filed a *formal* complaint with HR.
5. We haven't received a *formal* offer yet.
6. There was no *formal* agreement between them.

**3. Received through schools, colleges and training programs.** (систематическое, полученное в учебном заведении (об образовании))

7. He had little *formal* education but read constantly.
8. She has no *formal* training in design.

---

### formalized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrməlaɪzd/ &middot; *FOR-muh-lyzd*
**Русский:** формализованный, официально оформленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more formalized*, *most formalized*
**Form:** -ed from *formalize* (*formal* + *-ize*)

Made official, or given a fixed structure and rules.

1. The company has no *formalized* process for hiring.
2. The rules became more *formalized* as the club grew.
3. Most schools now have *formalized* anti-bullying policies.

---

### formative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrmətɪv/ &middot; *FOR-muh-tiv*
**Русский:** формирующий, определяющий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more formative*, *most formative*
**Form:** -ive from *form*

Having a strong influence on how a person or thing develops.

1. She spent her *formative* years in Detroit.
2. Losing his father was the most *formative* experience of his life.
3. Teaching abroad was *formative* for me.

---

### formidable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrmɪdəbəl/ &middot; *FOR-mih-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** грозный, внушительный; труднейший
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more formidable*, *most formidable*

Formidable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Impressive and powerful, and a little frightening.** (грозный, внушительный)

1. She was a *formidable* opponent in debate.
2. The team looks even more *formidable* this year.
3. He has a *formidable* memory for names.

**2. Very difficult to deal with.** (труднейший, серьёзный)

4. Rebuilding the town is a *formidable* task.
5. The mountain presents *formidable* challenges for climbers.

---

### formulaic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɔːrmjəˈleɪɪk/ &middot; *for-myuh-LAY-ik*
**Русский:** шаблонный, трафаретный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more formulaic*, *most formulaic*
**Form:** -ic from *formula*

Following a fixed pattern and lacking originality.

1. Most romantic comedies are pretty *formulaic*.
2. The speech was full of *formulaic* phrases.
3. Her later novels became more *formulaic*.

---

### forthcoming

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfɔːrθˈkʌmɪŋ/ &middot; *forth-KUHM-ing*
**Русский:** предстоящий; откровенный; предоставляемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the 'coming soon' sense before a noun, the other two after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable — the 'willing to talk' sense takes *more/most*: *more forthcoming*
**Form:** *forth* + *coming*
**Contrast:** The meaning changes with position: *a forthcoming report* will appear soon; *he wasn't forthcoming* means he didn't say much; *no help was forthcoming* means none was given.

Forthcoming has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Going to happen or appear soon.** (предстоящий, готовящийся к выходу)

1. Her *forthcoming* book is about the Civil War.
2. Details will be announced in a *forthcoming* report.

**2. Willing to give information or talk openly.** (откровенный, открытый)

3. He wasn't very *forthcoming* about his past.
4. The company has been more *forthcoming* since the lawsuit.

**3. Given or provided when needed; usually in negative sentences.** (предоставляемый, поступающий)

5. No explanation was *forthcoming*.
6. If funding isn't *forthcoming*, the project will stop.

---

### forthright

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrθraɪt/ &middot; *FORTH-ryt*
**Русский:** прямой, откровенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forthright*, *most forthright*

Saying honestly and directly what you think.

1. She's *forthright* about her political views.
2. He gave a *forthright* answer to a tough question.
3. I wish my manager were more *forthright* with us.

---

### fortified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrtɪfaɪd/ &middot; *FOR-tuh-fyd*
**Русский:** укреплённый; обогащённый; креплёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun, in the 'with something added' sense
**Form:** -ed from *fortify*

Fortified has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Protected by walls or other defenses against attack.** (укреплённый)

1. The soldiers held a *fortified* position on the hill.
2. The old city is *fortified* with thick stone walls.

**2. Having something added to make it healthier or stronger.** (обогащённый; креплёный (о вине))

3. Most breakfast cereals are *fortified* with iron.
4. Port is a *fortified* wine.
5. Buy milk that is *fortified* with vitamin D.

---

### fortuitous

**Pronunciation:** /fɔːrˈtuːɪtəs/ &middot; *for-TOO-ih-tuhs*
**Русский:** случайный; удачный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fortuitous*, *most fortuitous*
**Contrast:** Strictly it means 'by chance', but most Americans now use it to mean 'lucky' as well, and the two meanings usually overlap.

Happening by chance, especially by a lucky chance.

1. Our meeting at the airport was completely *fortuitous*.
2. A *fortuitous* phone call led to her first job.
3. The timing could not have been more *fortuitous*.

---

### fortunate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrtʃənət/ &middot; *FOR-chuh-nuht*
**Русский:** удачливый, везучий; удачный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fortunate*, *most fortunate*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive; *that*-clause; *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ate from *fortune*
**Opposite:** *unfortunate*

Lucky, or having good things that other people do not have.

1. We were *fortunate* to find an apartment so quickly.
2. It's *fortunate* that nobody was hurt.
3. We should help people who are less *fortunate* than we are.

---

### forward

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrwərd/ &middot; *FOR-wurd*
**Русский:** направленный вперёд; передний; развязный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — the direction and front senses before a noun, the 'too bold' sense after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable — the 'too bold' sense takes *more/most*: *more forward*
**Opposite:** *backward*
**Contrast:** Usually an adverb: *step forward*, *look forward to*. The adjective is the one before a noun (*forward motion*) or the 'too bold' sense (*a bit forward*).

Forward has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Moving or directed toward the front or ahead.** (направленный вперёд, поступательный)

1. The team made little *forward* progress in the second half.
2. Lean your body into a slightly *forward* position.
3. A *forward* pass is legal in football but not in rugby.

**2. At or near the front of a ship, plane or vehicle.** (передний, носовой)

4. Passengers in the *forward* cabin get off first.
5. The *forward* seats have more legroom.

**3. Too confident or friendly with someone you do not know well.** (развязный, нахальный)

6. It was a little *forward* of him to ask for her number so soon.
7. I hope I'm not being too *forward*.
8. She was more *forward* than the other girls in class.

---

### forward-looking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrwərd ˌlʊkɪŋ/ &middot; *FOR-wurd-luu-king*
**Русский:** дальновидный, прогрессивный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more forward-looking*, *most forward-looking*
**Form:** compound — adverb + participle

Planning for the future and open to new ideas.

1. The city has a *forward-looking* plan for public transit.
2. She's one of the most *forward-looking* leaders in the industry.
3. The report contains *forward-looking* statements about profits.

---

### fossilized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːsəlaɪzd/ &middot; *FAH-suh-lyzd*
**Русский:** окаменелый; закостенелый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — the 'unable to change' sense takes *more/most*: *more fossilized*
**Form:** -ed from *fossilize*

Fossilized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Turned into a fossil.** (окаменелый, окаменевший)

1. They found *fossilized* dinosaur bones in Montana.
2. The rock contains *fossilized* shells.

**2. Fixed and unable to change or develop.** (закостенелый)

3. The party is run by a *fossilized* leadership.
4. His ideas about women seem *fossilized*.
5. Teachers call mistakes that never go away *fossilized* errors.

---

### foster

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːstər/ &middot; *FAH-stur*
**Русский:** приёмный (временно)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *foster* parents care for a child for a time; *adoptive* parents become the child's legal parents for good. Russian *приёмный* covers both. *to foster* is also a verb: *foster a child*, *foster cooperation*.

Caring for a child in a family for a period of time without becoming the legal parents, or being cared for in this way.

1. They've been *foster* parents to more than twenty kids.
2. She grew up in a series of *foster* homes.
3. The shelter is looking for *foster* families for puppies.

---

### foul

**Pronunciation:** /faʊl/ &middot; *FOWL*
**Русский:** отвратительный; скверный; грубый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fouler*, *foulest*
**Contrast:** Also a noun and a verb in sports: *commit a foul*, *he fouled out*. Sounds the same as *fowl* (birds such as chickens).

Foul has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Very unpleasant to smell or taste.** (зловонный, мерзкий)

1. A *foul* smell was coming from the fridge.
2. The water tasted *foul*.
3. It was the *foulest* coffee I've ever had.

**2. Very bad or unpleasant, of weather, a mood or a temper.** (скверный, ужасный)

4. He's been in a *foul* mood all day.
5. *Foul* weather delayed the launch.

**3. Rude and offensive, full of swearing.** (грубый, непристойный)

6. The coach was fined for *foul* language.
7. She has a *foul* mouth when she's angry.

**4. Against the rules or the law; in baseball, hit outside the lines.** (нечестный, противоправный; вне поля (в бейсболе))

8. The umpire called it a *foul* ball.
9. Police suspect *foul* play in his death.

---

### foul-mouthed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfaʊlˌmaʊðd/ &middot; *FOWL-mowthd*
**Русский:** сквернословящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more foul-mouthed*, *most foul-mouthed*
**Form:** compound — *foul* + *mouth* + *-ed*

Using a lot of rude, offensive words.

1. The comedian is famous for his *foul-mouthed* rants.
2. My uncle gets *foul-mouthed* when he watches football.
3. She was the most *foul-mouthed* kid in the neighborhood.

---

### foul-smelling

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfaʊlˈsmɛlɪŋ/ &middot; *fowl-SMEL-ing*
**Русский:** зловонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more foul-smelling*, *most foul-smelling*
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle

Having a very unpleasant smell.

1. A *foul-smelling* liquid leaked from the truck.
2. The river became *foul-smelling* in the summer heat.
3. The swamp was the most *foul-smelling* place on the trip.

---

### four-wheel

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrˌwiːl/ &middot; *FOR-weel*
**Русский:** полноприводный; четырёхколёсный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — number + noun
**Contrast:** *four-wheel drive* (4WD) is the system; a car that has it is *a four-wheel-drive vehicle*, or informally *a four-by-four*.

Having four wheels, or with the engine's power sent to all four wheels.

1. You'll need *four-wheel* drive on those mountain roads.
2. Kids rode *four-wheel* ATVs across the field.
3. *Four-wheel* vehicles aren't allowed on the beach.

---

### fourfold

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɔːrfoʊld/ &middot; *FOR-fohld*
**Русский:** четырёхкратный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *four* + *-fold*, like *twofold*, *tenfold*
**Contrast:** Also an adverb: *Prices rose fourfold.*

Four times as big or as many; or having four parts.

1. There has been a *fourfold* increase in cases since May.
2. The company saw a *fourfold* rise in profits.
3. The program has a *fourfold* purpose.

---

### foxy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɑːksi/ &middot; *FAHK-see*
**Русский:** сексуальная; хитрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *foxier*, *foxiest*
**Form:** -y from *fox*

Foxy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Informal and a little dated: sexually attractive, usually of a woman.** (сексуальная, эффектная)

1. He thinks his new neighbor is pretty *foxy*.
2. She looked *foxy* in that leather jacket.

**2. Clever and good at tricking people.** (хитрый)

3. He's the *foxiest* negotiator I know.
4. The *foxy* old senator knew exactly how to get votes.

---

### fractional

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrækʃənəl/ &middot; *FRAK-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** незначительный; дробный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *fraction*

Fractional has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very small.** (незначительный, ничтожный)

1. Stocks rose by a *fractional* amount.
2. The new model is only a *fractional* improvement.

**2. Relating to fractions, or to parts of a whole.** (дробный, долевой)

3. *Fractional* numbers confuse a lot of kids.
4. They bought *fractional* ownership of a vacation home.

---

### fractious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrækʃəs/ &middot; *FRAK-shuhs*
**Русский:** раздражительный, капризный; склочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fractious*, *most fractious*

Easily annoyed and hard to control, or always arguing.

1. The *fractious* toddler refused to take a nap.
2. The party is deeply divided and *fractious*.
3. Negotiations grew more *fractious* as the deadline neared.

---

### fragile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrædʒəl/ &middot; *FRAJ-uhl*
**Русский:** хрупкий; неустойчивый; ранимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fragile*, *most fragile*
**Contrast:** American *fragile* rhymes with *agile*: FRAJ-uhl, not the British FRAJ-yl.

Fragile has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Easily broken or damaged.** (хрупкий, ломкий)

1. Be careful — those glasses are *fragile*.
2. Old books have *fragile* pages.

**2. Weak and easily made worse or destroyed, of peace, health or an economy.** (неустойчивый, шаткий)

3. The ceasefire remains *fragile*.
4. The economy is more *fragile* than it looks.

**3. Emotionally weak and easily upset.** (ранимый, уязвимый)

5. She's still feeling *fragile* after the breakup.
6. He has a *fragile* ego.

---

### fragmentary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfræɡmənˌtɛri/ &middot; *FRAG-muhn-tair-ee*
**Русский:** отрывочный, фрагментарный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fragmentary*, *most fragmentary*
**Form:** -ary from *fragment*

Made of small separate parts and incomplete.

1. We only have *fragmentary* evidence of what happened.
2. Early reports from the scene were *fragmentary*.
3. The historical record is even more *fragmentary* for that period.

---

### fragmented

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfræɡmɛntɪd/ &middot; *FRAG-men-tid*
**Русский:** раздробленный, разрозненный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fragmented*, *most fragmented*
**Form:** -ed from *fragment*

Broken into separate parts that do not fit or work well together.

1. The health care system is badly *fragmented*.
2. Social media has created a more *fragmented* audience.
3. I only have *fragmented* memories of that night.

---

### fragrant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfreɪɡrənt/ &middot; *FRAY-gruhnt*
**Русский:** ароматный, душистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fragrant*, *most fragrant*

Having a pleasant smell.

1. The garden was full of *fragrant* roses.
2. The kitchen smelled warm and *fragrant* with cinnamon.
3. Jasmine is more *fragrant* at night.

---

### frail

**Pronunciation:** /freɪl/ &middot; *FRAYL*
**Русский:** хрупкий, немощный; непрочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *frailer*, *frailest*

Frail has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Physically weak and thin, usually because of old age or illness.** (хрупкий, немощный)

1. My grandfather has grown *frail* since his surgery.
2. A *frail* old woman answered the door.
3. She looked *frailer* than I remembered.

**2. Easily damaged or broken; not strong.** (непрочный, хлипкий)

4. The *frail* wooden bridge swayed in the wind.
5. The country's *frail* economy could not survive another shock.

---

### framed

**Pronunciation:** /freɪmd/ &middot; *FRAYMD*
**Русский:** в рамке
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *frame*
**Contrast:** *to be framed* can also mean to be made to look guilty of a crime — that is the verb *frame*, not this adjective.

Put in a frame.

1. A *framed* photo of her parents hung by the door.
2. Is the print *framed*, or is it just a poster?
3. He keeps his diploma *framed* on the office wall.

---

### frank

**Pronunciation:** /fræŋk/ &middot; *FRANGK*
**Русский:** откровенный, прямой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *franker*, *frankest*; *more frank*, *most frank*

Honest and direct, even when the truth is unpleasant.

1. Let me be *frank* — your work hasn't been good enough.
2. We had a *frank* discussion about money.
3. The doctor was *franker* with me than I expected.

---

### frantic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfræntɪk/ &middot; *FRAN-tik*
**Русский:** обезумевший; лихорадочный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frantic*, *most frantic*

Frantic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So worried or afraid that you cannot control your feelings.** (обезумевший, вне себя)

1. Her parents were *frantic* when she didn't come home.
2. He made a *frantic* call to 911.

**2. Done very fast and in a hurried, disorganized way.** (лихорадочный, безумный)

3. There was a *frantic* search for the missing ring.
4. Things get more *frantic* at the restaurant around eight.

---

### fraternal

**Pronunciation:** /frəˈtɜːrnəl/ &middot; *fruh-TUR-nuhl*
**Русский:** братский; разнояйцевый (о близнецах)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — usually
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from Latin *frater* 'brother'

Fraternal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of or like brothers; friendly and supportive, or relating to a club of members who help each other.** (братский)

1. The two leaders spoke of *fraternal* ties between their countries.
2. He felt a *fraternal* bond with his teammates.
3. The Elks is a *fraternal* organization.

**2. Of twins, developed from two separate eggs and so not identical.** (разнояйцевый)

4. My sister and I are *fraternal* twins.
5. *Fraternal* twins can be a boy and a girl.

---

### fraudulent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɔːdʒələnt/ &middot; *FRAW-juh-luhnt*
**Русский:** мошеннический, поддельный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ulent from *fraud*

Intended to cheat people, often illegally.

1. The bank flagged several *fraudulent* charges on my card.
2. His insurance claim turned out to be *fraudulent*.
3. They were sued for *fraudulent* advertising.

---

### fraught

**Pronunciation:** /frɔːt/ &middot; *FRAWT*
**Русский:** чреватый, полный (опасностей); напряжённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *fraught with* only after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fraught*, *most fraught*
**Takes:** *with* + noun, in the 'full of' sense
**Preposition patterns:** [fraught with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#fraught-with)

Fraught has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of something bad, such as danger or problems.** (чреватый, полный)

1. The journey was *fraught* with danger.
2. Buying a used car can be *fraught* with problems.

**2. Causing or feeling a lot of worry and tension.** (напряжённый, нервный)

3. She has a *fraught* relationship with her father.
4. The mood in the office was more *fraught* than usual.

---

### frayed

**Pronunciation:** /freɪd/ &middot; *FRAYD*
**Русский:** обтрёпанный; расшатанный (о нервах)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frayed*, *most frayed*
**Form:** -ed from *fray*

Frayed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worn at the edges, so that loose threads show.** (обтрёпанный, потёртый)

1. His jeans were *frayed* at the cuffs.
2. Replace that *frayed* cord before it starts a fire.

**2. Strained and close to breaking, of nerves, tempers or relationships.** (расшатанный, на пределе)

3. Tempers were *frayed* after hours in traffic.
4. Our nerves were *frayed* by the time the results came in.

---

### freakish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfriːkɪʃ/ &middot; *FREE-kish*
**Русский:** странный, необычный, ненормальный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more freakish*, *most freakish*
**Form:** -ish from *freak*

Very unusual and strange, often in a way that seems unnatural.

1. A *freakish* storm dropped snow in May.
2. He has *freakish* strength for his size.
3. It was a *freakish* accident that nobody could have predicted.

---

### freaky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfriːki/ &middot; *FREE-kee*
**Русский:** жуткий, стрёмный, странный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *freakier*, *freakiest*
**Form:** -y from *freak*

Informal: strange and a little frightening.

1. That movie was really *freaky*.
2. It's *freaky* how much you look like your mom.
3. That's the *freakiest* thing that's ever happened to me.

---

### freckled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɛkəld/ &middot; *FREK-uhld*
**Русский:** веснушчатый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *freckle*

Covered with freckles, small brown spots on the skin.

1. A *freckled* boy with red hair waved at us.
2. Her arms were *freckled* from the sun.
3. He had a *freckled* nose and a big smile.

---

### free-range

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfriːˈreɪndʒ/ &middot; *free-RAYNJ*
**Русский:** свободного выгула
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *free* + *range*

Free-range has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of farm animals, or their eggs or meat: allowed to move around outside rather than kept in cages.** (свободного выгула)

1. We only buy *free-range* eggs.
2. The chickens on this farm are *free-range*.
3. *Free-range* turkey costs more at Thanksgiving.

**2. Informal: of children, allowed to play and go places without constant adult supervision.** (самостоятельный, без гиперопеки (о детях))

4. They believe in *free-range* parenting.
5. We had a *free-range* childhood, biking all over town.

---

### freehand

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfriːhænd/ &middot; *FREE-hand*
**Русский:** от руки
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *free* + *hand*
**Contrast:** Also an adverb: *She drew the map freehand.*

Drawn by hand without a ruler, stencil or other tool.

1. He made a quick *freehand* sketch of the building.
2. The artist is known for her *freehand* lettering.
3. The tattoo was a *freehand* design.

---

### freelance

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfriːlæns/ &middot; *FREE-lans*
**Русский:** внештатный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also an adverb and a verb: *work freelance*, *she freelances*. The person is a *freelancer*.

Working for different companies and paid for each job, rather than employed by one company.

1. She's a *freelance* graphic designer.
2. Most of the writers at the magazine are *freelance*.
3. He does *freelance* work on weekends.

---

### freestanding

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfriːˈstændɪŋ/ &middot; *free-STAN-ding*
**Русский:** отдельно стоящий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *free* + *standing*

Standing on its own, not attached to a wall or another building.

1. We replaced the built-in tub with a *freestanding* one.
2. The garage is *freestanding*, not attached to the house.
3. Many of the town's *freestanding* stores have closed.

---

### freewheeling

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfriːˈwiːlɪŋ/ &middot; *free-WEE-ling*
**Русский:** вольный, ничем не скованный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more freewheeling*, *most freewheeling*
**Form:** compound — *free* + *wheeling*

Not worried about rules or responsibilities; free and informal.

1. The show is known for its *freewheeling* interviews.
2. He lived a *freewheeling* life in 1970s California.
