3. The early days of the internet were far more *freewheeling*.

---

### freeze-dried

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfriːzˌdraɪd/ &middot; *FREEZ-dryd*
**Русский:** сублимированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *freeze* + *dried*

Preserved by freezing it quickly and then removing the water.

1. Astronauts eat *freeze-dried* food.
2. We packed *freeze-dried* meals for the camping trip.
3. Most instant coffee is *freeze-dried*.

---

### frenetic

**Pronunciation:** /frəˈnɛtɪk/ &middot; *fruh-NET-ik*
**Русский:** лихорадочный, неистовый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frenetic*, *most frenetic*

Fast, energetic and uncontrolled.

1. New York has a *frenetic* pace of life.
2. The band played a *frenetic* set.
3. The last week before launch was even more *frenetic*.

---

### frenzied

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɛnzid/ &middot; *FREN-zeed*
**Русский:** неистовый, исступлённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frenzied*, *most frenzied*
**Form:** -ed from *frenzy*

Wild, excited and out of control.

1. The sale set off *frenzied* shopping at the mall.
2. Sharks began a *frenzied* attack on the bait.
3. The crowd grew more *frenzied* as the band came on stage.

---

### frequent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfriːkwənt/ &middot; *FREE-kwuhnt*
**Русский:** частый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frequent*, *most frequent*
**Opposite:** *infrequent*, *rare*
**Contrast:** The adjective is stressed on the first syllable, FREE-kwuhnt. The formal verb *to frequent* (to go somewhere often) is often stressed on the second: free-KWENT.

Happening or doing something often.

1. She's a *frequent* flyer on Delta.
2. Power outages are *frequent* here in winter.
3. Storms have become more *frequent* in recent years.

---

### fresh

**Pronunciation:** /frɛʃ/ &middot; *FRESH*
**Русский:** свежий; новый; пресный; бодрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the 'just come from' and 'rude' senses after a verb
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fresher*, *freshest*
**Takes:** *from* or *out of* + noun, in the 'just come from' sense
**Opposite:** *stale* (bread); *frozen*, *canned* (food)

Fresh has 8 distinct senses.

**1. Recently made, picked or caught; not old, frozen or canned.** (свежий)

1. We bought *fresh* corn at the farmers market.
2. Is this bread *fresh*?
3. The *freshest* fish comes in on Tuesday mornings.

**2. New or different, replacing something old or used.** (новый, другой)

4. After the divorce she wanted a *fresh* start.
5. The team needs some *fresh* ideas.
6. Let me get you a *fresh* towel.

**3. Recent, just done, or still clear in the mind.** (свежий, недавний)

7. The memory is still *fresh* in my mind.
8. Watch out for the *fresh* paint!
9. There was a foot of *fresh* snow on the ground.

**4. Of air or a smell: clean, cool and pleasant.** (свежий, чистый)

10. Let's go outside and get some *fresh* air.
11. The sheets had a *fresh*, clean smell.

**5. Of water: not salty.** (пресный)

12. The island has no source of *fresh* water.
13. These fish can't survive in *fresh* water.

**6. Full of energy; not tired.** (бодрый, отдохнувший)

14. I felt *fresh* after a good night's sleep.
15. She looked *fresher* after a shower and a coffee.

**7. Having just come from a place, school or experience.** (только что (окончивший, прибывший))

16. He's *fresh* out of college.
17. The chef came to us *fresh* from a job in Paris.

**8. Informal and a little old-fashioned: rude or disrespectful, often in a sexual way.** (дерзкий, нахальный)

18. Don't get *fresh* with me, young man.
19. He got *fresh* with the waitress and was asked to leave.

---

### freshman

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɛʃmən/ &middot; *FRESH-muhn*
**Русский:** первокурсный; находящийся на первом сроке
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** More often a noun (*She is a freshman*). As an adjective it stands only before a noun: *freshman year*, never *the year is freshman*.

Freshman has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the first year of high school or college.** (первокурсный, для первого года обучения)

1. *Freshman* year was the hardest of the four.
2. He teaches a *freshman* composition class.

**2. Serving a first term in office.** (впервые избранный (о политике))

3. The bill was written by a *freshman* senator.
4. *Freshman* lawmakers get the worst offices in the building.

---

### fretful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɛtfəl/ &middot; *FRET-fuhl*
**Русский:** беспокойный, капризный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fretful*, *most fretful*
**Form:** -ful from *fret* 'to worry'

Worried and unable to relax; of a baby, crying and restless.

1. The baby was *fretful* all night with a fever.
2. She spent a *fretful* week waiting for the test results.
3. He grew more *fretful* as the flight was delayed again.

---

### Freudian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɔɪdiən/ &middot; *FROY-dee-uhn*
**Русский:** фрейдистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — informally *very Freudian*
**Form:** *Freud* + *-ian*

Freudian has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to Sigmund Freud and his theories of the mind.** (фрейдистский)

1. The novel is full of *Freudian* symbols.
2. Her therapist takes a *Freudian* approach.

**2. Showing hidden, often sexual, thoughts or feelings — especially in *a Freudian slip*.** (фрейдистский (об оговорке))

3. Calling my boss 'Mom' was a *Freudian* slip.
4. There's something very *Freudian* about his fear of snakes.

---

### frictionless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɪkʃənləs/ &middot; *FRIK-shuhn-luhs*
**Русский:** без трения; беспрепятственный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frictionless*, *most frictionless*
**Form:** -less from *friction*

Frictionless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In physics, having no friction.** (без трения)

1. Imagine a ball rolling on a *frictionless* surface.
2. No real machine is completely *frictionless*.

**2. Smooth and easy, with no delays or difficulties.** (беспрепятственный, лёгкий)

3. The app promises *frictionless* payments.
4. Online shopping has become almost *frictionless*.
5. The company wants a more *frictionless* checkout.

---

### fried

**Pronunciation:** /fraɪd/ &middot; *FRYD*
**Русский:** жареный; вымотанный; сгоревший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the informal senses after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *fry*, with *y* changed to *i*
**Contrast:** *fried* means cooked in oil or fat. Russian *жареный* also covers roasting and grilling: meat cooked in the oven is *roast*, on a grill *grilled*. Food cooked covered in hot oil is *deep-fried*.

Fried has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Cooked in hot oil or fat.** (жареный)

1. She ordered *fried* chicken and mashed potatoes.
2. Do you want your eggs *fried* or scrambled?
3. Too much *fried* food is bad for your heart.

**2. Informal: very tired and unable to think clearly.** (вымотанный, выжатый)

4. My brain is *fried* after that exam.
5. I'm totally *fried* — I need a break.

**3. Informal: of electronics, destroyed by too much electricity or heat.** (сгоревший)

6. The storm left our router *fried*.
7. Check whether the motherboard is *fried*.

---

### frightful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfraɪtfəl/ &middot; *FRYT-fuhl*
**Русский:** ужасный, кошмарный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frightful*, *most frightful*
**Form:** -ful from *fright*
**Contrast:** A little old-fashioned; everyday American English says *terrible* or *awful*. It is not the same as *frightening* (causing fear).

Very bad, unpleasant or shocking.

1. The traffic downtown was *frightful*.
2. Survivors described the *frightful* conditions in the camp.
3. Oh, the weather outside is *frightful*.

---

### frigid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɪdʒɪd/ &middot; *FRIJ-id*
**Русский:** ледяной, морозный; холодный (о манерах)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frigid*, *most frigid*
**Contrast:** False friend. Russian *фригидный* is only about sex; that sense of *frigid* is old-fashioned and offensive in English. Americans most often use *frigid* about weather and water.

Frigid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very cold.** (ледяной, морозный)

1. *Frigid* air moved into the Midwest overnight.
2. The water in the lake was *frigid*.
3. It was the most *frigid* January on record.

**2. Unfriendly and without any warmth.** (холодный, ледяной (о манерах))

4. She gave him a *frigid* stare.
5. The meeting ended on a *frigid* note.

---

### frilly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɪli/ &middot; *FRIL-ee*
**Русский:** с оборками, с рюшами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *frillier*, *frilliest*
**Form:** -y from *frill*

Decorated with a lot of frills, wavy strips of cloth or lace.

1. The little girl wore a pink *frilly* dress.
2. Her bedroom was full of *frilly* pillows.
3. This year's wedding dresses are *frillier* than ever.

---

### frisky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɪski/ &middot; *FRIS-kee*
**Русский:** резвый, игривый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *friskier*, *friskiest*

Frisky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of energy and wanting to play.** (резвый, игривый)

1. The puppy gets *frisky* in the morning.
2. The horses were *friskier* in the cold weather.

**2. Informal: feeling playful in a sexual way.** (игривый (в сексуальном смысле))

3. After a couple of drinks, he got a little *frisky*.
4. The couple got *frisky* on the dance floor.

---

### frivolous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɪvələs/ &middot; *FRIV-uh-luhs*
**Русский:** легкомысленный; пустяковый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frivolous*, *most frivolous*

Frivolous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not serious or sensible about things that matter.** (легкомысленный)

1. She was tired of his *frivolous* attitude toward work.
2. Don't be so *frivolous* — this is a serious decision.

**2. Having no real purpose or value.** (пустяковый, необоснованный)

3. The judge threw out the *frivolous* lawsuit.
4. He spends too much on *frivolous* purchases.
5. Critics called the new spending even more *frivolous*.

---

### frizzy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɪzi/ &middot; *FRIZ-ee*
**Русский:** мелко вьющийся, пушащийся (о волосах)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *frizzier*, *frizziest*

Of hair: tightly curled and dry, often in a messy way.

1. Humidity makes my hair *frizzy*.
2. She had big *frizzy* hair in the eighties.
3. My hair gets *frizzier* every summer.

---

### front

**Pronunciation:** /frʌnt/ &middot; *FRUHNT*
**Русский:** передний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *back*
**Contrast:** *in front of* is a preposition: *in front of the house* means outside, facing it; *at the front of the house* means in its forward part.

At or near the front part of something.

1. Please use the *front* door.
2. We got seats in the *front* row.
3. He lost two *front* teeth in a fight.

---

### frontal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrʌntəl/ &middot; *FRUHN-tuhl*
**Русский:** лобовой, фронтальный; лобный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *front*

Frontal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Aimed directly at the front; direct and open.** (лобовой, фронтальный)

1. The army launched a *frontal* assault on the city.
2. Her speech was a *frontal* attack on the president's policies.

**2. Relating to the front of the head or brain.** (лобный)

3. Damage to the *frontal* lobe can change a person's behavior.
4. The *frontal* bone forms the forehead.

---

### frostbitten

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɔːstˌbɪtən/ &middot; *FRAWST-bit-uhn*
**Русский:** обмороженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *frost* + *bitten*

Damaged by extreme cold.

1. The climbers returned with *frostbitten* toes.
2. His fingers were badly *frostbitten*.
3. The *frostbitten* plants turned black overnight.

---

### frosted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɔːstɪd/ &middot; *FRAW-stid*
**Русский:** матовый; глазированный; покрытый инеем
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *frost*
**Contrast:** American *frosting* is British *icing*, so *a frosted cake* is a cake covered with icing.

Frosted has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of glass: made rough so that you cannot see through it clearly.** (матовый)

1. The bathroom window has *frosted* glass.
2. She knocked on a *frosted* glass door marked 'Private.'

**2. Covered with frosting, a sweet creamy topping, or with sugar.** (глазированный)

3. The kids ate *frosted* cupcakes at the party.
4. He only likes *frosted* cereal.

**3. Covered with frost.** (покрытый инеем)

5. The *frosted* grass crunched under our boots.
6. The car windows were *frosted* over.

---

### frosty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɔːsti/ &middot; *FRAW-stee*
**Русский:** морозный; холодный (о приёме)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *frostier*, *frostiest*
**Form:** -y from *frost*

Frosty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very cold, with frost on the ground or on surfaces.** (морозный; ледяной)

1. It was a *frosty* morning in November.
2. The nights are getting *frostier*.
3. He grabbed a *frosty* bottle of beer from the cooler.

**2. Unfriendly and cold in manner.** (холодный, неприветливый)

4. She gave me a *frosty* look.
5. Relations between the two neighbors are *frosty*.

---

### frothy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrɔːθi/ &middot; *FRAW-thee*
**Русский:** пенистый; лёгкий, несерьёзный; перегретый (о рынке)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *frothier*, *frothiest*
**Form:** -y from *froth*

Frothy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Full of or covered with small bubbles.** (пенистый, с пеной)

1. She ordered a *frothy* cappuccino.
2. Beat the eggs until they're *frothy*.

**2. Light, fun and not serious.** (лёгкий, несерьёзный)

3. It's a *frothy* summer comedy.
4. Her new novel is even *frothier* than the last one.

**3. Of a market or prices: rising too fast and likely to fall.** (перегретый)

5. Analysts warned that the housing market was *frothy*.
6. Tech stocks look *frothy* right now.

---

### frozen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfroʊzən/ &middot; *FROH-zuhn*
**Русский:** замёрзший; замороженный; оцепеневший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -en participle of *freeze*

Frozen has 5 distinct senses.

**1. Turned into ice, or made hard by cold.** (замёрзший)

1. The kids went skating on the *frozen* pond.
2. The ground was *frozen* solid.

**2. Of food: kept at a very low temperature to preserve it.** (замороженный)

3. Grab a bag of *frozen* peas.
4. *Frozen* pizza is his idea of dinner.
5. Is the chicken fresh or *frozen*?

**3. Of a person: very cold.** (замёрзший, продрогший)

6. Come inside — you must be *frozen*!
7. My feet are *frozen*.

**4. Unable to move because of fear or shock.** (оцепеневший, застывший)

8. She stood *frozen* in the doorway.
9. He was *frozen* with fear.

**5. Of money, prices or a screen: fixed and not able to change or move.** (замороженный (о счетах, ценах); зависший (об экране))

10. Their bank accounts are still *frozen*.
11. My screen is *frozen* again.

---

### frugal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfruːɡəl/ &middot; *FROO-guhl*
**Русский:** бережливый, экономный; скромный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more frugal*, *most frugal*
**Contrast:** Of a person, *frugal* and *thrifty* are praise and *cheap* or *stingy* are criticism. *economical* is usually about things: *an economical car*.

Frugal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Careful to spend very little money.** (бережливый, экономный)

1. My parents were *frugal* and never bought anything on credit.
2. We'll need to be more *frugal* this year.
3. He's *frugal*, but he's not cheap.

**2. Of a meal: simple and cheap.** (скромный)

4. They ate a *frugal* dinner of beans and rice.
5. The monks lived on *frugal* meals.

---

### fruitful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfruːtfəl/ &middot; *FROOT-fuhl*
**Русский:** плодотворный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fruitful*, *most fruitful*
**Form:** -ful from *fruit*
**Opposite:** *fruitless*

Producing good or useful results.

1. We had a *fruitful* meeting with the investors.
2. The partnership has been *fruitful* for both companies.
3. This year's talks were more *fruitful* than last year's.

---

### fruitless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfruːtləs/ &middot; *FROOT-luhs*
**Русский:** бесплодный, безрезультатный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *fruit*
**Opposite:** *fruitful*

Producing no useful result.

1. After a *fruitless* search, they called the police.
2. Our efforts to reach him were *fruitless*.
3. She spent years on a *fruitless* attempt to get the case reopened.

---

### fruity

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfruːti/ &middot; *FROO-tee*
**Русский:** фруктовый (о вкусе, запахе)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fruitier*, *fruitiest*
**Form:** -y from *fruit*
**Contrast:** *fruity* means tasting or smelling of fruit. Something made from fruit uses the noun *fruit* before the noun: *fruit juice*, *fruit salad*, not *fruity juice*.

Tasting or smelling of fruit.

1. This wine is light and *fruity*.
2. The candle has a sweet, *fruity* smell.
3. The red is *fruitier* than the white.

---

### frumpy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfrʌmpi/ &middot; *FRUHM-pee*
**Русский:** безвкусно одетый, старомодный, невзрачный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *frumpier*, *frumpiest*

Wearing plain, old-fashioned clothes that make a person, usually a woman, look unattractive; of clothes, making someone look this way.

1. She felt *frumpy* next to her stylish friends.
2. I don't want a *frumpy* sweater — show me something more modern.
3. That dress makes me look *frumpier* than my grandmother.

---

### fucked-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʌktˈʌp/ &middot; *fuhkt-UHP*
**Русский:** хреновый; ненормальный; обдолбанный (грубо)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun, usually two words after a verb: *it's fucked up*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fucked-up*, *most fucked-up*
**Form:** compound — participle + particle, from the phrasal verb *fuck up*
**Contrast:** Vulgar slang and highly offensive — avoid it at work and with people you don't know. The polite version is *messed-up*.

Fucked-up has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Vulgar slang: very bad, wrong, unfair or badly damaged.** (хреновый, дерьмовый)

1. That's a *fucked-up* thing to say to your sister.
2. The whole situation is *fucked up*.

**2. Vulgar slang: mentally or emotionally damaged.** (ненормальный, с тараканами)

3. He was pretty *fucked up* after the war.
4. She had a *fucked-up* childhood.

**3. Vulgar slang: very drunk or high on drugs.** (обдолбанный, в хлам)

5. Everyone at the party was completely *fucked up*.
6. He got *fucked up* on cheap vodka.

---

### fucking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌkɪŋ/ &middot; *FUHK-ing*
**Русский:** грёбаный, долбаный (грубо)
**Type:** emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Vulgar and highly offensive. Before an adjective or adverb it is an adverb (*fucking cold*), not this adjective. Milder: *freaking*, *damn*.

Vulgar slang: used before a noun to add strong emphasis, anger or annoyance.

1. Where are my *fucking* keys?
2. This whole meeting is a *fucking* joke.
3. Turn off that *fucking* music!

---

### fulfilled

**Pronunciation:** /fʊlˈfɪld/ &middot; *fuul-FILD*
**Русский:** удовлетворённый, реализовавший себя
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fulfilled*, *most fulfilled*
**Form:** -ed from *fulfill*
**Opposite:** *unfulfilled*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *fulfill*, *fulfilled*, *fulfillment*; British spelling drops an *l*: *fulfil*, *fulfilment*.

Happy and satisfied because you are doing something useful or using your abilities fully.

1. She feels *fulfilled* in her work as a nurse.
2. He wanted a more *fulfilled* life than his father had.
3. Retirement left him bored and far from *fulfilled*.

---

### full-blooded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈblʌdɪd/ &middot; *fuul-BLUHD-id*
**Русский:** чистокровный; энергичный, безоговорочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — usually
**Comparison:** not gradable — the 'wholehearted' sense takes *more/most*: *more full-blooded*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

Full-blooded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of pure ancestry, with both parents from the same group or breed.** (чистокровный)

1. The breeder sells *full-blooded* Arabian horses.
2. My grandfather was a *full-blooded* Italian and cooked like one.

**2. Strong, enthusiastic and complete.** (энергичный, безоговорочный)

3. The senator gave *full-blooded* support to the plan.
4. It was a loud, *full-blooded* rock performance.

---

### full-blown

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈbloʊn/ &middot; *fuul-BLOHN*
**Русский:** полномасштабный, в полном разгаре
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — usually
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *full* + *blown*

Fully developed, with all the features of something.

1. The argument turned into a *full-blown* fight.
2. Experts fear a *full-blown* recession.
3. Her cold turned into *full-blown* pneumonia.

---

### full-bodied

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈbɑːdid/ &middot; *fuul-BAH-deed*
**Русский:** насыщенный, с богатым вкусом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more full-bodied*, *most full-bodied*
**Form:** compound — *full* + *body* + *-ed*

Of wine, coffee or a sound: rich and strong.

1. Try a *full-bodied* red with the steak.
2. This coffee is dark and *full-bodied*.
3. The cello has a warmer, more *full-bodied* sound than the viola.

---

### full-fledged

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈflɛdʒd/ &middot; *fuul-FLEJD*
**Русский:** полноценный, полноправный; полномасштабный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — usually
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *full* + *fledged*, from a bird with all its feathers grown
**Contrast:** American *full-fledged*; British writing says *fully fledged*.

Completely developed, or having reached full status.

1. She's now a *full-fledged* attorney.
2. What started as a hobby became a *full-fledged* business.
3. The protest grew into a *full-fledged* movement.

---

### full-grown

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈɡroʊn/ &middot; *fuul-GROHN*
**Русский:** взрослый, полностью выросший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *full* + *grown*

Having reached full adult size.

1. A *full-grown* male can weigh 600 pounds.
2. The tree isn't *full-grown* yet.
3. She has three *full-grown* children.

---

### full-length

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈlɛŋθ/ &middot; *fuul-LENGTH*
**Русский:** полнометражный; в полный рост, до пола
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Full-length has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a movie, album or book: of the normal, complete length.** (полнометражный, полноформатный)

1. Their first *full-length* album comes out in May.
2. It's a *full-length* documentary, not a short.

**2. Reaching the whole height or length of something.** (в полный рост; до пола)

3. There's a *full-length* mirror in the bedroom.
4. She wore a *full-length* gown.

---

### full-on

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈɑːn/ &middot; *fuul-AHN*
**Русский:** полный, настоящий, по полной
**Type:** emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only — mostly
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Informal. It is also an adverb meaning 'completely': *She went full on into training*.

Complete and not held back in any way.

1. It wasn't a disagreement — it was a *full-on* argument.
2. They're in *full-on* panic mode.
3. He greeted me with a *full-on* bear hug.

---

### full-page

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈpeɪdʒ/ &middot; *fuul-PAYJ*
**Русский:** на всю страницу, полосный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Filling a whole page.

1. They took out a *full-page* ad in the Times.
2. The magazine ran a *full-page* photo of the crash.
3. Each chapter opens with a *full-page* illustration.

---

### full-scale

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈskeɪl/ &middot; *fuul-SKAYL*
**Русский:** полномасштабный; в натуральную величину
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Full-scale has 2 distinct senses.

**1. As large or as complete as it can be.** (полномасштабный)

1. The country is on the edge of a *full-scale* war.
2. Police launched a *full-scale* investigation.

**2. Of a model or drawing: the same size as the real thing.** (в натуральную величину)

3. The museum has a *full-scale* replica of the shuttle.
4. They built a *full-scale* mock-up of the cockpit.

---

### full-size

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈsaɪz/ &middot; *fuul-SYZ*
**Русский:** полноразмерный, обычного размера
**Type:** size
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also *full-sized*. Of a bed, *full-size* names a standard American size between a twin and a queen.

Of the normal, full size rather than a smaller version.

1. We rented a *full-size* SUV.
2. The apartment has a *full-size* kitchen.
3. They sleep in a *full-size* bed.

---

### full-strength

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈstrɛŋθ/ &middot; *fuul-STRENGTH*
**Русский:** неразбавленный, полной крепости
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *watered-down*

Not weakened or diluted; at its full power.

1. Take one *full-strength* tablet with food.
2. *Full-strength* coffee keeps me up all night.
3. The cleaner is too harsh if you use it *full-strength*.

---

### full-term

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈtɜːrm/ &middot; *fuul-TURM*
**Русский:** доношенный; полного срока
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *premature*

Born or carried for the whole normal length of a pregnancy.

1. The baby was *full-term* and weighed eight pounds.
2. A *full-term* pregnancy lasts about forty weeks.
3. *Full-term* babies usually go home within a day.

---

### full-throated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʊlˈθroʊtɪd/ &middot; *fuul-THROH-tid*
**Русский:** во весь голос; безоговорочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — usually
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *full* + *throated*

Loud and with no holding back, whether a sound or a show of support.

1. He gave a *full-throated* defense of the policy.
2. The crowd let out a *full-throated* roar.
3. The senator offered *full-throated* support for the bill.

---

### fulsome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʊlsəm/ &middot; *FUUL-suhm*
**Русский:** неумеренный, приторный (о похвале)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fulsome*, *most fulsome*
**Contrast:** Traditionally an insult: *fulsome praise* is so thick that it sounds fake. Many Americans now use it simply to mean *full* or *thorough*, and careful readers still object.

Fulsome has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So excessive that it sounds insincere.** (чрезмерный, приторный)

1. He thanked the host in *fulsome* terms.
2. The review's *fulsome* praise made me suspicious.

**2. Full and generous — a newer use, common in the press.** (полный, исчерпывающий)

3. The senator promised a *fulsome* response to the questions.
4. Reporters asked for a more *fulsome* account of the meeting.

---

### fun

**Pronunciation:** /fʌn/ &middot; *FUHN*
**Русский:** весёлый, приятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fun*, *most fun*
**Contrast:** *fun* means you enjoy it; *funny* means it makes you laugh. *a fun movie* is entertaining, *a funny movie* is a comedy. *fun* is also a noun (*We had a lot of fun*), and *funner*/*funnest* are children's forms.

Enjoyable; giving pleasure.

1. We had a *fun* weekend at the lake.
2. She's a *fun* person to work with.
3. Skiing turned out to be *more fun* than I expected.

---

### fun-filled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌnˌfɪld/ &middot; *FUHN-fild*
**Русский:** полный развлечений, весёлый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-filled*, like *action-filled*

Full of enjoyable activities.

1. The resort promises a *fun-filled* weekend.
2. They spent a *fun-filled* afternoon at the fair.
3. It was three *fun-filled* days of music and food.

---

### fun-loving

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌnˌlʌvɪŋ/ &middot; *FUHN-luhv-ing*
**Русский:** жизнерадостный, весёлый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fun-loving*, *most fun-loving*
**Form:** compound — noun + *-loving*, like *peace-loving*

Enjoying parties, jokes and good times.

1. He's a *fun-loving* guy who never takes things too seriously.
2. They're a *fun-loving* family.
3. Her *fun-loving* side comes out on vacation.

---

### functional

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌŋkʃənəl/ &middot; *FUHNGK-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** работающий, исправный; практичный; функциональный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more functional*, *most functional*
**Form:** -al from *function*
**Opposite:** *nonfunctional*
**Contrast:** A *dysfunctional* family is not one that fails to work in this sense — *dysfunctional* means unhealthy relationships.

Functional has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Working, and able to be used.** (работающий, исправный)

1. Only one of the elevators is *functional*.
2. The kitchen is small but fully *functional*.

**2. Designed to be practical rather than attractive.** (практичный, функциональный)

3. The furniture is plain and *functional*.
4. It's a *functional* building, not a beautiful one.
5. The new layout is *more functional* than the old one.

**3. Relating to the way something works.** (функциональный)

6. There's no *functional* difference between the two models.
7. The scan showed no *functional* damage.

---

### functionally illiterate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌŋkʃənəli ɪˈlɪtərət/ &middot; *FUNGK-shuh-nuh-lee i-LIT-ur-it*
**Русский:** функционально неграмотный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** adverb + adjective

Able to read a little, but not well enough to handle everyday forms, signs and instructions.

1. About a tenth of the adults tested were *functionally illiterate*.
2. He hid for years that he was *functionally illiterate*.
3. The program helps *functionally illiterate* adults fill out job forms.

---

### functioning

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌŋkʃənɪŋ/ &middot; *FUHNGK-shuh-ning*
**Русский:** действующий, работающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *fully functioning*, *high-functioning*
**Contrast:** In *The machine is functioning* this is the verb, not the adjective.

Working as it should.

1. It's the only *functioning* hospital in the region.
2. They want a *functioning* government, not a caretaker.
3. A fully *functioning* immune system clears the virus in days.

---

### fundamental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʌndəˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *fuhn-duh-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** фундаментальный, основной, коренной
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more fundamental*, *most fundamental*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** -al from Latin *fundamentum* 'foundation'
**Contrast:** The noun *the fundamentals* means the basics: *the fundamentals of accounting*.

Forming the base of something, and therefore the most important.

1. There's a *fundamental* difference between the two plans.
2. Free speech is *fundamental* to a democracy.
3. The problem is *more fundamental* than a software bug.

---

### fundamentalist

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfʌndəˈmɛntəlɪst/ &middot; *fuhn-duh-MEN-tuh-list*
**Русский:** фундаменталистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *He was raised by fundamentalists*.

Following the strictest, most literal form of a religion or belief.

1. He was raised in a *fundamentalist* church.
2. The party has taken a *fundamentalist* line.
3. Her reading of the text is strictly *fundamentalist*.

---

### funded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌndɪd/ &middot; *FUHN-did*
**Русский:** финансируемый, обеспеченный средствами
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *well funded*, *underfunded*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Opposite:** *unfunded*

Paid for with money provided for the purpose.

1. The program is *funded* by the state.
2. She works in a well-*funded* research lab.
3. Is the project fully *funded* yet?

---

### funereal

**Pronunciation:** /fjuːˈnɪriəl/ &middot; *fyoo-NIR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** похоронный, мрачный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more funereal*, *most funereal*
**Form:** from *funeral*
**Contrast:** *funeral* is the noun and the word you use before a noun (*a funeral home*); *funereal* means gloomy, like a funeral.

Slow, dark and gloomy, the way a funeral is.

1. The party had a strangely *funereal* mood.
2. He gave the news in a *funereal* tone.
3. The music was slow and *funereal*.

---

### fungal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌŋɡəl/ &middot; *FUHNG-guhl*
**Русский:** грибковый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — mostly
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *fungus*

Caused by or consisting of fungus.

1. She's being treated for a *fungal* infection.
2. *Fungal* growth ruined the crop.
3. It's a *fungal* disease that spreads in wet weather.

---

### fungible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌndʒəbəl/ &middot; *FUHN-juh-buhl*
**Русский:** взаимозаменяемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** A law and finance word, familiar to most people from *non-fungible token*.

Able to be exchanged for another unit of exactly the same kind.

1. Money is *fungible* — one dollar is as good as another.
2. These parts are *fungible*, so any supplier will do.
3. *Fungible* goods can be replaced one for one.

---

### funky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌŋki/ &middot; *FUHNG-kee*
**Русский:** необычный, стильный; фанковый; с душком
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *funkier*, *funkiest*

Funky has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Unusual and stylish in a relaxed, offbeat way.** (необычный, стильный)

1. She wears *funky* glasses.
2. It's a *funky* little coffee shop on Third.
3. The place got *funkier* after the new owners took over.

**2. Of music: with a strong, danceable rhythm.** (фанковый, ритмичный)

4. The band has a *funky* bass line.
5. I love the *funky* stuff they recorded in the seventies.

**3. Smelling bad.** (с душком, вонючий)

6. Something in the fridge smells *funky*.
7. His gym bag is seriously *funky*.

---

### fur-lined

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːrˌlaɪnd/ &middot; *FUR-lynd*
**Русский:** на меху, с меховой подкладкой
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-lined*, like *silk-lined*

Having fur on the inside for warmth.

1. She pulled on her *fur-lined* boots.
2. He wore a parka with a *fur-lined* hood.
3. The gloves are *fur-lined*.

---

### furnished

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːrnɪʃt/ &middot; *FUR-nisht*
**Русский:** меблированный, с мебелью
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *fully furnished*, *partly furnished*
**Opposite:** *unfurnished*
**Contrast:** *furnished* means it has furniture. The furniture itself is *the furnishings*.

Supplied with furniture.

1. We're renting a *furnished* apartment near campus.
2. The place comes fully *furnished*.
3. Is the room *furnished*, or do we bring a bed?

---

### furrowed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːroʊd/ &middot; *FUR-ohd*
**Русский:** изборождённый; нахмуренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed participle of *furrow*

Furrowed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Marked with deep lines or wrinkles.** (изборождённый морщинами, нахмуренный)

1. His *furrowed* brow told me the news was bad.
2. Her face was *furrowed* from years in the sun.

**2. Cut into long grooves, as a plowed field is.** (вспаханный бороздами)

3. We walked across the *furrowed* field.
4. The *furrowed* ground was ready for seed.

---

### furry

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːri/ &middot; *FUR-ee*
**Русский:** пушистый, покрытый мехом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *furrier*, *furriest*
**Form:** -y from *fur*

Covered with fur, or soft like fur.

1. A *furry* little dog ran up to us.
2. The blanket is soft and *furry*.
3. Her cat gets *furrier* every winter.

---

### furtive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfɜːrtɪv/ &middot; *FUR-tiv*
**Русский:** скрытный, украдкой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more furtive*, *most furtive*
**Opposite:** *open*

Done quietly and secretly, so that nobody notices.

1. He took a *furtive* glance at his phone.
2. They exchanged *furtive* looks across the table.
3. There was something *furtive* about the way she left.

---

### fussy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌsi/ &middot; *FUHS-ee*
**Русский:** привередливый; капризный; вычурный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fussier*, *fussiest*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Contrast:** *picky* is the everyday American word for the first sense: *a picky eater*.

Fussy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Hard to please, and worried about small details.** (привередливый)

1. He's *fussy* about what he eats.
2. Don't be so *fussy* — it's just a sandwich.
3. She's *fussier* than her sister about grammar.

**2. Of a baby: crying and unsettled.** (капризный, беспокойный)

4. The baby's *fussy* tonight.
5. He gets *fussy* around bedtime.

**3. Of a design: crowded with too many small details.** (вычурный, аляповатый)

6. The wallpaper is a little *fussy* for my taste.
7. The dress is pretty, but the lace is *fussy*.

---

### futile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfjuːtəl/ &middot; *FYOO-tuhl*
**Русский:** бесполезный, тщетный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more futile*, *most futile*
**Opposite:** *effective*

Certain to fail, so not worth doing.

1. It was a *futile* attempt to save the building.
2. Arguing with him is *futile*.
3. Every effort so far has proved *futile*.

---

### future

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfjuːtʃər/ &middot; *FYOO-chur*
**Русский:** будущий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *past*
**Contrast:** Also a common noun (*the future*). As an adjective it goes only before a noun: *future plans*, never *the plans are future*.

Happening or existing at a later time.

1. We are setting money aside for *future* expenses.
2. *Future* students will use the new library.
3. He introduced her as his *future* wife.

---

### futuristic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌfjuːtʃəˈrɪstɪk/ &middot; *fyoo-chuh-RIS-tik*
**Русский:** футуристический, как из будущего
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more futuristic*, *most futuristic*
**Form:** -istic from *future*

Looking as if it belongs to the future.

1. The car has a *futuristic* dashboard.
2. The building looks *futuristic* against the old skyline.
3. Their designs are *more futuristic* than practical.

---

### fuzzy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈfʌzi/ &middot; *FUHZ-ee*
**Русский:** пушистый; размытый, нечёткий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *fuzzier*, *fuzziest*
**Contrast:** *warm and fuzzy* is a fixed phrase for a comfortable, sentimental feeling: *The ad gave me a warm and fuzzy feeling*.

Fuzzy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with short soft hairs.** (пушистый)

1. He gave her a *fuzzy* teddy bear.
2. The peaches were warm and *fuzzy*.

**2. Not clear or sharp, whether a picture, a memory or a rule.** (размытый, нечёткий)

3. The photo came out *fuzzy*.
4. My memory of that night is *fuzzy*.
5. The rules get *fuzzier* the more you read them.
